**THE BANQUE INDUSTRIELLE DE CHINE** The causes of the collapse of the Banque Industrielle de Chine have to be looked for in the grave error which the management committed after the Armistice in neglecting the Far East market to entangle themselves in European business. The greatest part of the total losses (estimated to amount to-day at Frcs : 400,000,000.00) were in fact, sustained by the European branches of the Bank, while the China Agencies did not suffer more from the world-wide crisis of 1920-1921 than any other foreign institution established in China. It has been ascertained that the losses incurred by these China Branches were more than largely covered by the profits made by the Saigon office, even taking into account the rather heavy cost of an untimely shipbuilding experience at Hsin Ho near Tientsin. province. The High School entrance examination is conducted directly by the College. The enrollment for the school year, 1920-21, was 550 students, of whom 346 were in the High School and 210 in the Middle School. — p. 289 ([scan leaf 350](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n350/mode/1up))

## Who's Who

**AMUERHLINGKUEI** (阿穆爾靈圭).—Mongol. Prince of First Order, December, 1891. Great-grandson of Prince Seng-ko-lin-ch'in. Lieutenant-General of Bordered White Chinese Banner, January, 1910. Superintendent of Peking Octroi, February, 1912. Member for Mongolia of the National Council. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**BIEN, F. S** see Pien Fu-sun. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**BIEN, Z. S** see Pien Shou-sun. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CHAI, C. L** see Ch'ai Chun-lin. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CH'AI CHUN-LIN (C. L. Chai) (柴春霖)** .—Kansu. Born 1892. Graduated from University of Wisconsin, U.S.A., B.A. Councillor to the Cabinet. Member of Parliament. Professor, Peking National University. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHEN-FANG, (張鎮芳)** .—Honan. A cousin of ex-President Yuan Shih-kai. Tientsin Taotai, December, 1907. Salt Commissioner at Tientsin, February, 1918. Seconded to assist Ministry of Finance in connection with the Patriotic Loan, January, 1919. Was appointed Acting Tutuh of Chihli in February, 1912, where he was denounced by the gentry. Acting Tutuh of Honan, March, 1912. Subs. do., October 28, 1912. Director-General of the Coal Mining Co. of Liuhokou, Honan. Appointed to the Council of Regency on the abortive restoration of the Manchu dynasty, July, 1917. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHENG-HSUN (張振勳)** .—Member of Tsanchengyuan. Organiser of motor-bus service in Peking. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHI-HSU, (張繼煦)** .—Hupeh. Born 1877. Studied in Hupeh Literary School and graduated from a college in Japan in pedagogics. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Education, since 1917. Appointed Commissioner of Education for Anhui, Sept. 1920. Author and translator of various books on History of Education of China, of Europe, etc. Second and third class Chiaho decorations. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CHANG CH'I-KUANG, (張緝光)** .—Honan. Born Aug. 23, 1863. Secretary, Ministry of Communications since April 29, 1918. In the Manchu regime, was member of Board of Education. After formation of the Republic, has been Superintendent of Customs, Changsha and Taoyin in Kwangsi Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHIEN, (張謇)** .—Kiangsu. A noted Hanlin scholar (Optimus), who declined to take office (except that of Adviser to the Board of Commerce, 1904) in order to devote his energies to the fostering of industry and commerce. Appointed "Pacificator" in Kiangsu, November, 1911. He was offered, but refused, the post of Minister of Agriculture, Commerce, and Industry in Yuan Shih-kai's November (1911) Cabinet. He is also reported to have been offered, and to have refused, the Premiership, after Tang Shao-yi's resignation. During 1911 he was engaged upon schemes for the promotion of commerce in Manchuria, and the formation of Sino-American commercial enterprises. Director-General Huaiho Conservancy, March 13, 1913. Minister of Industry and Commerce and temporary Minister of Agriculture and Forestry, September 11, 1913. Director-General of the National Conservancy Bureau, 1915. Member of the Tsanchengyuan, 1915. "Friend of Sungshan" (see Hsu Shih-ch'ang). Now devotes himself to commercial enterprises at Nantungchow, his native place. Elected Managing-Director of Bank of Communications, June, 1922. First Order of Merit. — p. 787 ([scan leaf 860](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n860/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHIH (張志)** —Szechuan. President, High Court of Justice, Anhui. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHIH-HSIANG (張治祥)** —Szechuan. A graduate of a Law College in Tokio. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Joined Tungmenghui, 1906. Attempted to raise revolution in Chengtu, but was arrested when the attempt failed, 1908; and when the independence of Szechuan was declared he was released and appointed as delegate for the amalgamation of the War Offices of Chengtu and Chungking, and then Chief of the Department Foreign Affairs, Szechuan. Resigned 1913, when elected as member of the Chung Yi Yuan. President, Kung Ho College. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHIH-TAN (張志潭)** —Chihli. Was a Chujen and a member of the Board of Army in the late Ching dynasty. Was successively Taoyin of Sui Yuan, Vice-Minister of Interior; Chief Secretary of the Cabinet; Vice-Minister of War; Superintendent of Peking Municipal Bureau. In the Chin Cabinet of 1920, Chang was Minister of Interior. Was Superintendent of National Famine Relief Bureau, 1920-21. Transferred to the Ministry of Communications, May 1921. Resigned, Dec. 25, 1921. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHIN (張謹)** —Appointed Commissioner of Education for Chihli, Oct. 3, 1922. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHING-HUI (張景惠)** —Fengtien. Born 1872. Studied in the Chiang Wu Military School. Major-General in the Army. Commanding General of the First Division of Fengtien Army. Tutung of Charhar, 1920. Resigned, May 15, 1921. Participated in the Chihli-Fengtien struggle and was defeated by Wu Pei-fu's forces at Changsintien in May 1922. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHING-YAO (張敬堯)** —General. Formerly Commander of Regiment of the 6th Division. Brigadier-General of the 3rd Mixed Brigade. Promoted to Commanding-General of the 7th Division. Appointed Commissioner for the Extirpation of Bandits at the Boundary of Kiangsu, Anhui, Shantung and Honan. Tutung of Suiyuan, Oct. 18, 1917. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG CHU-FEN (張澍葵)** —Anhui. Born 1875. Studied law in his native province. Vice-Chairman of General Chamber of Commerce, Anking, since Nov. 1920. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG CH'UN-NIEN (張椿年)** —Hupeh. Born 1861. Studied in the Police Training School of Kiangsi. Vice-Chairman, Wuchang General Chamber of Commerce. Sixth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG EN-SHOU (張恩綬)** —Chihli. Born 1880. A Graduate of a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Member of various committees in Chinputang. At the end of Ching Dynasty he was a clerk in the Board of Communications. During the Revolution he organized the Pao An Hui in Tientsin. In August, he was appointed Director of the Law School, Tientsin, and under his management until January, 1913, there was an increase of students from 400 to 1200. Translated several Japanese books into Chinese. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG FENG-TAI (張鳳臺)** —Head of Finance Department, Honan. January 19, 1913. Civil Governor for Honan, July 5, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan. Again Civil Governor of Honan, 1920. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG FU-YUAN (張復元)** —Chekiang. Born 1878. Graduate of Japanese University. After returning from Japan he was teacher to several Law Schools in Chekiang. During Revolution he was made Chief of the Civil Department and Acting Chief of the Department of Justice, 1912. A member of the Tsan Yi Yuan. Editor of certain periodicals and a lawyer. Chinputang. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG, H. W** see Chang Hsin Wu. — p. 788 ([scan leaf 861](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n861/mode/1up))

**CHANG HSI-LUAN, (張錫鑾)** —Honan. Formerly Taotai at Mukden. Acting Commissioner of Finance, Mukden, August, 1907. Substantive Commissioner of Finance, January, 1908. Governor of Shansi, November, 1911. From March to August, 1912, was Tutuh of Chihli. In August was appointed Commissioner for the Pacification of Western Frontier of Manchuria. Appointed Acting Tutuh of Fengtien, November 3, on the resignation of Chao Erh-hsün Substantive ditto, November 16, 1912. Additional Acting Tutuh of Kirin, June 13, 1913. Chiangchun of Hupeh, 1915. Member of Tsanchengyuan. Field-Marshal, December, 1915. — p. 789 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n862/mode/1up))

**CHANG HSIANG-LIN, (張祥麟)** —Shanghai, Age 31, Educated at St. John's University, Shanghai, and Columbia University, New York. Secretary of the Cabinet Office since 1917. Has been Acting Director of the Translation Bureau of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Associate Councillor of the Ministry of Interior, Associate Secretary of the Ministry of Communications, Secretary of the Bureau for the Custody of Enemy Property, Attache to the Chinese Legation in Washington, D.C., Assistant Editor of the Peking Daily News, Member of the American Society of International law, etc. Awarded Third class Chiaho Decoration in January, 1920. Was one of the Secretaries to the Chinese Delegation at Washington (1922) and is now Consul at New York. — p. 789 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n862/mode/1up))

**CHANG HSIN-WU, (H. W. Chang), (張新吾)** —Kiangsu. Born Dec. 14, 1879. Graduated from the Peiyang University, Tientsin and a University in Japan. Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. Acting Vice-Minister in 1912 and 1914. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 789 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n862/mode/1up))

**CHANG HSIU-SHENG, (張秀升)** —Shansi. Born 1880. Studied in the Normal School Department of the Imperial Peking University. Was once Dean of Faculty, Imperial Shansi University. Principal, First Provincial Normal School of Shansi in 1912. Vice-Chairman, Shansi Provincial Educational Association since 1912. Fifth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 789 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n862/mode/1up))

**CHANG HSUEH-LIANG, (張學良)** —Fengtien. Son of Chang Tso-lin. Studied in the Chiang Wu T'ang of Three Eastern Provinces. Adviser to Tuchun of Kirin, Oct. 1919. Officer, President's Body Guard, May, 1920. Brigade-General. Third class Wenhu decoration. — p. 789 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n862/mode/1up))

**CHANG HSUN, (張勳)** —Kiangsi. An old-type military officer. Brigade-General, Ninyuanfu, Szechuan, 1904. Commander-in-Chief, Yunnan, February, 1908, but did not proceed. Commander-in-Chief, Kansu, September, 1908. Commander-in-Chief of the Kiangnan forces, 1911, with headquarters at Nanking, and permission to wear the Yellow Jacket. Was in command at Nanking when that city was attacked by the revolutionary forces, and effected a masterly retreat to the northern bank of the river, although he had no warships at his disposal. Thereafter he commandeered practically all the available rolling stock of the Tientsin-Pukow Railway, using it as a portable camp for his forces. Though barbarously severe, he won universal admiration by the manner in which he preserved discipline among his forces, and peace and order in the neighbourhood in which he encamped. He was made Viceroy of the Liang-Kiang provinces as an inducement to reconquer them for the Imperial family. He continued to occupy important points upon the Tientsin-Pukow Railway for months after the abdication of the Manchus, although offered commands in Tibet and elsewhere. Full General, July 17, 1913. Pacificator of Kiangsu, July 19, 1913. Recaptured Nanking, September 1. Appointed Tutuh of Kiangsu. Owing to the outrages of his troops had to apologize to Japanese Consul at Nanking. Succeeded by Feng Kuo-chang. Appointed Field-Marshal and High Inspector of the Yangtze provinces, 1915. Summoned by President Li Yuan-hung to Peking in June, 1917, he insisted on the dissolution of Parliament, and subsequently proclaimed the restoration of the Manchu dynasty. His troops were immediately overwhelmed. Chang Hsun took refuge in the Dutch Legation (July 12), where he remained until he was pardoned by President Hsu Shih-chang, Oct. 23, 1918. Appointed Commissioner of Forestry and Agriculture for Jehol, 1921. — p. 789 ([scan leaf 862](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n862/mode/1up))

**CHANG HU, (張弧)** —Chekiang. Salt Commissioner, Changlu (Chihli), May, 1912. Salt Commissioner, Liang Huai, November 14, 1912. Vice-Minister of Finance, September 14, 1913. Chief of Central Salt Administration. Dismissed June 21, 1915. Reappointed Vice-Minister of Finance and Chief of Central Salt Administration, in 1920. Appointed Minister of Finance, Dec. 25, 1921. Fled to Japan in May, 1922, when Chang Tso-lin was defeated by the Chihli party. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CHANG HUAI-CHIH, (張懷芝)** —Shantung. Was a petty military officer in his own province during the last dynasty. Since the Republic, Chang has been successively Defence Commissioner of Tientsin and of Paotingfu, Tartar General of Charhar, 1915. Holds the title of Chi Wu Chiang Chun, Tuchun of Shantung, 1916. Chief of General Staff, at present time. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CHANG HUNG-CH'UAN (張宏銓)** —Hunan. Born 1878. A Graduate of the Normal School of Hunan and studied in a Normal School in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. After graduating in Hunan Normal School was appointed by Mr. Hsiung Hsi-ling to manage educational affairs in the west of Hunan. After returning from Japan was appointed by the Viceroy Chao of Fengtien to become sectional chief of the Agriculture Experiment Department in Fengtien, and then was sent to investigate the agricultural and other conditions of the three provinces. A member of the Committee of Chinputang. Author of the Result of the Investigation of the Cultivation of the Three Eastern Provinces. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CHANG I-LIN, (張一麐)** —Kiangsu. Hanlin. Was Secretary to ex-President Yuan from the time Yuan became Victory of Chihli till his death, except during Yuan's exile when Chang became Secretary to the Governors of Chekiang and Kiangsu. Minister of Education Oct. 1915—April 1916. Adviser to President Hsu 1918 15 1922. Elected member of Kiangsu Provincial Assembly, Aug. 1, 1921. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CHANG I-OU (張軾歐)** —Kiangsu. Born 1879. After studying in Nanyang College and Chentan Academy in Shanghai, he attended a technical school at Hainent, Belgium. Ingenieur des Mines. Chief of the Department of Mineralogy, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. Commissioner of Industries for Kiangsu since Oct. 1917. Second class Tashou and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CHANG I-PENG, (張一鵬)** —Kiangsu. Commissioner of Finance, Kiangsi, 1917. Vice-Minister of Justice, May, 1918. Again Aug. 1920. Resigned February, 1921. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CHANG I-SHU (張翼樞)** —Hunan. Born 1885. Studied in France. Appointed by the Nanking Government Representative to France, and resigned third month of the same year. In the sixth month he was appointed Commissioner of Foreign Affairs of Yunnan, and was appointed in the fourth month of the second year special Delegate for Foreign Affairs. Supervisor of the Tengyueh Customs, 1915. Concurrently Tao-yin of Tengyueh. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CH'ANG JU-CHING** —Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Tarbagatai, April 25th, 1913. — p. 790 ([scan leaf 863](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n863/mode/1up))

**CHANG JUI-CHI, (張瑞璣)** Shansi. Born 1871. A Chinshih of the late Dynasty, and was an official in Shensi. In the time of the Revolution was appointed Councillor to the Shensi Army and Chief Adviser of Civil Affairs. Returned to Shansi, spring of 1912, when he was made Chief of Finance Department. In January, 1913, was appointed Chief Civil Administrator, but refused to accept the post. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Author of The Poetry and other Literary Work of Loheng. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG KUANG-CHIEN, (張廣建)** Acting Provincial Treasurer, Shantung, November, 1911. Acting Governor, January, 1912. Summoned to Peking, March, 1912. Governor of Shuntienfu, December 24, 1912. Frontier Commissioner, Shensi and Kansu, September 19, 1913. Was Tuchun and Civil Governor of Kansu, April 1916 till spring, 1921. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG KUO-KAN, (張國淦)** Hupeh. Assistant in the Bureau of Estimates, Cabinet, June, 1911. Retired January, 1912. Head of Civil Service Department of Cabinet, May, 1912. Secretary to Cabinet, October 6, 1912. Appointed Minister of Education, April 23, 1916. Minister of Agriculture, June 30, 1916. Again, July 17, 1917. Appointed Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, June 12, 1922, and concurrently Acting Minister of Interior before Tan Yen-kai attended office. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG LIEN, (常連)** Deputy Lieutenant-General, Ninghsia, June, 1911. Tartar General Ninghsia, July 29, 1912. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG LIEN-KUEI, (張聯魁)** Shansi. Born 1880. A Graduate of the University at Tokio. Chujen of the late Dynasty. In the first year of Republic was a Director of the High Agriculture and Forestry College in Shansi, then a member of the Provisional National Council, and a member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Chengyuhui. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG LU-CH'UAN, (張魯泉)** Shantung. Born 1880. Graduate of a High School in Shantung and the Agricultural School in Paoting. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Has been engaged in an enterprise in the colonization and cultivation of Fengtien Province. After having graduated he was engaged in an investigation into agricultural conditions of several places. During the Revolution he led an army and captured Kaomi and Chucheng, and when the Manchu abdication took place he came to Tsinan and organized the Provisional Provincial Assembly. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG MIN-CHEN** Compiler, Commission for Codification of Laws, July 26, 1912. Councillor, Department of Legislation. Cabinet, August 31, 1912. Deputy for Organization of National Assembly, September 10, 1912. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG MING-CHI, (張鳴岐)** Shantung. Acting Financial Commissioner, Kwangsi, July, 1905. Acting Governor, December, 1906. Governor of Kwangsi, June, 1907. Acting Viceroy of Liangkiang provinces, October, 1910, and substantive Viceroy, April, 1911, until the Revolution. Chief Civil Administrator, Kwangsi, October 24, 1913. Civil Governor of Kwangtung, 1914. Was Governor of Kwangsi, 1915. Retired. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG PEI-CHUEH (張培爵)** Civil Administrator of Szechuan, July, 1912, to September 24, 1913. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG PING-LIN, (章炳麟)** Chekiang. Born 1867. Noted for his literary accomplishments and his intense interest in Politics. When the Nanking Republican Government was established, Chang was asked to be High Adviser to the Cabinet. Leader of the Tungyi party. Appointed Political adviser to the Government in Peking. Participated in the organization of the Canton Military Government, 1917. First Class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 791 ([scan leaf 864](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n864/mode/1up))

**CHANG PO-LIEH (張伯烈)** Hupeh. Studied law in Japan. A strenuous opponent of the nationalisation of the railways by the Manchu Government. A Hupeh member and Vice-Speaker (Sept. 1922.) of the House of Representatives. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG PO-LING, (張伯苓)** Tientsin. Born April 5, 1876. Studied in the Naval College, Tientsin. Later attended Teachers' College, Columbia University, (U.S.A.) President, Nankai College and President of the Tientsin Y.M.C.A. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG SHAO-PO, (張紹伯)** Fengtien. Born 1869. Studied in the College of Russian Language. Appointed Commissioner for Foreign Affairs for Sinkiang since April 25, 1913. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG SHAO-TSENG, (張曾紹)** Councillor to President, April 19, 1912. Military Governor of Suiyuancheng, October 12, 1912. Deputy Military Commissioner and Assistant-Resident at Kiachta, 1915. Suggested the Lushan Conference for the discussion and settlement of the Civil and Military Affairs during Autumn, 1921. Appointed Civil Governor of Shensi, June 18, 1922. Minister of War. June, 1922 to date. Was appointed President of Commission on Mongolian Frontor affairs, Nov. 1922. Second order of Merit. Appointment as Premier ratified by Parliament, Jan., 1923. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG SHI-YU** Secretary of the General Department at the Headquarters of the Generalissimo of the Army and Navy, 1915. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG SHOU-LING, (張壽齡)** Kiangsu. Chinshih. Studied in Japan. Served in financial affairs in Shantung and Chihli and had been Magistrate of Tientsin and other places in Chihli during the late Ching Dynasty. Held several positions in Manchuria under Viceroy Chao. Chief Secretary to Tutuh of Kiangsu and concurrently Chief of the Revenue Bureau of Kiangsu. Vice-Minister of Finance, February, 1914. Adviser to the late President Feng Kuo-chang. Appointed Director-General, National Wine and Tobacco Administration, which post he held till Dect. 9, 1921. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG SHU-YUAN, (張樹元)** Shantung. Born 1878. Graduated from a Military Officers Training School in Japan. Commander of the 5th Army Division. Tuchun of Shantung, and concurrently acting Civil Governor. Participated in the Anfu and Chihli political struggle. Cashiered of all the ranks, decorations. Pardoned by Presidential Mandate of Jan. 2, 1922. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG TA-YI (張大義)** Yunnan. Born 1883. Graduate of a Law School in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was editor of Yunnan Cha-chih. and Yunnan Colloquial Newspaper. A member of the Committee of Tung Meng Hui. He was one of those who captured Shanghai. He organized a regiment to attack Nanking. Chief Secretary of the Ministry of the Interior in the Nanking Government. After the removal of the Republican capital from Nanking to Peking he was made investigator of the Bureau of Merits, for the Yunnan section. Was appointed Chief of the High Court of Yunnan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG TIEN-YAO, (張典堯)** Shensi. Born 1881. President, Si-an Y.M.C.A. since March, 1918. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG TING-I (張鼎彝)** Chihli. Born 1871. Chujen. Was elected Member of Parliament 1913. Became a Hsien Magistrate in Suiyuan upon dissolution of Parliament by Yuan Shih-kai. Commissioner of Finance for Suiyuan since March 1919. Third class Chiaho and fourth class Wenhu decorations. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSAI-YANG, ([illegible])** Chekiang. Was appointed Civil Governor of Chekiang, Oct. 28, 1922. — p. 792 ([scan leaf 865](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n865/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSO-HAN, (張佐漢)** —Chihli. Born Nov. 19, 1876. Studied and graduated from the Normal Department of a university in Japan. Chujen. Chairman. Chihli Provincial Educational Association, since July, 1919. Author of Psychology, Ethics, etc. Formerly of Kuomintang. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSO-HSIANG, (張作相)** .—Fengtien. Has been Brigadier General of the 54th Brigade of the 27th. Army Division. Now Commanding General of the 27th. Concurrently Chief of Staff of the Inspecting-General of Manchuria. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSO-LIN, (張作霖)** .—Mukden. General Chang is under fifty years of age. He received no education in his youth. Fought on the side of Japan, during the Russo-Japanese War. After the war, General Chang surrendered to the Chinese Government at request of Japan. He and his Hunghutze were taken into the Chinese Government service and received quick promotion on account of their bravery. Appointed Military Governor of Fengtien in 1911 which position he is still holding. He first commanded the 27th Army Division and now has under his control over 100,000 men scattered all over Manchuria. He served ex-President Yuan faithfully until the collapse of the latter's monarchical movement in 1916. When General Chang Hsun made his coup d'etat in 1917, he assisted General Tuan Chi-jui in restoring the Republic. Was appointed Inspecting-General of the Three Eastern Provinces in 1918. Jointly with General Tsao Kun, led an expeditionary force to disband the Anfu political club in the summer of 1920. Attended the "Super-Tuchuns, Conference" at Tientsin in May 1921. Ordered to be relieved of all his posts after being defeated by the Chihli party, May 1922. Since his dismissal he has defied the Central Government and now virtually rules Manchuria as an independent Province. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSU-YIN, (張祖蔭)** .—Yunnan. Born 1891. Graduated from Agricultural College, Peking. President, Yunnan Provincial Educational Association since July, 1920. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSUNG-HSIANG, (章宗祥)** .—Chekiang. Graduate of a Law College in Japan, Superintendent of Police at Peking, 1908-10. Deputy Commissioner of Constitutional Department of the Cabinet, June, 1911. Retired, January, 1912. Chief of Law Department of Cabinet, April, 1912. Chief Justice of Supreme Court, June, 1912, and President of the Commission for the Codification of Laws. Appointed Minister of Justice, Feb. 20, 1914. Has been Minister of Agriculture and Commerce and of Education. Was Chinese Minister to Tokio, 1916-1919. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSUNG-YUAN, (章宗元)** .—Chekiang. Graduated in Commerce from University of California, U.S.A. Acting Vice-Minister of Finance, June, 1912. Vice-Minister, August, 1912. Financial Commissioner to England, November 6, 1912. Director-General, Bureau of Currency, 1914. For sometime President, Tongshan Engineering College. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG TSU-LIEN, (張祖廉)** .—Chekiang. Chujen. Was actively interested in the Lunghai Railway of which he became the Director-General, Oct., 1922. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG WEN-SHENG, (張文生)** .—Kiangsu. Lt. General in the Army. Has been in military service in Kiangsu since the Ching regime. Was Defence Commissioner of Suchow and Haichow, Ku., before he was appointed Tuchun of Anhui to succeed Ni Ssu Chung, 1920. Was made a Chiang Chun and summoned for duty in Peking, Oct., 1922, when the office of Tuchun in Anhui was abolished. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG WU (Dr. Phil. Chang Wu) (張武)** .—Anhui. Born 1883. Studied in Japan and Germany. Adjutant in the Anhui troops. Chief, Sur- veying Bureau. Councillor, Ministry of War. Member of Bureau of Economic Investigation and became its Councillor, Feb. 1920. Tungmenghui. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 793 ([scan leaf 866](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n866/mode/1up))

**CHANG WU** Dr. Phil., see Chang Wu. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YAO-CHENG, (張耀曾)** —Yunnan. Born 1884. Graduate of a University in Japan. When the Nanking Government was organized he was the representative of Yunnan. Was a member of both the Advisory Council of Nanking and Provisional Council in Peking. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan and the Constitutional Drafting Committee. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Minister of Justice, 1916. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YIN-TANG, (張蔭棠)** —Kwangtung. Accompanied Mr. Tang Shao-yi to Calcutta, 1904. Special Commissioner to investigate Tibetan affairs, 1905. Assistant Resident Tibet, December, 1906. Negotiated Trade Regulations, 1907-8. Senior Councillor, Waiwupu, January, 1909. Chinese Minister at Washington, August, 1909. Resident in Tibet after the Revolution. Member of Tsanchengyuan, 1914. Retired. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YING-CHU, (張映竹)** —Shantung. Born Oct. 1, 1875. Educated in Japan. Presiding Judge, Kirin Higher Procurator's Court. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YING-HUA (張英華)** —Chihli. Born 1888. Studied in Manchester, England. Salt Commissioner for Szechuan, Jan. 1919, and for Hotung, Shansi, 1921. Appointed Commissioner of Finance, Kansu, Jan. 1922. Adviser to General Wu Pei-fu and General Tsao Kun. Superintendent of Soochow Customs, June 18, 1922. Vice-Minister and Acting Minister of Finance, July-Sept. 1922. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YU-CHUAN, (張煜全)** —Kwangtung. Graduated from Pei Yang University, Tientsin, and Studied in Japan, America and Germany. Secretary, Waichiaopu, August 16, 1912. Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Kiangsu, June 26, 1913. Acting Superintendent of Customs, Wuhu, October 13, 1913; also Delegate for Foreign Affairs for Anhui. Councillor of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Attached to the Chinese delegation to Washington Conference, Nov. 1921. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YU-CHUN** —Military Adviser to the President, April, 1912. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YU-HAI, (張育海)** —Kiangsi. Born 1889. Bachelor of Law. Graduated from Department of Political Science, Imperial University of Tokio, Japan. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Finance, Military Government, April, 10, 1920. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YU-KENG (张玉庚)** —Shantung. Born 1870. Graduate of a Japanese Normal School. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was several years a teacher to various schools. Chinputang. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YU-TUNG** —Kweichow. Born 1881. A Graduate of a Japanese University. A Councillor of the Ministry of Interior, and one of the delegates for the Preparation of the National Assembly. Was a member of the Tung Meng Hui. Author of The Principle of the Constitution. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YUAN-CH'I, (張元奇)** —Fukien. Born 1860. Chin-shih 1886. Hochow Prefect and Vice-Minister of Education in Ching Dynasty. Vice-Minister of Interior, 1912. Civil Governor for Fukien, 1913. Head Civil Service Bureau 1914. Later became Civil Governor for Fengtien. Censor, 1916. Member of Parliament, 1919. Chief, Financial Investigation Commission, 1920. Author of Poetical Works. First class Chiaho decoration. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHANG YUAN-SHAN, (Yuan Shan Djang), (章元善)** —Kiangsu. Born October, 1892. Prepared at Kiangnan Provincial College, Nanking, and Tsinghua College, Peking. Graduated in 1915 from Cornell University, U.S.A. B.A. Chemist to the Peiyang Sanitary Department since September 1915. For three years was also chemist of the Chihli Provincial Industrial Laboratory. Was lecturer in Sanitary Chemistry, Government University, Peking and teacher in the Chihli First Middle School. Appointed General Secretary of Chihli Anti-Narcotic Society winter, 1918. Served as General Secretary of the North China International Society of Famine Relief September, 1920—Dec. 1921. Assistant Executive Secretary, China International Famine Relief Commission, Jan. 1922 to date. — p. 794 ([scan leaf 867](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n867/mode/1up))

**CHAO CH'ING (趙鯨)** —Yunnan. Born 1871. A Graduate of Law School in Yunnan. Was a teacher of both Normal School and Middle School in Yunnan. Established more than forty primary schools in Yunnan. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential order of Nov. 4,1913. — p. 795 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n868/mode/1up))

**CHAO CHING-HUA, (趙慶華)** —Chekiang. Born 1872. Graduated from the Diocesan School (Hongkong), and Telegraph College, Canton. Adviser to the President. Managing-Director, Canton Kowloon Railway, 1911-13; Tientsin-Pukow Railway, 1913-16; Shanghai-Nanking Railway and Shanghai-Ningpo-Hangchow Railway since 1916. Third class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. — p. 795 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n868/mode/1up))

**CHAO CH'UN-NIEN, (趙椿年)** —Kiangsu. Born 1867. Chujen of the late Dynasty. Vice-Minister of Finance, October, 1912, and Vice-Director of Shui Wu Chu, April, 1913. After obtaining the degree of Chin-shih, he was appointed magistrate of Kiangsi, where he held several responsible posts, and was ordered to Peking by special edict to fill several important posts as director of various Bureaux. Was a member of the late Tzuchengyuan, and Councillor of both the Ministries of Industry and Commerce and Finance. Kuomintang. Retired, October 13, 1913. Accountant of Bank of China, 1915. Appointed Joint Investigator of the Likin System. Supervisor of the Collection of Taxes in Peking, Dec., 1916. Vice-Director, Audit Bureau. Third class Paokwang and First class Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 795 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n868/mode/1up))

**CHAO CHUN-TING, (趙春霆)** —Hunan. Commissioner of Pacification, Kweichow, July 29, 1912. Military Governor of South Hunan, 1917. — p. 795 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n868/mode/1up))

**CHAO CHUNG-CH'I, (趙鍾奇)** —Yunnan. Born 1879. Graduated from Engineering Department, Army Officers' Training School, Japan. Brigadier-General in the Army. Chief of Staff to the Tuchun of Yunnan, 1914. Joined the Military Government and appointed Chief of Staff to the Tuchun of Szechuan, 1917. Taoyin of Yung Ning, Szechuan since 1918. Third Order of Merit, Second class Wenhu Decoration. — p. 795 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n868/mode/1up))

**CHAO ERH-HSUN, (趙爾巽)** —Chinese Bannerman. Prefect of Kweiyang, 1893. Judicial Commissioner of Anhui, April, 1895, and of Shansi, July, 1893. Financial Commissioner, New Dominion, November, 1898, and of Shansi, 1902. Governor, Hunan, January, 1903. Acting President of Board of Revenue, August, 1904. Tartar General, Mukden, May, 1905. Viceroy, Szechuan, May, 1907, but did not proceed. Viceroy, Hu-Kuang, September, 1907. Viceroy, Szechuan, March, 1908, where he remained until recalled to take up the post of Viceroy of Manchuria in April, 1911. He held this post until a Bill was passed placing him upon an equal footing with the Tutuhs of Kirin and Shengking. As Tutuh of Fengtien, however, he was virtually given supreme control of military and diplomatic affairs in the three Manchurian provinces. Resigned post of Tutuh of Fengtien, November 3, 1913. "Friend of Sungshan" (see Hsu Shih-ch'ang). Director, Ching History Compilation Bureau. First Order of Merit. — p. 795 ([scan leaf 868](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n868/mode/1up))

**CHAO HENG-CHIN** .—Consul-General at Vladivostok. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAO HENG-TI** , (趙恒惕).—Hunan. Born 1880. Studied in Japan. Graduated from a Japanese Military school. Was in service in Kwangsi and then in Hunan, before he became the Commanding Officer of the First Hunan Army Division. Now in Command of Hunan Army, although he is without the title of Tuchun. Second order of Merit. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CH'AO KUN-LIN (K. L. Chau)**  (巢坤霖).—Kwangtung. Born 1890. Graduate, Durham University, England. M.A. Professor of English and Latin, Tsing Hua College, Peking. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAO PING-LIN** , (趙炳麟).—Kwangsi. Born 1874. Hanlin Compiler and Censor in the late Ching Dynasty. A member of Chung Yi Yuan. Director of Mining Affairs in Kwangsi. Adviser to the President. Commissioner of Industries for Shansi. Oct. 1917. Author of Memorials of the Censorate, The Poetry and Essays of Po-Yen, Events of Kwang-Hsu and Hsuan Tung, Mining in Shansi, History of Russia, etc. Kuomintang. Second class Tashou Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAO SHIH-CH'IN**  (趙時欽).—Szechuan. Born 1877. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Most of his time was engaged in teaching-work, and was editor of Szechuan Daily News and Hsi-su Hsin Wen. Kung-hotang. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAO, T. L** , see Chao Tien-lin. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CH'AO T'I** , (趙倜).—Honan. Born 1870. Studied in the Peiyang Military Academy. Tuchun of Honan from June 1916 to May 10, 1922, when he was dismissed as a result of his brother's attack on General Feng Yu-hsiang's troops. Teh Wu Chiang Chun. First class Wenhu and Tashou Pao-Kuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAO TIEN-LIN (T. L. Chao)** , (趙天麟).—Tientsin. Born 1887. Studied in Pei Yang University, Tientsin, and in America. President of Pei-Yang University till 1919. Served in the Kailan Mining Administration. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAO TSENG-FANG** .—Supervisor of the Changsha Customs, and later of the Canton Customs, 1915. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAO YU-KE** , (趙玉珂).—Chihli. Chief of Staff of the Tuchun of Chihli and concurrently Defence Commissioner of Tientsin since April 1917. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHAU, K. L** , see Ch'ao Kun-lin. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHEN AN-LIANG**  (陳安良).—Kwangtung. Born 1869. Educated in Hongkong. A close friend of Dr. Sun Yat-sen. Was a teacher of English in Hongkong and Hunnan. Appointed Commissioner for Foreign Affairs for Hunan May, 1913. Resigned Oct. 1913. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHEN, C. W** , Educated in Queen's College, Hongkong. Appointed Chief of the Bureau of General Affairs in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in April, 1920. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CHEN CHAO-CH'ANG** , (陳昭常).—Kwangtung. Associated with Jeme Tien-yu in the construction of the Peking-Kalgan Railway. Junior Councillor of Ministry of Communications, December, 1906. Removed from office, January, 1907. Acting Deputy Lieutenant-General of Hunch'un, December, 1907. Acting Governor of Kirin, August, 1908. Governor of Kirin, July, 1910. Tutuh of Kirin, 1912. Member of the Kung-hotang. Resigned position of Tutuh and Chief Civil Administrator of Kirin, June 13, 1913. Appointed Civil Administrator of Kwangtung, June 14, 1913. Did not take up duties. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CH'EN CHEN-HSIEN** , (陳振先).—Kwangtung. Born 1876. Graduate of the University of California. Became a Christian at 19 years of age. Was attaché to the Chinese Legation at Washington, and an interpreter of the Chinese Consulate in California. When returned to China obtained the title of Chinshih, 1907, and Hanlin Compiler, 1908. Was five years Chief of the Department for the Agricultural Experiments, and Director of the Agricultural School at Fengtien. Sent to Europe and America by Viceroy Chao of Mukden to study the system of cultivation and colonization, 1911. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Forestry, May, 1912. Minister of the same Ministry, July, 1912. Acting Minister of Education, March, 1913, and resigned the post of Minister of Education May, 1913. Resigned the post of Minister of Agriculture and Forestry, September, 1913. Author of Ten Rules Governing the Use of Variant Tones in Cantonese, One View of the Integral Calculus, etc., etc. — p. 796 ([scan leaf 869](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n869/mode/1up))

**CH'EN CH'ENG-HSIU, (陳承修)** Kiangsu. Born 1886. Educated at Nanyuan College, Shanghai, and Technical College, Osaka, Japan. Delegate to The Panama Exhibition, 1915. Chief of Department of Labour and Commerce, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, since 1917. Second class Wenhu, Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CHEN CHI-YIN** Councillor, Ministry of Industry and Commerce, September 5, 1912. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CHEN CHI-YU (陳其尤)** Kwangtung. Born 1892. Studied economics in a Japanese university. Appointed Superintendent of Customs, Chaochow, and concurrenly Commissioner of Foreign Affairs since Oct. 1920. Mintang. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CH'EN CH'I-YUAN, (陳其瑗)** Kwangtung. Born 1887. Graduated from the Mining Department of Imperial Peking University. Vice-President, Canton Y.M.C.A. Since Dec. 31, 1919. Author of Notes on Philippine Islands and of "Metallurgy." Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CHEN CHIN-T'AO, (陳錦濤)** Nanhai, Kwangtung. Born 1871. M.A. Ph.D. Graduated from Queen's College, Hongkong; Columbia University, New York, U.S.A.; Yale University, New Haven, U.S.A. One of China's best known financial experts. Former Manager of the Taching Government Bank. Chief, Department of Budgets, Ministry of Finance. Organizer and Vice-Director, Bureau of Printing and Engraving. Chairman, Currency Reform Commission. Member, Tzu Cheng Yuan. Vice-Minister of Finance in Yuan Shih-kai's Cabinet. Minister of Finance, Nanking Provincial Government, 1911. Representative to the International Conference of Chambers of Commerce, Boston, Auditor-General, Central Audit Bureau. Financial Commissioner to Europe. November, 1913 Minister of Finance and Acting Minister of Foreign Affairs, 1916. Minister of Finance, Canton Military Government, 1920. Author of Distribution of Wealth, Public Schools in Four Countries, etc. Non-partisan. Second class Pao Kuang Ta Shou Chiaho Decoration. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CHEN CHIUNG-MING, (陳烱明)** Kwangtung. Tutuh of Kwangtung, June 1913. Drove out the Kwangsi Tuchun Mo Yung-hsin in 1920 and was appointed Civil Governor. Concerned in the Yunnan revolt, 1915-16. Commander-in-Chief of the Kwangtung troops. Civil Governor of Kwangtung. Minister of War in the Canton Government. In 1922 his troops attacked and overthrew Sun Yat-sen, and after the latter's flight, Chen re-assumed command of all the Kwangtung forces, but was himself driven out of Canton in January, 1923. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CH'EN EN-TAO, (陳恩燾)** Fukien. Born 1860. Educated at Naval College, Foochow and in a naval school in England. Major General. Supervisor, Amoy Customs, Oct. 1913, and concurrently Delegate for Foreign Affairs. Department Chief, Ministry of Navy since 1918. Third class Chiaho, Paokuang Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CHEN ER-NGAN** Governor, Szechuan, 1915. — p. 797 ([scan leaf 870](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n870/mode/1up))

**CHEN HSIA-LING, (陳遐齡)** —Military Commissioner of the Szechuan. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CHEN HUAN** —General. Deputy Chief of the General Staff, January, 1915. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CHEN HUAN-CHANG, (陳煥章)** —Kwangtung. Born 1880. Is a pupil of Kang Yu Wei and advocates that Confucianism be made China's State religion. Editor of "Chinese Reformer," 1899-1900. Professor of Chinese, Shih Ming High School, 1902. Promoted to be Principal of the same institution in the following year. Chinshih, 1905. On a government scholarship, Dr. Chen proceeded to America to secure a modern education. After completing the preparatory courses, he entered Chicago and Columbia Universities 1907. Ph.D. 1911. Returned to China, January, 1911. Formed the National Confucian Association. Was its President since its founding in 1911. Made Legal Adviser to the President, 1913. At the same time, he became editor of "Confucian Association Monthly." Member of 2nd Parliament. Is now adviser to the President and to the Cabinet office. Wrote a number of books on Confucianism. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CH'EN HUAN-NAN (陳煥南)** —Hunan. Born 1881. Graduate of Middle School at Hupeh, and a returned student from Japan. After returning from abroad was engaged as teacher in Government schools, and established forty-eight Primary schools in his native town. Was four years agitating for Revolution in Kwangsi; afterwards fled from his native place to evade arrest. Rendered valuable services in winning Kwangsi to the revolutionary cause. Was clerk to the Military Department in Kwangsi Tutuh's Yamen; Military Adviser of the 3rd Army Division. Political Adviser to the Tutuh of Hunan; member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Promoted Chin Liu Antimony Mines, Hsinming, Ho. in the autumn of 1915. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CHEN I (Chen Yi) (陳繹)** —Fukien. Born 1885. Graduate, Peiyang University, Tientsin, and London School of Economics, London University. B.Sc. (Ecom). Auditor, Foreign Loan Department, 1913-16. Joined Bank of China, Peking. Chief Secretary, Chinese American Bank of Commerce, since 1920. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CHEN I-FAN (Ivan Chen) (陳貽範)** —Commissioner for Foreign Affairs Kiangsu, April 15. 1913. Commissioner for Pacification of Tibet, June 14, 1913. Chinese Delegate to Simla Conference. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CH'EN KUANG-YPAN, (陳光遠)** —Chihli. Of the Chihli clique in the Peiyang Party. Lieut.-General. Commissioner of Occupation, Chihfeng, July 21, 1913. On the dismissal of Tuan Chi-jui he joined other Tuchuns in their opposition to President Li. Was appointed Chihli Titu during Chang Hsun's Coup d'Etat July 1, 1917. Appointed Tuchun of Kiangsi, concurrently Commanding General, 12th Division of Army, July 27, 1917. Resigned June 15, 1922. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CHEN KUO-HSIANG (陳國祥)** —Kweichow. Born 1880. Chujen 1897; Chinshih-Hanlin Compiler, 1903. Graduated from a law school in Japan. Upon return to China, he became director of Honan Law College which position he held for about three years. Besides, he was at various times interested in the Honan Provincial Assembly, local Government, etc. Elected to the first Republican Parliament. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CH'EN LU, (Tcheng Loh), (陳篆)** —Fukien. Born 1876. Graduated from Hupeh Tsu Ch'iang College and from La Faculté de Droit de Paris. Licencié en Droit. Chief, Department of Foreign Affairs, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, April, 1912. Interpreter to the five Commissioners sent to Europe, 1906. Attaché to the Chinese Legation, Paris. Delegate to the Second Peace Conference at The Hague. Teacher, Law Department, Government University, Peking. Chinshih; Hanlin. Minister to Mexico. High Military Commissioner and Resident-General at Urga, 1915. Appointed vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs, May 4, 1918. In charge of the Ministry Nov. 10, 1918 to 1920. Minister to France, 1921. Author of historical works on Mongolia and Types of French Documents, etc. First class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. Second class Paokuang Tashou Chiaho Decoration. Wearer of medals from the Russian, Japanese, Belgian, Swedish governments. — p. 798 ([scan leaf 871](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n871/mode/1up))

**CH'EN MAO-TING (陳懋鼎)** .—Fukien. Chinshih, 1890. Acting Junior Secretary and Acting Senior Secretary of Waichiaopu, 1909. Councillor of Privy Council, August, 1911. Councillor of Waichiaopu, April, 1912. Superintendent Nanking Customs and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs at Nanking, July 22—Nov. 20, 1914. Chining Taoyin Nov. 20, 1914,—July 30, 1915. Secretary of Cabinet, July, 1917. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CH'EN PAO-CH'EN (陳寶琛)** .—Fukien. Born about 1849. Chinshih, 1868. Sub-Chancellor of Grand Secretariat, with rank of a Vice-President of Board of Ceremonies, April, 1910. Interested in education, railway development and Anti-Opium Campaign in his native province, while retiring from official occupation, after becoming for sometime Nanyang Viceroy. Governor of Shansi, June, 1911. Tutor to the ex-Emperor, since July, 1911. Advisery Minister to Privy Council, August, 1911. Vice-President Imperial Historiography Department, 1912. Appointed to the Council of Regency in the abortive restoration of the Manchu Dynasty, July, 1917. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CH'EN P'EI-K'UN, (陳培錕)** .—Fukien. Hanlin. Superintendent, Provincial College, Agriculture College, Fukien. Taoyin in Fukien and Police Superintendent of Foochow. Shamen Taoyin since Nov. 1918. Second class Chiaho and Third class Wenhu decorations. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CHEN PI (陳璧)** .—Minister of Communications in the late Ching Dynasty. Councillor of Tsangchengyuan. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CHEN PING-K'UN, (陳炳焜)** .—Kwangsi. Born 1861. Was Associate Director of Kwangsi Martial Court. General of the 1st Kwangsi Army Division, and concurrently Defence Commissioner of Kweilin. Holds the rank of Lieut. General in the Army. Civil and Military Governor of Kwangsi and Military Governor of Kwangtung. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CH'EN SHAO-T'ANG, (陳紹唐)** .—Kwangsi. Born 1889. Studied in the University of Pennsylvania, U.S.A. Teacher, Kwangsi Provincial College, Hydraulic Engineering College. General Secretary, Nanking Y.M.C.A. Translated Gettell's work on politics. Eighth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CHEN SHIH-CHANG (A. Pinzen Cheng) (陳世瑢)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1885. Graduate, University of Birmingham, B.Sc. Admitted by examination as Associate to the Institute of Chemistry of Great Britain and Ireland in 1915. Elected as Fellow of the Institute of Chemistry in 1919 (F.I.C.) Lecturer on chemistry to Peking Technical College and Peking High Normal College, 1916-21. Professor of Chemistry, Peking National University, 1917. Became Dean of the Department of Chemistry. Consulting Chemist to Liu-ho-kou Mining Co., Vice-President of the Chinese Society of Chemical Industry. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CHEN, T. C** , see Ch'en T'ung chi. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CHEN TAO-YUAN (陳道源)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1888. Graduate of Peiyang University, Tientsin, and Cornell University, U.S.A. M.E. Glasgow University, Scotland. Joined Vickers Ltd., 1910-12. Served as draughtsman. Associate Member, Institute of Naval Architects, London. Draughtsman, Kiangnan Dock, Shanghai, 1912-14. English Secretary to Salt Chief Inspectorate, 1915. Chief Engineer Superintendent, Government Dockyard, Foochow, 1915-17. Technical Expert, Ministry of Navy, 1917 to date. Appointed to naval commission to study war preparations of the allies, 1917. Secretary to the Chinese Delegation, Washington Conference, 1921. — p. 799 ([scan leaf 872](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n872/mode/1up))

**CHEN TEH-HUI (陳德輝)** Kiangsu. Born 1880. Graduated in America. At one time Manager of Bank of Kiangsu. Chief Manager of the Commercial Savings Bank, Shanghai, 1915. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CH'EN TENG-SHAN, (陳登山)** Hupeh. Born Nov. 9, 1864. Graduated in Normal and law courses in colleges in Japan. Provincial Republican Government, 1912. Councillor, Ministry of Army, Peking, July 1912. Appointed to that post, February, 1920. Author of notes on finance, prison, local government, etc. Fourth class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CH'EN TIEN-CHI, (陳天祺)** Chekiang. Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Soochow, April 15, 1913. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHEN T'UNG-CHI (T. C. Chen) (陳同紀)** Kwangtung. Born Dec. 9, 1885. Japanese educated. Pursued commercial subjects while in Japan. Was once connected with the Board of Education in 1912 and also head of Checking department of the Taching Bank. Sent to Japan to investigate financial administration. Was allowed to work in the Japanese Board of Finance and a noted Japanese Bank for sometime. Upon his return, he was in 1912 appointed to a post in the Ministry of Finance, and also in the Bank of China. Having held one or two official positions in Tsitsihar, he became superintendent of Customs first at Kweichow, Sze, and in 1915 at Chungking. Became Manager of Chungking Branch of the Bank of Communications. Fourth class Chiaho Decoration. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CH'EN WEI** Head of Department of Ministry of Finance, November 10, 1912. Acting Chief Director of Bank of China, July 5, 1913. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHEN WEI-KENG** Director of the Salt Transport Office in Kirin and Heilungkiang, 1915. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHEN WEN-TSAO** Finance Secretary of Shensi, 1915. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHEN YI, (Fukien), see Chen I**  — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CH'EN YI, (陳毅)** Hunan. Senior Secretary, Ministry of Communications, January, 1912. Councillor, Bureau for Mongolia and Tibet, August, 1912. Deputy for Organization of National Assembly, September, 1912 Secretary, Ministry of Communications, December 18, 1912. Resident Commissioner at the Court of the Hutukhtu, Urga, 1917. Again, 1920. Cashiered April, 1921. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CH'EN YU, (陳鈺)** Shansi. For many years, a magistrate in Chihli. Chief of Financial Department, Shansi, February, 1913. Chief Civil Administrator, Shansi, June 4, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan, 1914. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHEN YU-CHUNG** Kiangsi. Born 1873. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan, Chinputang. Chairman of the Peking Chamber of Commerce, 1918. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CH'EN YUNG-SHAN** Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Swatow, April 25, 1913. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHENG, A. PINZEN, see Chen Shih-chang**  — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHENG CHIA-HAO** Hupeh. Born 1881. Educated in Jih Hsin College of Wuchang, and a college in Japan. One of the representatives in the negotiations between the North and the South. Was Councillor of the Tutuh's Yamen of Hupeh, and the examiner of the high officials of Hupeh. Adviser to the Right Wing of the Northern Expeditionary Army. Was proprietor of Hupeh Daily News. Member of the Tsan Yi Yuan. Established Te-yu-hui in Wuchang to plan for Revolution; this hui was reorganized and became Minhsia, which was finally amalgamated into Kunghotang. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHENG CH'IEN, (鄭謙)** Kiangsu. Commissioner of Administration for Heilungkiang, Nov. 1917. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHENG CHIH-FAN, (Tzefan Tsen) (鄭芝蕃)** Kiangsu. Born 1887. Graduated from Cornell University and Harvard University. B.A. Was teacher to various colleges and schools in Shanghai, Anking, Nanking and Peking. Professor, Tsing Hua College, Peking. — p. 800 ([scan leaf 873](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n873/mode/1up))

**CHENG CH'IN, (鄭钦)** —Shantung. Born 1868. Studied in Normal School, Shantung and Graduated from a Japanese College. Chairman, Shantung Provincial Assembly since Sept. 1, 1918. Fifth Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CHENG CHING-FU (程慶福)** —Anhui. Born 1864. Chairman of General Chamber of Commerce, Nanking, since Nov. 1920. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CHENG FENG-JEN (鄭豊稔)** —Fukien. Born 1873. Graduated from a normal school. Admitted to the Bar, 1910. Elected member of Fukien Provincial Assembly in 1912, becoming its Vice-Chairman the following year. Was Presiding Judge, High Court of Justice for Fukien from June 1919 to Dec. 1920. Mintang. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CHENG KE, (程克)** —A native of Kaifeng, Honan, Mr. Chen Ke, the present Minister of Justice, graduated from the Law School in the Manchu regime. He studied in the Imperial University of Tokyo where he graduated in law. He is now 40 years old. Upon his return from Japan, he organized a revolutionary organ in Tientsin with the object of overthrowing the Manchu regime. He was arrested by the police, but through good offices of Chao Ping-chun, former Chihli Governor and Prime Minister he was liberated and given official positions. After the establishment of the Republic, Mr. Chen became Councillor of the Ministry of the Interior, a deputy of the Bureau for Parliamentary Affairs and Adviser to the President. In 1913 he was elected a Senator. In June 1914 he became Taoyin of Hai Chung, Shensi, and it was at the time when General Lu Chien-chang was Chiangchun of the province. General Lu tried to encourage the plantation of poppy, but Mr. Chen strongly objected to this policy. Consequently he had to be transferred to the position of High Commissioner for Altai in December of 1915. During his residence in Altai he looked after both foreign and Chinese affairs of the place with satisfaction to all concerned. He remained there for five years, and two years ago he retired. Last year he assisted General Feng Yu-hsiang in Honan in the management of military affairs. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CHENG YEN-HSUN (程炎動)** —Anhui. Born 1885. Graduated from Chihli Police Officers' Training School, 1906. Appointed Intendant of River Police in Anhui, 1913, Superintendent of Police for Anhui and concurrently of Anking since 1919. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG SHIH-CHI, (程世濟)** —Kiangsi. Born 1876. Educated in Chen Tan College, Shanghai, and a Graduate of Law School at Peking. Was secretary to a railway company, and other yamens; two years as a teacher of a Railway College at Shanhaikwan; attaché to the Chinese Legation at Paris for four years; three years a clerk in the Railway Department of the Ministry of Communications. Director of Tao-Ching Railway Company, appointed in October, 1911. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG TE-CH'UAN, (程德全)** —Szechuan. Acting Tartar General of Heilungkiang in the winter of 1906. Acting Governor of Heilungkiang, May, 1907. Resigned, March, 1908. Acting Governor of Fengtien, May, 1909. Governor of Fengtien, July, 1909. Governor of Kiangsu, April, 1910. Appointed Tutuh of Kiangsu on April 13, 1912. Belongs to no party. Superseded during the Rebellion, 1913. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG TO (程鐸)** —Kiangsi. Born 1884. Graduate of a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. After returning from Japan started a Law College in Kiangsi. During the time of Revolution he with a few friends organized the War Office in Kiangsi, and became Chief of the Bureau of Foreign Affairs. Then was elected as member of the Provisional Assembly of Kiangsi. He was appointed Magistrate of Chi An, which post he refused to accept, but was engaged as teacher and director of several law colleges. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 801 ([scan leaf 874](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n874/mode/1up))

**CHENG WEI-FAN** (Cheng Wei-van), (鄭維潘).—Chekiang. Born 1891. Graduate, Ohio State University, U.S.A. M.S. Telephone Engineer of the Ministry of Communications. Electric Engineer of the Peking Electric Tramway Co. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHENG WEI-VAN** See Cheng Wei-fan. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHENG YUAN-LIANG** (鄭元良).—Shantung. Born 1880. Commissioner of Police for Kansu and Superintendent of Police, Lanchow, since 1917. Second class Chiaho, third class Wenhu decorations. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHI CHIN-CHUN** (汲金純).—Fengtien. Born 1877. Commanding General of the 28th Army Division. Holds the rank of Lieutenant-General in the Army. Appointed Tutung of Jehol, Sept., 1912. Relieved. May 29, 1922. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CH'I CHUNG-CHIA** (齊忠甲).—Kirin. Born 1863. Hanlin Compiler, and a Taotai of Kweichow. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. In Tang-yuenhsien, Kirin, he started an immigration enterprise, and within the space of a few years he has succeeded in sending several thousands of families to colonize several thousands of "chings" of waste land. Chinputang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHI CHUNG-YIN** (籍忠寅).—Chihli. Studied in Japan. Member of Chihli Tzu Yi Chu. Dean of Peiyang Law Colleges. Member of Tsan Yi Yuan, Commissioner of Finance, Yunnan, July, 1915 Councillor in the office of Tutuh, Yunnan. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHI HSIEH-YUAN** (齊燮元).—Chihli. Was Defence Commissioner of Nanking and concurrently Commanding General of the Sixth Army Division. Appointed on Dec. 3, 1920 Acting Tuchun of Kiangsu after the death of Li Shun, which post he has held ever since. Was made a full General in the Army, Oct. 10, 1922. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CH'I KO-I'AN** .—Outer Mongolia. Born 1888. Graduate of the Police College Peking. Prince of the 4th Order of Ko-er-ko; member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan for Outer Mongolia. Chingputang. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHI YANG** (戚揚).—Chekiang. Born 1861. A Hanlin scholar in the Manchu Court. Has been magistrate of many hsiens in Fukien, Kiangsi, Kiangsu and Chekiang. At the time of revolution, Chi was made Secretary in charge of Domestic Affairs for Kiangsi. Was Civil Governor of Kiangsi till Spring, 1921. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHI YAO-CHUN** (齊耀瑤).—Kirin. Born June 1877. Appointed Commissioner of Finance for Kirin, Dec. 8, 1919. Commissioner of Industry, 1920. Superintendent of Customs, Antung, since May, 1920. Second class Chiaho and third class Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CH'I YUEH-LIN** (齊耀琳).—Kirin. Yung-ting-ho Tao Chihli, February, 1908. Tientsin Tao, April, 1908. Jud. Comm., Chihli, January, 1910. Provincial Treasurer, Honan, November, 1911. Governor, Honan, December, 1911 · resigned, April, 1912. Chief Civil Administrator, Kirin, June 13, 1913. Civil Governor of Kiangsu, August, 1915-1919. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHI YUEH-SHAN** (齊耀珊).—Kirin. Chinshih of Ching Dynasty. Has been in Hupeh for some years holding various positions. Member of Tsangchengyuan. Civil Governor of Chekiang. Transferred to Shantung. Minister of Interior. Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, Dec. 25, 1921. Concurrently Acting Minister of Education till April 9, 1922. Resigned, June 11, 1922. First class Wenhu decoration. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHIA CHI-CH'UAN** (賈濟川).—Honan. Born 1885. Studied in the Provincial school of Honan. Later entered Chihli law college, graduating 1911. Kuanchung Taoyin, Shensi, and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Shensi, since July 11, 1918. Member of Parliament. Chinputang. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 802 ([scan leaf 875](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n875/mode/1up))

**CHIA PIN-CHING (賈賓卿)** —Acting Deputy Lieutenant-Governor, Kweihua-cheng, June 16, 1913. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG CHAO-CHUNG, (江朝宗)** —Anhui. Acting Commander of Gendarmerie, July 14, 1912. Deputy Lieutenant-General, Bordered Red Chinese Banner, August 19, 1912. In June, 1917, he accepted the post of Acting Premier in order to countersign Li Yuan-hung's mandate dissolving Parliament. Third Order of Merit. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG HSI-MING (蔣羲明)** —Hupeh. Born 1877. A Graduate of a University in Japan. Delegate for the preparation of the establishment of the Bank of China. Was a teacher in the Government University in Peking. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Author of Salt Government, Taxes, etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG KO-CH'ING** —Chief Administrative Secretary, Fukien. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG MOU-HSI (蔣懋熙)** —Kiangsu. Acting Police Commissioner, Honan, in the Ching dynasty. Acting Commissioner of Finance, Kiangsu. Commissioner of Finance, Hupeh, April, 1915. Transferred to Chekiang in June, and to Honan, Nov. 1917. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG P'EI-HUA, (蔣培華)** —Fukien. Born 1874. Studied in the Ying Hua and Fu Ying Academies. President, Foochow Y.M.C.A. since 1913. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG TIEN-TO, (江天鐸)** —Kwangtung. Born 1879. Graduated from Waseda University, Japan. Was a teacher to High Police School in Peking, 1910. Member of Chung Yi Yuan, 1913. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, summer, 1917. In charge of the Ministry, 1920. Chairman of various commissions of food supply, etc. Appointed Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, June 15, 1922. Second class Tashou Chiaho, First class Tashou Chiaho, First class Wenhu Decorations. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG TSENG-WEI** —Sent as Pacification Commissioner to Yunnan, December, 1915. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG TSO-PIN, (蔣作賓)** —Hupeh. Born 1883. After graduation in a Middle School he studied in Japan, and is a Graduate of the Training School for Military Officers in Japan. Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. Played a prominent part in the Revolution of 1911, for which he agitated for a long time. Is Lieutenant-General of the Army, and was the Vice-President of the Ministry of War, appointed on January 4, 1912. Was a member of Tung Meng Hui, but resigned the membership. Led people's army during the struggle for a Self Government in Hupeh, Summer 1921. Tuchun Wang Chan-yuan was then compelled to give up his once-powerful dual post of Tuchun of Hupeh and Inspecting-General for Hunan and Hupeh. Third Order of Merit. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG TSUN-I, (T. Y. Tsiang), (蔣雲緯)** —Chekiang. Born Nov. 13, 1877. Chujen and Chinshih. Member of Ministry of Communications since 1908. Director of Chinese Government Telegraphs from 1918. to May 13, 1922. Second class Tashou Chiaho and second class Wenhu decorations. Also received medals of merit from Denmark and Japan. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG WEI-CH'IAO, (蔣維喬)** —Kiangsu. Born 1872. Educated in the High School of Nanking. Councillor to the Ministry of Education. Chief Secretary to the Ministry of Education at Nanking, May, 1912. Director of Ai-kuo Girls' School at Shanghai, and was one of the editors of the Commercial Press at Shanghai. Appointed Commissioner of Education, Kiangsi, Oct. 16, 1921, but did not proceed. Non-partisan. Author of several Chinese Readers for the Primary schools. — p. 803 ([scan leaf 876](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n876/mode/1up))

**CHIANG WEN-HSI, (姜文熙)** —Kiangsu. Born 1875. Graduated from the Peiyang Medical College, M.D. Councillor, Medical Department, Ministry of Army since 1917. Second class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIANG YEN-HSING, (蔣雁行)** —Judicial Commissioner, Chiang Huai, July 5, 1913. Defence Commissioner, Kiang Pei, August 27, 1913. Tutung of Suiyuan, Oct., 1916. Deputy-Chief of General Staff at present. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIANG YUNG, (江庸)** —Szechuan. Born April 29, 1878. Graduated from the Political Science Department of Waseda University, Japan. Chujen. Judge, Supreme Court 1905. Chief Justice, Higher Court, Peking, 1912. Vice-Minister of Justice, 1914. Acting Minister of Justice, 1917. Director of Students studying in Japan, 1918. Appointed Chief, Law Codification Bureau July 29, 1920. Second class Tashou Chiaho, First class Tashou Chiaho decorations. Second Order of Merit. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIEN NUNG-HSUN, (錢能訓)** —Chekiang. Born 1870. Chujen and Chinshih. Member, Board of Justice in Ching Dynasty. Was once Commissioner of Justice for Shensi and Acting Governor of that province. Secretary of State 1915. Chief of Administrative Court 1917. Minister of the Interior, 1918. Appointed Prime Minister, February, 1918. Resigned 1919. President of Commercial and Industrial Bank of China. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIEN SHAN** —Yunnan. Born 1877. Censor. Graduate of Yunnan Law School. Established Primary schools for boys and girls in his native town, and opened an Industrial Institute for those who were unemployed. Organized Tien Hsueh Hui, a revolutionary organ. Was member of the Tzu Yi Chu of Yunnan. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan and member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Has been connected with some enterprises in cultivation of waste land, cattle raising, and salt business. Chinputang. Author of Historical Essays, Explanation on some Difficult Parts of Olden Poems, The Principles of Law, How to develop Yunnan, and The Future of China. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN T'AI, (Dr. Tsien Tai), (錢泰)** —Chekiang. Born April 5, 1883. LL.D., University of Paris. Councillor, Ministry of Justice since Dec. 25, 1915. Wrote several books on legal subjects in French. Third class Chiaho, Wenhu decorations. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN, Y. M** see Ch'ien Yung Ming. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN YUNG-MING, (Y. M. Ch'ien), (錢永銘)** —Kiangsu. Graduated in a Commercial College in Japan. Manager, Bank of Communications, Shanghai branch. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIN CHAO-YEN (金兆槐)** —Chekiang. Born 1878. A Graduate of the Government University in Peking. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan and the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Was Director of the Middle School in Chinhwafu; a teacher of various other schools; the Magistrate of Yungchia Hsien. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIN CHIH, (Thomas King), (靳志)** —Honan. Born 1877. Chinshih Studied in France and England. Member of the Boards of Labour and of Commerce in the Ching dynasty. Secretary to the President, 1912. Secretary to the Chinese Legation in Holland, 1913. Secretary to the Cabinet. Member of the Bureau of Statistics. Secretary to the Chinese Legation in Belgium, 1918. Charge d'Affaires, 1919.
