Secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1921. Joined the Chinese Delegation as French Secretary, Washington Conference, 1921. Officer of Rites in the Presidential Palace. Councillor, Ministry of Communications. Adviser to the Lunghai Railway. Author of poetic works. Second class Chiaho and third class Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIN MING-PO, (Konway M. P. Tsing), (秦銘博)** —Kiangsu. Graduate of Nanyang College, Shanghai, and University of Leeds, England. Assistant Foreman, Tongshan Locomotive Works, Peking-Mukden Railway, 1910-11. Locomotive Superintendent, Kirin Changchun Railway, 1911-13. Engineer Expert. Ministry of Communications, 1911-13. District Locomotive Superintendent, Works Manager, Assistant Chief Locomotive Superintendent, Tientsin-Pukow Railway, 1913 to date. — p. 804 ([scan leaf 877](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n877/mode/1up))

**CHIN PANG-CHENG, (P. C. King), (金邦正)** —Anhui. Born June 1886. Studied in the Nankai Middle School, Tientsin and the Customs College, Peking. Graduated from Cornell University, (U.S.A.) B.A. M.A. (in forestry). Principal, First Provincial Forestry College of Anhui, Dec. 1914; Director, Forestry Bureau, Jan., 1915; Principal, Agriculture College, Peking, Oct., 1917; Director, Tsing Hua College since Sept., 1920. to Spring, 1922. Member of Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIN PANG-PING, (金邦平)** —Anhui. Joint Examiner, with Wu Chao-hsu, an American adviser and a Japanese adviser, of students who have graduated abroad. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, March, 1915, Minister April 23, 1915. till June, 6. Director-General of the National Conservancy Board January, 1916. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIN SHAO-TSENG (金紹曾)** —Tientsin. Councillor of Ministry of War. Departmental Chief in the Ministry. Promoted Vice-Minister, 1921. In charge of the Ministry, May, 1922. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIN YUN-P'ENG (靳雲鵬)** —Shantung. Born 1877. Graduated from the Pei Yang Military Academy and upon graduation was appointed to command only a few soldiers. Was made a military officer in Chekiang and promoted to the post of Commander-in-Chief of Chekiang troops. Was given the rank of Lieutenant-General during the revolution of 1911 when he was with General Tuan Chi-jui, Commander-in-Chief of Imperial Army. Appointed Military Governor of Shantung in 1913. When Chang Huai-chih was to be appointed Tuchun of Shantung, General Chin was "promoted" to the Chiangchunfu in June, 1916. Sent to Japan with Chu Tung-feng in November 1917 to witness the manoeuvres. Appointed to the War Participation Bureau and there acted on behalf of his Chief, General Tuan Chi-jui. Appointed Minister of War, June 11, 1919. This position he held till May 1921. Was Prime Minister, winter 1919 to Dec. 18, 1921. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIN YUNG, (金永)** —Chekiang. Governor of Shansi, 1914-15. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIU C. Y** see Chiu Chang-yun. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIU CHANG-YUN, (C. Y. Chiu), (裘昌運)** —Kiangsu. Born 1890. Graduate, Soochow University, B. A. Purdue University (U.S.A.), B.S. Post Graduate Work Columbia University, U.S.A. Secretary: National Oil Bureau 1914; Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, 1915; and the Senate, 1917. Was in business in Shanghai, 1917-21. Secretary, Shantung Rehabilitation Commission, 1922. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIU KUAN-FEN (邱冠棻)** —Kiangsi. Born 1885. A Graduate of a University in Japan. Was a Chujen and a Magistrate in Shensi, in the late Dynasty. During the Revolution he was made Chief of Finance Department in the War Office, and was a sectional chief in the Finance Department of the Province. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIU NGUI** see Ch'ou Ao. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHIU TUN-YU** —Chekiang. Jud. Com. and Hei-Shui Tao Heilungkiang, winter, 1906 Commissioner of Heilungkiang. April, 1909, Head of Law Preparation Department, January 19, 1913. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CH'OU AO, (Chiu Ngui), (仇鰲)** —Hunan. Born 1879. Graduated from a law college in Tokyo. Was connected with the Law Department in the Nanking Republican Government, 1912. Appointed Superintendent of Customs, Changsha and Yochow and concurrently Commissioner of Foreign Affairs, 1917-1920. Kuomintang. — p. 805 ([scan leaf 878](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n878/mode/1up))

**CHOU CH'ANG-SHOU** Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Ningpo, April 15, 1913. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU CHAO-HSIANG (周肇祥)** .—Chekiang. Police Intendant, Szechuan, August, 1910. Vacated office, July, 1911. Intendant of Industry, Fengtien, November, 1911. Acting Inspector-General of Police Administration, Peking, July 18, 1913. Acting Salt Commissioner, Shantung, July 22, 1913. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU CHIN-PIAO (周晉鏢)** .—Chekiang. Born 1847. Was magistrate in Kiangsi. Interested in commercial enterprises. Promoted and was several times elected Chairman of the Shanghai General Chamber of Commerce. Taoyin of Shanghai, 1914. Special Delegate of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Kiangsu, 1915. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU CHUN, (Zin-Yen Chow; Z. Y. C. Chow), (周均)** .—Shanghai, Ku. Born Sept. 18, 1892. B.A., St. John's University, Shanghai. Formerly teacher in Kiangsu. Was secretary to various commissioners of education and Foreign Affairs. Secretary to the Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Chekiang. Sixth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU HOU-KUN (周厚坤)** .—Kiangsu. Studied in Shanghai, Tongshan and America. S.B., S.M., Chief of Transportation Department. Tayeh Mines, 1917. Chief of Technical Division, Hanyehping Iron and Coal Co., Shanghai, 1918 to date. Is English Secretary, Po Lo Coal Mining Co. Chief Engineer, Pao Hua Iron Mining Co., and Chin Yeh Iron Mining Co. Inventor of a Chinese typewriter. Author of "Dynamic Stability of Aeroplanes," "Strength of Bamboo," etc. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU HSIN, (周心)** .—Kwangtung, Born April 1882. Studied in the Naval College and Law College, Kwangtung. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Finance, October, 1918. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU HSUEH-HSI, (周學熙)** .—Anhui. Salt Commissioner, Chihli, January, 1907. Mourning, December, 1907. Promoter and Director of Peking Waterworks. Elected President of the Anhui Railway Co., with approval of the Throne March, 1908. Was largely instrumental in arranging the amalgamation of the Kaiping and Lanchow Mines during the winter of 1911-12. Elected Minister of Finance in Lu Chenghsiang's Cabinet, on July 26, 1912. Director-General of Revenue Council, December 18, 1912. Resigned on the formation of Hsiung Hisling's Cabinet. Minister of Finance, 1914. Director-General of the Salt Bureau. 1915. Resigned, 1915. Actively interested in Cotton Industry since 1919. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU SHAN-TUNG, (S. T. Chow), (周善同)** .—Kiangsu. Studied in Edinburgh M.A. Traffic Manager, Tientsin-Pukow Railway, Mar-1914. Asst. Managing-Director, Peking-Mukden Railway, May 1922. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU SHU-MO, (周樹模)** .—Hupeh. Born 1864. Hanlin, 1885. Was commissioned to conduct the civil service examinations in Shansi and in Kwangtung by the Manchu Emperor. Was invited by Viceroy Chang Chih-tung to teach in the Lianghu College. Was also a censor in the Ching Dynasty. Was once Commissioner of Public Instructions, Senior Assistant to the Viceroy of Three Eastern Provinces. Governor of Heilungkiang. After repeated invitations of ex-President Yuan, Mr. Chou accepted in 1915 the position of President of the Administrative Court and that of President of the Commission for Punishing High Officials. These two positions, he resigned after a year's service. Author of "Memorials of Heilungkiang" and "Poems of Siu Kou Tsai." First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 806 ([scan leaf 879](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n879/mode/1up))

**CHOU TZU-CH'I, (周自齊)** —Shantung. Educated in America. Acting Junior Secretary of the Board of Foreign Affairs, September, 1908. Acting Senior Secretary of the same Board, January, 1909. Junior Secretary (January, 1909) and Senior Secretary (May, 1909) of the Board of Foreign Affairs. Acting Junior Councillor (July, 1909) and Acting Senior Councillor (August, 1909). Accompanied Prince Tsai Hsun's Naval Mission to Japan and America, August, 1910, and also Prince Tsai Chen's Coronation Mission in 1911. Rejoined Board of Foreign Affairs upon his return. Appointed Vice-Minister of Finance in Yuan Shih-kai's November Cabinet on the refusal of that post by Dr. Chen Chin-tao. Acting Minister of Finance, March, 1912. After complications arose in connection with the Belgian Loan he was appointed Tutuh of Shantung (March, 1912). Acting Governor, Bank of China, August 21, 1913. Minister of Communications, September 11, 1913. Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, 1915. Member of the Senate, 1916. Was one of the chief promoters of Yuan Shih Kai's Monarchical movement. Interested in the promotion of the Peiyang Ironworks. Minister of Finance in the Chin Cabinet of 1920. Resigned, May, 1921. Adviser to the Chinese Delegation to Washington Conference, Nov., 1921. Minister of Education and Acting Premier, Apr. 8, 1922, during Liang Shih Yi's absence. Resigned June 12, 1922. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHOU WAN-PENG, (周萬鵬)** —Kiangsu. Born 1864. Studied in the Rensellear Polytechnical Institute, (U.S.A.) Chief, Chinese Government Telegraph Administration. Department head, Ministry of Communications Director, Telegraph Office, Shanghai and Superintendent of Telegraphs of Kiangsu 1917-1921. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHOU YI-CHUN, (Tsur Ye-Tsung) (周詒春)** —Anhui. Graduate of St. John's University and Yale University, U.S.A. Dean of Tsinghua College. Became President 1913: resigned, 1918. Member of Senate till 1920. Manager of the Chung Foo Union Bank, Peking. Accompanied Chu Chi-chien to France, July, 1921, (see Chu Chi-chien). — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHOW S. T** see Chou Shan-tung. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHOW, Z. Y. C** see Chou Ch'un. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHOW, ZIN-YEN** see Chou Ch'un. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHU CHAO** —Director of the Gold Mining Office in Heilungkiang, 1915. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHU CHAO-HSIN (朱兆莘)** —Kwangtung. Born 1879. Graduate of the Government University in Peking and Columbia University, U.S.A. Secretary to the President, 1913. Teacher to the Peking University. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Tachungtang. Author of The Chinese Loans, etc. Consul-General at San Francisco. Councillor, Chinese Legation at London. Charge d'Affaires during Dr. Wellington Koo's absence. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHU CH'I-CHIEN, (朱啓鈐)** —Kweichow. Was a magistrate in Kiangsi and a Taotai in Chihli. Superintendent of Peking Inner Police in 1904. Subsequently a Director of the Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Minister of Communications (July, 1912) in Lu Cheng-hsiang's and Chao Ping-chun's Cabinet. Appointed Acting Premier, July 27, 1913, but did not accept post. Minister of Interior, September 11, 1913. Promoted Yuan Shih Kai's Monarchical project. Appointed Chief Delegate for the North at the Peace Conference with the South, February, 1919. Sent to France to represent ex-President Hsü Shih-chang at conferring of Hon. Doctor's degree to ex-President Hsü, July, 1921. Visited Japan on way home. Arrived at Tientsin, Aug. 10, 1921. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHU CHIA-PAO, (朱家寶)** —Yunnan. Chinshih. Was a Chihli magistrate, Was once Commissioner of Finance for Kiangsu. Governor of Anhui, 1909. Governor of Chihli, 1915-1917. Now in retirement. — p. 807 ([scan leaf 880](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n880/mode/1up))

**CHU HE-HSIANG, (Louis Ngaosiang Tchou), (朱鶴翔)** —Paoshan, Ku. Born 1888. Educated in the Futan and Chentan Colleges in Shanghai and in Belgium. B.A., LL.B., Doctor in Politics and Diplomacy (Belgian). Junior Councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Dec. 31, 1915. Secretary, Jan. 25, 1916. Member of Investigation Commission, Cabinet Office, March 11, 1916. Professor of Law, Government University, Sept. 1916. Acting Vice-Minister, May, 1916, and April 1917. Director of Chihui Affairs, Jan. 15, 1918. Author of Le Regime des Capitulations et la regime constitutionnelle en Chine (1915) and China and Japan (1915), Works on Treaties of Peace between Germany and Austria and the Allies. Fourth class Wenhu and Paokuang Chiaho Decorations. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU HSIAO-WEI (朱孝威)** —Supervisor of the Chianghai Customs, Kwangtung, Jan., 1915. Transferred to Lungchow Customs, Kwangsi, Nov., 1915. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU HSIEN-WEN (朱獻文)** —Chekiang. Councillor, Law Department of Cabinet, November 27, 1912. Judge, Supreme Court. Chief Justice, Kiangsi Higher Court, Mar., 1914. Chief Justice, Peking Higher Court. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU HSING-YUAN, (祝惺元)** —Peking. Born 1880. Educated in the Peking Imperial University and in Japan. First Secretary, Chinese Legation in Washington. Secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, July, 1918. Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Chihli, 1920. Second class Tashou Chiaho and Second class Wenhu decorations. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU KIN-HOU, see Hsu Ting Chueh**  — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CH'U LIEN (區濂)** —Kwangtung. Acting Salt Commissioner, Lianghuang, August 8, 1913. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU PAO-SAN, (朱葆三)** —Chekiang. Born 1847. Was sometime manager of Shanghai Branch of the Bank of China. Chairman Chinese Chamber of Commerce, Shanghai, 1918. (alias Chu Pei-chen 朱佩珍). — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU PEI-CHEN, see Chu Pao-san**  — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU PENG-SHOU, (朱彭壽)** —Chekiang. Supervisor of the Changsha Customs, and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Hunan, June, 1914. Supervisor, Ichang Customs, and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Hupeh. Oct., 1915—June, 1916. Secretary to the Cabinet, Dec. 1917. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU SHEN, (朱深)** —Chihli. Born 1881. Graduated from a Japanese University with a degree of LL.B. Upon his return, Chu practised law. Appointed Acting Chief Procurator, Peking, Aug. 1912. Chief of High Procuratorate of Peking, November, 1913. Was appointed Chief of Chief Procuratorate November. 1915. Became Minister of Justice, September, 1917. Upon the resignation of Chien Nun Hsun, Chu was concurrently appointed Minister of Interior. This latter office he held until 1920. A member of the defunct Anfu Club and is now in exile. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU TING-CHI, (朱庭祺)** —Kiangsu. Born 1886 Graduate of Harvard University. Councillor of the Ministry of Industry and Commerce. English Secretary, Shanghai-Nanking Railway. Transferred to Commission on Shantung Rehabilitation, May, 1922. Co-Director General Kiaochao-Tsinanfu Railway, Dec. 1922. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHI WEN-FU, (朱文繡)** —Appointed to succeed Dr. Shih Pin as second Secretary to the Chinese Legation at Washington, Aug. 1921. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU YING-KUANG, (屈映光)** —Chekiang. Born 1885. Graduated from the Chih Cheng Institute (a High School). Became its teacher upon graduation. Later he became the Superintendent of Ing Shan Commercial School, Taichow. Was teacher of Military Survey School in Anhui. It was in this latter place that Gov. Chu came in contact with military people. Connected with the Fifth Army of Chekiang as its adviser in 1912. Promoted to Chief of Civil Affairs of Chekiang and still later was appointed Civil Governor of his own province. Granted by "Emperor Yuan" the rank of first class Count. When Chekiang declared independence of Peking as protest against Yuan's monarchical movement, Chu joined his people, although he was for a few days made Tutuh of Chekiang. Owing to opposition from the people, he resigned the post of Civil and Military Governor and went to Peking, where he promoted the organization of the Sino-American Association. For a time, was Civil Governor of Shantung in 1920. Fourth Order of Merit. First class Wenhu decoration. — p. 808 ([scan leaf 881](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n881/mode/1up))

**CHU YI TS'ANG, (M. Thomas Tchou), (朱肯滄)** —Szechuan. Born 1895. Studied in different parts of Europe, particularly in the University of Glasgow, Scotland, B.Sc. (in Mech. and Civil Eng.), B.Sc. (in Naval Architecture). Adviser to the State Arsenal, Chengtu, Sze., 1917. Professor of English and Physics, Chengtu Higher Normal School, 1918. Prof. of Mechanical Engineering, Changsha Polytechnic Institute, 1918-19. Prof. of English and Sciences in English Methodist College, Ningpo 1919, to date. President, Ningpo Y.M.C.A. since 1920. — p. 809 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n882/mode/1up))

**CHUAN LIANG, (權量)** —Wuchang, Hup. Born May 1873. Educated in Japan. Chujen. Director, Commercial Department, Imperial Peking University. Councillor, Department Head, Ministry of Communications. Acting Vice-Minister, May, 1916, and April 1917. Director of Chihui Railway till May 1921. Managing Director, Kirin-Changchun Railway. Author of Textbooks on Algebra, Banking, Japanese language, etc. Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho, Second class Tashou Chiaho, Wenhu decorations. — p. 809 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n882/mode/1up))

**CHUAN, S. H** see Ch'uan Shao Ch'ing. — p. 809 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n882/mode/1up))

**CH'UAN SHAO-CH'ING, (S. H. Chuan), (全紹清)** —Tunghsien, Chi. Born July 12, 1883. After graduation from the Peiyang Medical College, Tientsin, Ch'uan went to John Hopkins University and Harvard University for special study. M.D. Chief of Medical Staff, Chinese Army, President, Army Medical College Aug. 1914. to 1922. Appointed Vice-Minister of Education, June 17, 1922. Resigned shortly afterward. Appointed Director, Central Sanitary Department, Peking, Sept., 1922. Author of "The Most Extraordinary City of the World," "Chinese Patients and Their Prejudices," and a score of articles on medicine and travel. Second class Paokuang Chiaho, Wenhu, Chiaho decorations. Wearer of a Gold medal and other medals awarded by foreign states. — p. 809 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n882/mode/1up))

**CHUANG YUN-K'UAN, (莊蘊寬)** —Kiangsu. T'ai-P'ing-Ssu-Shun Tao, Kwangsi, spring, 1908. Commissioner for the opening of Pukow, December 4, 1912. Tutuh of Kiangsu, 1913. President of the Board of Censors, 1915. Resigned, February, 1916. Chief, Auditing Bureau. — p. 809 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n882/mode/1up))

**CH'UN, PRINCE, (Tsai Li), (醇親王載灃)** —Manchu. Ex-Regent. Brother of the late Emperor Kwang Hsu. Succeeded to his father's title in January, 1891. Lieutenant-General of Plain White Banner, June, 1901. Special Envoy to Germany to apologize for the murder of the German Minister, July, 1901. Supernumerary Grand Councillor, June, 1907. Grand Councillor, February, 1908. Member of Commission of Constitutional Reform and Government Council, 1908. Appointed Prince Regent on the death of the Emperor Kwang Hsu, November 13, 1908. Took the oath of observance of the Constitution on November 26, 1911. Resigned from the Regency on December 6, 1911. — p. 809 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n882/mode/1up))

**CHUNG SHIH-MING (鍾世銘)** —Tientsin. Studied in Peiyang University and in America. English Teacher to the Technical College of Chihli, 1911. Secretary of the Salt Administration and became its Councillor. Appointed Acting Vice-Minister of Finance and concurrently Director- General of the Salt Administration, Dec. 9, 1921. In charge of the Ministry, Mar. 11, 1922. Now released from all his posts. — p. 809 ([scan leaf 882](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n882/mode/1up))

**CHUNG WEN-HU (鍾文虎)** —Chekiang. Taoyin of the West Szechuan Circuit, 1915. Superintendent of the Chunchuanyuan Bank of Szechuan, 1915. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**CHUNG WEN-YUEH, (鍾文耀)** —Kwangtung. Born 1860. B.A., Yale University Class of 1883. Managing-Director, Shanghai-Nanking Railway. Director—Shanghai Mint. Second class Chiaho and Fourth class Wenhu decorations. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**DJANG YUAN-SHAN** see Chang Yuan Shan. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**DO, BIN** see T'ao Pin. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**EN HUA, (恩華)** —Kiangsu. Born 1872. Educated in Japan. Chujen, Chinshih. Department Head, Ministry of Education, 1907-10. Com-cillor, 1910-11. Member of Tzu Cheng Yuan, 1911. Secretary, Cabinet Office, 1912-13. Member of Parliament, May-Oct. 1913. Was sent on several missions to Mongolia. Major General, Dec. 1919, Vice-Director, Bureau of Mongolia and Tibet, Feb., 1920 to Sept. 2, 1920. Reappointed, June 17, 1922. Author of a map of Tang-nu-wu-liang-hai. Second class Tashou Chiaho, Third class Paokuang Chiaho, Wen hu decorations. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FAN TSENG-HSIANG (樊增祥)** —Hupeh. Judicial Commissioner, Shensi, August, 1901. Employed in Government Council, July, 1901. Judicial Commissioner, Chekiang, January, 1903, and Shensi, October, 1903. Financial Commissioner, Shensi, December, 1904. Cashiered, January, 1907. Rank restored, January, 1908. Financial Commissioner, Nanking, spring, 1908. Civil Administrator, Hupeh, April, 1912. Member of Tsan Chen Yuan, 1914. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FAN YUAN-LIEN, (范源濂)** —Hunan. Educated in Japan. Studied in various Japanese Colleges law and education. Has a reputation as a Chinese scholar. Vice-Minister of Education on the formation of the first Republican Cabinet, and subsequently Minister of Education in Lu Cheng-hsiang's Cabinet. Resigned, April, 1913. Minister of Education in Tuan Chi-jui's Cabinet, July, 1917. Again in Chin Yun-peng's Cabinet Aug. 1920. Visited America on two occasions. Now President, Teachers' College, Peking. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FANG SHENG-CHENG, (方聖徵)** —Fukien. Born 1875. A Graduate of the Normal School of Fukien, and was a Vice-Chujen of the late Dynasty. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. In 33rd year of Kwang Hsu was a teacher at the Yun-siu school; in 34th year, an Educational Supervisor of Yun-siu, and in the 1st year of Republic, Chairman of the Anti-Opium Society in Yun-siu. Chinputang. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FANG SHU, (方樞)** —Anhui. Japanese-educated. Councillor, Department of Legislation, Cabinet, August 31, 1912. Deputy for Organization of National Assembly, October 9, 1912. Chief Secretary to the Cabinet, 1917. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FEI CHI-HE (費起鶴)** —Chihli. Born 1878. Graduate, Oberlin College (U.S.A.), B.A. and Yale University M.A. Was teacher in Tientsin and Paoting. Member of Ministry of Education, 1912. Secretary, Peking Y.M.C.A., and Principal of Peking Y.M.C.A., School. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FEI, H. J** see Fei Hsing-jen. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FEI HSING-JEN, (H. J. Fei), (費奧仁)** —Chihli. Born 1886. Studied in Oberlin College and Yale University, U.S.A., M.A. Secretary, Peking Y.M.C.A., 1914-15. Teacher, Tsing Hua College, Peking, 1915-16. English Secretary, Peking Hankow Railway, 1917-19. Manager, Jen Li Rug Co., Peking, 1917. Asst. Manager, Meier, Chen & Co., Peking, 1922. — p. 810 ([scan leaf 883](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n883/mode/1up))

**FENG CHEN-CHI, (馮振驥)** —Hupeh. Born 1887. Graduated in law from the Imperial University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan, Chinputang. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FENG LAN-HSIU, (馮蘭秀)** —Shantung. Born Oct. 30, 1870. Graduated from the Kirin Law College. Chairman of Kirin Chamber of Commerce since 1913. Fifth Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FENG SZU-CHIH, (馮司直)** —Shanghai. Born 1884. Educated in Japan. Chairman, Shansi Provincial Educational Association. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FENG WEN-YU, (馮文煜)** —Chihli. Born 1880. Graduated from the Telegraph School of Peiyang. Chief Telegraph Department, Civil Governor's Office (Chihli). Director, Chinese Telephone Administration, Tientsin Exchange, since July, 1920. Second class Chiaho and Fourth class Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FENG YU-HSIANG (馮玉祥)** —Anhui. Commander of the 11th Division Appointed on Aug. 25, 1921, Acting Tuchun of Shensi upon sudden and mysterious death of Yen Hsiang-wen who had been appointed only two months previously when Chen Shu-fan fled. Transferred to Honan, May 10, 1922. Was Appointed Inspecter of Army and transferred his troops to Peking, Oct., 1922. His troops played a decisive part in the Chihli-Fengtien war. Known as the Christian General. Second Order of Merit. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FENG YUAN-HSUN, (馮元勳)** —Szechuan. Born June 7, 1881. Graduated in Mining from Belgian Colleges. Chief, Department of Education, Civil Administration of Szechuan since Feb. 23, 1902. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FENG YUAN-TING (馮元鼎)** —Secretary to Tang Shao-yi when the latter was Customs Taotai at Tientsin. For some time Joint Director of the Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Vice-Minister of Communications. In charge of construction of Hukuang Railways, June 18, 1913. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FONG, F** Sec., see Kuang Fu Shao. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FOO, PING-SHEUNG** see Fu Ping-ch'ang. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FU CHIANG, (傅彊)** —Chekiang. Born 1876. A Graduate of a Law School in Japan. Special Delegate for Foreign Affairs at Kirin, April 13. Chinputang. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FU LIANG-TSO, (傅良佐)** —Hunan. Assistant Secretary to the President, April, 1912. Acting Commissioner of Occupation, Chi Yu, June 28, 1913. Vice-Minister of War. During the Anfu downfall, 1920, was imprisoned by Civil Governor Tsao of Chihli while on a secret mission sent by Tuan Chi-jui to Tientsin. Released, May 1921. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FU PING-CH'ANG, (Ping-Sheung Foo), (傅秉常)** —Kwangtung. Born 1895. Graduate of Hongkong University. B.Sc. Appointed Superintendent of Lianghai and Peihai Customs and concurrently Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Peihai since Jan. 1921. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FU TING-SHENG (符鼎升)** —Kiangsi. Born 1878. Graduate of Yi Hsueh Kuan, Peking, and of High Normal School at Tokio. Was President of the Chinese Republican Association (branch office) at Kiangsi, 1911. Chief of the Education Department, Kiangsi, 1912. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan, 1913. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Commissioner of Education for Kwangtung, Sept., 1917. Transferred to Kiangsu, Nov., 1917. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FU TSENG-HSIANG, (傅增湘)** —Szechuan. Hanlin. Secretary to the Viceroy of Chihli, when the post was held by Yuan Shih-kai, 1903. Director of several girls' schools in Chihli. Secretary to the Commander-in-Chief of Kiang-pei, 1950. Appointed Commissioner of Education of Chihli, 1908. Was a Censor 1914-1916. Appointed Minister of Education, December, 1917 to May, 1919. Adviser to the President. A well-known Chinese scholar and a collector and critic of old Chinese books, articles of art; etc. Frist class Tashou Chiaho Decoration. — p. 811 ([scan leaf 884](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n884/mode/1up))

**FU YU-FEN, (傅嶽棻)** —Hupeh. Born 1878. Chujen. During the Ching Dynasty, Fu was connected with the yamen of the Governor of Shansi. Was once acting President of the Shansi Imperial University. Transferred to Peking, Fu became member of the Commission for Preparation of the Constitutional Government. Promoted to Chief of Administrative Department of the Board of Education. In the Republican Government, Fu was connected with the Cabinet in its Law Compilation Bureau. In 1920, Fu was appointed Vice-Minister of Education, acting for the Minister, until Fan Yuan Lien took over the office in the Autumn of the same year. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HAN-CHANG, (哈漢章)** —Hupeh. Assistant Director, Military Council, Board of War, 1907. A Director-in-Chief of the General Staff, September, 1909. Military Adviser to the President. April 9, 1912. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**TE-ERH** —Tu-lu-fan, Sinkiang. Born 1862. Self-educated. The 4th class hereditary Changchin of the late Ching Dynasty, and has the control of the Tien Tribe of Mohammedans. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Chinputang. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HAN KUO-CHUN, (韓國鈞)** —Kiangsu. Born 1858. Acting Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Fengtien, April, 1910. Substantive do., September 10th. Commissioner for the Interior, Kirin, December 11. Summoned to Peking, December 12, 1912. Chief Civil Administrator, Kiangsu, June, 9. 1913. Governor of Anhui, 1915. Governor of Hunan, 1915. Appointed Civil Governor of Shantung, April 5, 1922, but refused. Transferred to Kiangsu, June 15, 1922. Second class Tashou Chiaho Decoration. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HAN YU-CHEN, (韓玉辰)** —Hupeh. Born 1882. A Graduate of Hupeh Law School. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan and a lawyer. Was a Sectional Chief of the Department of Justice, Hupeh: an editor of Chung Hwa Min-kuo Kung Pao, etc., and a teacher of Law School, Hupeh. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HO CHIEH, (何杰)** —Kwangtung. Born 1888. Studied in Canton Christian College and Tongshan College. Graduated from Colorado School of Mines. E.M. and Lehigh University, M.S. Surveyor, in the Colorado State Geological Survey, 1921. Professor of Mining and Metallurgy, the National University of Peking, 1914-18. Assistant Engineer. Chow-Hsiang Railway, 1917. Dean of the Department of Geology, National University of Peking, 1918 to date. Concurrently Chief of the Engineering Department, 1920 to date. Member of American Inst. of Mining and Met. Engineers. Member of Association of Chinese and American Engineers. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HO FENG-LIN, (何豐林)** —Anhui. Born 1877. Graduated from the Pei Yang Military Academy. Lieutenant-General in the Army. Brigadier General of the 7th and 8th Army Brigades. Defence Commissioner of Ningtai (Chekiang). Now Military Commissioner of Shanghai. Second Order of Merit. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HO HOU-CH'I, (何厚琦)** —Shansi. Born 1866. Formerly Adviser to the Civil Governor of Fengtien. Tung-pien Taoyin and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs of Antung, Manchuria, March, 1920, and of Yingkow since April 28, 1821. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HO KUO-CHANG, (賀國昌)** —Kiangsi. Police Intendant (Acting), Kweichow, spring, 1908. In charge as Chief Civil Administrator, Kiangsi, June 8, 1913. — p. 812 ([scan leaf 885](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n885/mode/1up))

**HO P'EI-YUNG, (何佩瑢)** .–Hupeh. Graduated from Military School in China, he attended a military college in Japan, specializing in Infantry. Graduated from Japanese school in 1907, and returned to China in the summer of the same year. Detailed for service in the Ministry of Army. Later was transferred to the Second Division of the Pei Yang Army. In two years, he was promoted to be chief of the Second Division. Was given the rank of Major General, September, 1913. Appointed Civil Governor of Hupeh, 1917. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HO SHIH-KUO, (何士果)** .—Kwangtung. Born 1865. A Graduate of Kwangtung Tungwen College, and studied in Japan. Chinshih of the late Dynasty. Was teacher of several schools, Director of Tsing-hua College, Magistrate of Kirin Fu. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Author of The Outline of the Russo-Japanese War, etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HO TING-KUEI, (賀庭桂)** Anhui. Born 1880. A Graduate of the Bookkeeping school of Hupu. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was appointed to assist in the organization of the police in Peking. Established Hsusung College, and Director of the Industry College of Kweichow. Was appointed to establish the Court and Procuratorate, and model prison of Kweichow. Author of Chuminchung, and other poetical and literary works. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HO YU** .—Secretary to Cabinet, May 17, 1913. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HO YI-SHIN** .—Chekiang. Born 1877. Graduate of the Engineering Department of the Japanese Imperal University at Tokio. Investigator of the Chekiang Bureau of Investigation of Mines, 33rd year of Kwang Hsu, and in the 8th moon of the same year was appointed a member of the Commission of the examination of returned students. Proceeded to Japan to investigate into the system of Japanese Imperial Universities, 34th year of Kwang Hsu. Was Director to the Engineering Department of the Government University, Peking. Chief of the Department of Mines in the Ministry of Industry and Commerce. Was appointed Acting Director of Government University in Peking, December, 1912. Resigned, November, 1913. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HOA, T. T** , see Hua Nan-kuei. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HSIA SHOU-KANG, (夏壽康)** .—Hupeh, Chinshih. Commissioner of Interior, Hupeh, May 1912. Acting Chief Civil Administrator, Hupeh, October 30, 1912. Chief of Civil Service Bureau, September 25, 1913. Acting Chief Censor, July, 1914. Judge, High Court of Probation. Civil Governor of Hupeh 1920. Resigned March, 1921. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HSIA YI-TING, (夏詒霆)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1881. Began his diplomatic career as student-interpreter of Chinese Legation in Berlin. Has been Attache to the legation in Spain and Secretary to the legation at Paris. Was once Chargé d'Affaires of the Legation in Spain. Consul General in Yokohama, Japan. Councillor of the special diplomatic mission sent to Belgium and France. In 1914, Mr. Hsia was Secretary to the Cabinet and later of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Was Councillor to the same Ministry under Minister Lu Cheng-hsiang, in 1915. Chief Secretary to the Commission for Studying of Politics. Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs, officiating as Minister before the assumption of the office by Dr. Wu Ting-fang. Adviser to the President and to the Cabinet. Member of War Commission. Minister to Peru and Brazil. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HSIA YUAN-LI, (夏元瑮)** .—Chekiang. Born 1883. Studied in Nanyang College, Shanghai, Yale University (U.S.A.) Ph.B. Berlin University (Germany) (Studied under Einstein and Planck) Director of Science Department and Professor of Physics, Peking Government University. Author of several scientific works. Councillor to the Cabinet. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 813 ([scan leaf 886](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n886/mode/1up))

**HSIANG HSIANG** (項驤).—Chekiang. Born 1880. Graduate of the University of Columbia. U. S. A. Is the Councillor of the Ministry of Finance and a member of the Committee of the Bank of China. Appointed Vice-Minister of Finance, June 1922. Chinputang. Author of A Comparison of the Administration of Four Countries, The Politics, The Recovery of Sovereign Rights, The Taxes, The Change to a Gold Standard, and The Salvation of the Country by Discharging the National Debts. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIANG·HSIEN** (項銑).—Yunnan. Born 1887. Graduated from Military Academy of Yunnan. Police Commissioner for Yunnan and concurrently Superintendent of Police of Yunnanfu, since March, 1921. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIAO TU** (蕭度).—Honan. Born 1871. First studied in the Kwangtung Naval School, then studied Railroads in Japan. Chujen. Was a magistrate in Honan during the Manchu regime. Chief Procurator for Hunan since March 1919, under the Military Government. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIAO YAO-NAN** (蕭耀南).—Hupeh. Commanding-General of the 25th Army Division. Appointed Tuchun of Hupeh on August 9, 1921, on the downfall of the "Super-Tuchun" Wang Chan-yuan. Second Order of Merit. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIEH, AUSTIN C. N** see Hsieh Ch'uan An. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIEH CH'UAN-AN** (Austin C. N. Hsieh) (謝傳安).—Hupeh. Born 1884. Educated in America. At one time, Superintendent of Customs, Soochow. Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Honan since May 21, 1920. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIEH CH'UAN-T'ING** (Sia Chwang Ting) (謝銓庭).—Fukien. Born 1875. Educated in Anglo-Chinese College, Foochow. Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Honan, since Dec. 9, 1920. Member of Executive Committee of Honan International Famine Relief Committee. Third class Paokuang Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIFH HUAN-WU** (謝桓武).—Honan. Magistrate in Shansi, Commissioner of Industry, Honan, 1913. Member of the Tsanchengyuan (Council of State). — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIEH I-YUAN** (謝翊元).—Kiangsu. Born 1886. Graduate of Peking Law College. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chinputang. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIEH SHU-LIN** (謝書林).—Fengtien. Born 1875. Graduate of Normal and other schools in Fengtien. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. In the 3rd year of Hsuan Tung, from the 2nd to the 5th months, he was made Chairman of the Society for the Prevention of Plague in Liucheng. Manager of Ming Hing Bank. Chinputang. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIEH YIN-CHANG** (謝陰昌).—Kiangsu. Born Jan. 31, 1877. Educated in Japan. Appointed Commissioner of Education for Fengtien, September 1917. Wrote several books on public and social education. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIN HAN** (辛漢).--Kiangsu. Born 1877. Graduate of a University in Japan. In the late Dynasty he was a clerk in the Minchengpu, and the Chief Procurator of Chekiang. After the Restoration, Chief of the Library of Nanking and Magistrate of Nanking district, and Director of the private Law School of Nanking. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Judge, Court of Justice, Liaoyang. Member, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. Chinputang. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIU CHI** .—Manchu. Deputy Lieutenant-General, Plain Yellow Banner, until September, 1911. Lieutenant-General, Manchu Bordered Blue Banner, June 20, 1913. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG CH'ENG-CHANG** (熊成章).—Szechuan. Born 1885. Graduate of Political Science Department of a Japanese University at Tokio. Was during Ching dynasty a member of Board of Interior and also judge at various courts in Shansi and Heilungkiang. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Secretary to ex-President Sun at Nanking. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Now engaged in educational work in his native province. — p. 814 ([scan leaf 887](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n887/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG HSI-LING** (熊希齡).—Hunan. Metropolitan Graduate of 1894. A prominent Hanlin scholar, who subsequently studied abroad in Japan and Europe. He was an intimate friend of the reformer Kang Yu-wei, and after the latter's downfall he was arrested, but promptly released. He held posts in the Board of Agriculture, Industry and Commerce of Kiangsu and Fengtien, was Acting Commissioner of Foreign Affairs in Hupeh in 1910, and at the time of the Wuchang outbreak was Salt Finance Supervision Commissioner at Mukden. He accompanied Tuan Fang on his foreign mission in 1905. On the outbreak of the Revolution he went south and joined the republicans, becoming President of the Republican Committee in Hunan. He was appointed Minister of Finance in the first Republican Cabinet, and was responsible for the conduct of the loan negotiations with the International Group from the time of his arrival in Peking until he resigned, in June, after Tang-Shao-yi gave up the Premiership. He continued to be employed by the Government in the capacity of Chief of a Commission for the negotiation of foreign loans after his resignation, and it was he who was mainly responsible for negotiating the Crisp Loan. Subsequently Lieut-General of Jehol until appointed Premier, July 31, 1913. Finance (Additional), September 11, 1913. Director-General of the National Oil Administration, 1915. Director of Flood Relief, Chihli, and Director-General of the Grand Canal Improvement Board, 1917. Organized and is still the Director of a modern Orphanage in the Western Hills, near Peking. Called to Hunan to direct self-government movement in that province, May 1921. Returned Peking, July 1921. Second Order of Merit. — p. 815 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n888/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG KE-WU** (熊克武).—Szechuan. Born 1882. Graduated from the Tung Ping Military College in Japan. Commanded the 5th Szechuan Army. Has been Defence Commissioner of Chungking and of Szechuan Frontier. Being military leader of Szechuan, he joined the Canton Military Government in 1919. Toward end of 1920, was appointed Civil Governor of Szechuan by the Peking Government but did not accept office. Was succeeded by Liu Hsiang. Second Order of Merit. — p. 815 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n888/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG PING-CHI** (熊炳琦).—Was appointed Civil Governor of Shantung. Concurrently, Director-General of Administration of Kiaochao Port, 1922. Was also commissioned to co-operate with Dr. C. T. Wang in the Settlement of the Shantung Question. Second Class Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 815 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n888/mode/1up))

**HSU CHEN-PENG** (徐振鵬).—Kwangtung. Brigadier-General, Vice-Minister of Navy, since May 4, 1918. Second class Wenhu Decoration, Second class Pao Kuang Ta Shou Chiaho Decoration. — p. 815 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n888/mode/1up))

**HSU CHIEN (George Chien Hsu)** (徐謙).—Anhui. Born 1872. Chinshih and Hanlin of the late Ching Dynasty. Graduated from the Chin Shih Kuan (Law College for Hanlins) Appointed by Tai Hung Tsi, Chief Justice of Peking Local Court with instructions to reorganize that Court along modern lines. In 1907, became Attorney-General of the Peking Higher Court. Represented China at the International Prison Conference, Washington, 1910. Resigned from office at the outbreak of the Revolution, 1911. Was made Vice-President of the Supreme Court after peace pact in Shanghai. Became Vice-Minister of Justice, 1912. Resigned after half year's service, and joined Dr. Sun Yat-sen. Practised law in Shanghai, 1913-1915. Joined the Christian Church, 1916. Again was Vice-Minister of Justice in the first Tuan Chi-jui's Cabinet under President Li Yuan-hung. Resigned at the dissolution of the Parliament. Joined in 1917 Dr. Sun in Canton where the Military Government was established. There in 1918 he became Minister of Justice and concurrently Dr. Sun's representative on the Administrative Council. Proceeded in 1919 to Paris as representative of the Canton Military Government and also unofficially of Chinese Christian Churches, serving as adviser to the Chinese Peace delegation. Upon return from Paris, joined the Tientsin and Peking Yi Shih Pao ("Social Welfare") as their Editor-in-Chief, while he retained nominally his post in the Canton Government as the President of Supreme Court. Resigned from Yi Shih Pao, May 1920, and was in active service in Canton in that capacity. Came to Shanghai with Dr. Sun Yat-sen in Autumn, 1922. Was asked to join Wang Chung-hui's Cabinet, in 1922 but never assumed office. — p. 815 ([scan leaf 888](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n888/mode/1up))

**Hsu Chih-ch'en, (徐之琛)** —Yunnan. Born Aug. 1884. Studied in the Yunnan Provincial College and in a French School in Annam. Special Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Yunnan since Aug. 3, 1918. Supervisor of the Mengtze Customs. Third class Chiaho, Wenhu (Canton Military Government) and a third class medal of award from the French Government. — p. 816 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Hsu Chih-ch'i, (徐之槩)** —Chekiang. Born 1869. Chujen. Appointed Chining Taoyin, Shansi, Aug. 1916. Author of Muling Chenshou and a work on salt administration. Non-partisan. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 816 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Hsu Chung-chih, (许崇智)** —Kwangtung. Born 1881. Graduated from the Military Officers' Training School of Japan. Has been training officer of the Tenth Foochow Army. Brigadier General of the 20th. Army Brigade. Now General of the 14th Army Division. — p. 816 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Hsu En-yuan, (徐恩元)** —Born 1885. Chekiang. Hsuitsai. Joined the Nanyang College, Shanghai, in 1897. After graduation, he went to England in 1905. Entered the University of London, 1908. After completing the course of study in Political Economy in the School of Economics and Political Science of that University, he took up research work on currency and banking. He won the Jevons Memorial Research Scholarship. Member of the Royal Geographical Society, of the Royal Society of Arts, etc. After leaving the University, he worked in the Union of London and Smith's Bank for nearly two years, from the head office down to the country branches. For some time while in England, he was attached to Wang Ta-hsieh, Chinese Constitutional Commissioner to London, and studied the methods of English local government in matters of finance, education, politics, sanitation, prisons, poor laws, etc. Government Delegate to the Currency Conference, London, 1911. Fellow of University College, London. Upon returning to China, he was at various times appointed: Chief, Department of Public Loans of the Ministry of Finance; Director of the Bureau of Audit; Special Commissioner of Currency Reform; Director of the Bureau of Public Credit; Deputy Director-General of the Bureau of Currency; Deputy Auditor-General of the Board of Audit, May 1914 to July 1917. Was on several occasions appointed as Acting Director-General of Audit. Hsu was appointed concurrently as Governor of the Bank of China, in June 1916, when a moratorium was in effect. While in the Bank, he arranged to resume specie payment and succeeded in doing so for its branches in Shantung, Shensi, Kwangtung, Tientsin and Kalgan. Councillor of the Cabinet. In 1919, he founded the Chinese American Bank of which he is Vice-President. First class Ta Shou Paokwang Chia Ho, First class Wen Hu, First class Ta-shou Chia Ho decorations and the French Legion d'Honneur. — p. 816 ([scan leaf 889](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n889/mode/1up))

**Hsu Fo-su** (徐佛蘇).—Hunan. Graduated from a normal school in Japan. Councillor of the Cabinet. Appointed Director General, Currency Bureau, June 1, 1922. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu, George Chien** see Hsu Chien. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Hsin-lu** (Singloh Hsu), (徐新六).—Hangchow, Chekiang. Born 1890. Educated in the Nanyang College, Shanghai; University of Birmingham; University of Manchester; Ecole des Sciences Politiques. B.Sc. B. Com. Councillor, Ministry of Finance, 1915. Member of Bank of China, 1916-17. Accountant of Hanyang Iron Works, 1917. Secretary, 1917-20. Sub-Manager, Bank of China, Peking Branch, 1918. Chief Secretary of National Commercial Bank and Director of Sintoon Overseas Trading Co., Ltd., 1922. Third class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Lan-chou** (許蘭洲).—Chihli. Born 1877. Lieutenant-General and later Brigadier General in the Fengtien Army, stationed in Shensi. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Ming-shih** (許名世).—Shantung. Born 1876. Graduated from the Provincial Normal School of Shantung. Chairman, Shantung Provincial Educational Association since July, 1917. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Nai-lin** (徐顯霖).—Kirin. Member of Tsanchengyuan. Member of Tsan Yi Yuan. Appointed Civil Governor for Kirin, Dec. 4, 1919. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Pao-heng** (許寶衡).—Chekiang. Chujen. Received various minor appointments among the Boards during Ching dynasty, Secretary to Cabinet, May, 1912. Chief of Department of Civil Service, October 18, 1912. Additional Temporary Acting Chief of Rewards Department, July 29, 1913. Confirmed in latter post, September 25, 1913. Acting Vice-Minister of Interior, 1914. Re-Appointed Chief, Rewards department, June 17, 1922. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Shan-ching** .—Consul-General in Panama. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Sheng-chin** (徐聲金).—Hupeh. Born March 1, 1874. Graduated in law from a Japanese College. Presiding Judge, Kansu Higher Court of Justice from Dec. 1918. Chief of Kirin Higher Court, Oct. 16, 1921. Second class Tashou and Paokwang Chiaho decorations. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**Hsu Shih-ch'ang** (徐世昌).—Honan. Probationary Grand Councillor, June, 1905. Minister of Government Council, June, 1905. President, Board of Police, October, 1905. Grand Councillor, February, 1906. Removed from Grand Council, November, 1906. Special Mission to Manchuria, December, 1906. President of Board of Interior, December, 1906. Viceroy of Manchuria, April, 1907. President of Board of Communications, February, 1909. Director-General, Tientsin-Pukow Railway, July 1909. Grand Secretary, February, 1910. Grand Councillor, August, 1910. Appointed Vice-Premier in Prince Ching's Cabinet, in May, 1911. Removed from that post, and appointed Vice-President of the Privy Council on November 1, 1911. Chief of General Staff, November, 1911. High Commissioner for Training Imperial Guard, and Grand Guardian to the Emperor, December, 1911. Relieved of post on General Staff, February, 1912. On the resignation of the Prince Regent was appointed, with Shih Hsu, Grand Guardian of the Emperor. A "Sworn Brother" of President Yuan Shih-kai. Secretary of State, 1915. With Chao Erh-hsün, Li Ching-hsi, and Chang Chien received the title of "The Four Friends of Sungshan" (i.e. of Yuan Shih-kai). On the failure of Yuan Shih-kai's attempt to establish a monarchy Hsu Shih-ch'ang resigned his Secretaryship and retired to Honan. Returned to Peking, November, 1916, to mediate between the President, Li Yuan-hung, and the Premier, Tuan Chi-jui. During the unsettled period, 1917-18, he remained detached from Peking politics, but without losing his influence over the contending factions. On September 4, 1918, elected President of the Republic of China, at a joint meeting of the Senate and House of Representatives of the so-called "Tuchuns' Parliament," by 425 out of 436 votes. Received Hon. Dr.'s degree from University of Paris. Sent Chu Chi-chien to represent him, June 1921. Vacated Presidency, June 1, 1922, and left for Tientsin on the following day. — p. 817 ([scan leaf 890](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n890/mode/1up))

**HSU SHIH-CHANG, (徐世章)** —Honan. Born 1888. Educated in the Peking Language School and in Belgium. B.Sc. in Commerce. Formerly Director Pukow-Hsinyangchow Railway, Managing Director, Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Director-General, National Railway System. Vice-Minister of Communications, from Aug. 1920. Appointed Director-General of the Currency Bureau, Dec. 27, 1921. Co-Director, Government Famine Relief Bureau. Retired, May 1922. Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho, Tashou Chiaho, and Wenhu decorations. Brother of President Hsu Shih-ch'ang. — p. 818 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n891/mode/1up))

**HSU SHIH-HSIANG, (S. S. Shü) (徐世襄)** —Tientsin. Cousin of ex-President Hsü Shih-chang. Studied in England and Germany. Attaché to the Commission sent abroad for investigation of Politics in the Ching dynasty. Secretary to President. Member of the Ministry of Finance. Superintendent of Customs, Kalgan and Tunghai (Chefoo). Second class Wenhu decoration. — p. 818 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n891/mode/1up))

**HSU SHIH-YING, (許世英)** —Anhui. Born 1872. President of High Court of Justice at Mukden, September, 1908. Chief Justice of the High Court at Peking, May, 1912. Minister of Justice, July, 1912. Sent to Great Britain, Germany, France, Russia, Italy Austria, Belgium. Holland and other countries to investigate the judicial system. Was also sent to the United States of America to attend the Eighth Conference on Prison Reforms. Has been in the service of police and judiciary for fifteen years. Resigned, September 4, 1913. Chief Civil Administrator Fengtien, October 13, 1913. Governor of Fukien, 1915. Minister of Communications, 1916; resigned, May, 1917. Appointed Civil Governor of Anhui Sept. 29, 1921. Appointed Minister of Justice, Dec. 1922 but did not take up appointment. First class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 818 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n891/mode/1up))

**HSU SHOU-SHANG, (許壽裳)** —Chekiang. Born 1883. Educated in Japan, graduating from the Tokio Higher Normal School. Was member of the Ministry of Education and teacher in the Imperial Peking University, Higher Normal School in Peking, Dean of Faculty, Chekiang Provincial Normal School. Appointed Commissioner of Education for Kiangsi since November, 1917. Author of various lecture notes on Education, Geography, etc. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration — p. 818 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Hsu, Shöwin Wetzen** see Hsü Wei Chen. — p. 818 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n891/mode/1up))

**HSU SHU-TSENG, (徐樹錚)** —Kiangsu. Was private Secretary to ex-Marshal Tuan Chi-jui. Sent to Japan to Study Military Science by Tuan. Secretary-in-Chief of the Cabinet; resigned, November, 1916. Played a prominent part in Peking in 1917-18. Sent on a special mission to Japan, October, 1918. During the armed struggle between the Chihli military leaders and Anfu Club, Hsu, who was commanding General of the North-West Frontier Army, was in Chief Command. After the Anfu army was defeated, Hsu fled for refuge in the Japanese Legation which notified the Chinese Government on Nov. 16, 1920, that he mysteriously escaped. He has remained at large ever since. Generally referred to as "Little" Hsu. In Oct. 1922 was implicated in the revolt against the Fukien Tuchun, and another Mandate was issued ordering his arrest. — p. 818 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n891/mode/1up))

**Hsu, Singloh** (see Hsü Hsin Lu.) — p. 818 ([scan leaf 891](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n891/mode/1up))

**HSU TING-CHUEH** (Chu Kin-hou). (徐廷爵即徐建緞).—Kwangtung. Born 1877. Prepared in Shanghai. Entered the Pei Yang University in Tientsin before going to America to study Textile Industry. Upon his return to China, Hsu was appointed Secretary to the Commissioner of Industries for Fengtien. Later, was made Director of Bureau of Mines in Fengtien. Was Managing-Director of the Peking-Mukden Railway up to 1920. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HSU WEI-CHEN** (Shöwin Wetzen Hsü), (徐維震).—Chekiang. Born 1881. Educated in the Nanyang College, Shanghai, Law School, University of Chicago, U.S.A., Law School, The Indiana University, U.S.A., LL.B. Law Compiler, Board of Communications, Judicial Officer of the Second class. Board of Navy; Councillor, Board of Education and member of Law Bureau in the late Ching Dynasty. Member, Ministry of Finance, 1912. Justice, Supreme Court, August 1912. Legal Adviser to the Garrison Commissioner of Shanghai, 1913. Justice, Higher Special Court, Nov. 1917. Member, Court of Equity, July, 1918. Chief Justice. Higher Court of Justice for Shansi since May 1920. Wrote and translated several books on naval, and legal subjects. Second class Chiaho, Wenhu and Third class Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HSU YUAN** (徐沅).—Kiangsu. Customs Taotai, Tientsin, April, 1912. Supervisor of Customs, Tientsin, December 1, 1912. Special Delegate of Foreign Affairs, Tientsin, March, 1913, and Honan, June 12, 1913. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU, C. P** see Hu Chen-ping. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU CHAO-TSUNG** (胡朝宗).—Hupeh. Head of Department for Foreign Affairs, Hupeh, September 15, 1912. Title changed to Foreign Affairs Delegate, January 16, 1913. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU CHEN-PING** (C. P. Hu) (胡振平).—Kiangsu. Graduate of Nanyang College, Shanghai, and London University. Charge d'Affaires in Mexico, 1914-18. Associate Director, Treaty Department, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU CHIA-CHI** (胡家祺).—Tientsin. Born 1871. Graduated from a Japanese university. Was connected with educational work in Chihli and Shantung and member of Ministry of Education before becoming Commissioner of Education for Kiangsu, March 1919. Resigned, June 22, 1922. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU CHING-YI** (胡景伊).—Szechuan. Born 1879. Graduated from a Military College in Japan. Officiating Tutuh of Szechuan, July, 1912. Substantive, June, 1913. Member of the Tsanchengyuan, 1915. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU HAN-MIN** (胡漢民 alias Hu Yen Hung 胡衍鴻).—Kwangtung. Studied in Japan, where he met Dr. Sun Yat-sen, of whom he became a warm admirer. Assisted the revolutionary cause as editor of a newspaper at Hongkong. After the resignation of the Provisional Tutuh of Kwangtung he was appointed to the post, but soon afterwards relinquished it to accompany Dr. Sun to Nanking in the capacity of secretary. On the resignation of Dr. Sun, Hu accompanied him to Canton, where the Acting Tutuh Chen Chiung-ming promptly resigned in his favour. Hu was there upon re-elected Tutuh by the Provincial Assembly. Is a member of the Kuomintang. Commissioner of Pacification of Tibet, June 14, 1913. Deprived of all rank during the rebellion of 1912. Joined the Canton Military Government since its establishment and received the title of Councillor. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU HUI** (胡蕙).—Commissioner of Foreign Affairs, Kiangsi, April 15, 1913. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**HU O-KUNG** (胡鄂公).—Hupeh. Born. 1885. Educated in the Chiang Han College, Hupeh; High Agriculture College, Peiyang; and High Agriculture College, Kiangsi. The Commanding Officer of both water and land forces, Hupeh during the Revolution. Chief of the General Department of Tutuh's office. Vice-Chief of the General Staff, Han-Yang, and the Chief of the General Staff in the North, 1911. Director for the Bureau for devising means of livelihood for the Manchus at Ching-nan. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan; Director, Peking Law College; adviser to the President's Office. Secretary to the Tuchun of Szechuan. A Taoyin in Szechuan, Commissioner of Administration for Hupeh, April 1921. Kunghotang. Author of The New Heaven and the New Earth, Literary Works of Ao-kung, etc. — p. 819 ([scan leaf 892](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n892/mode/1up))

**Hu Shang-i, (胡商彝)** :—Yunnan. Born 1873. Chujen, Chinshih. Studied in the Chin Cheng Seminary, Yunnan and in Japan. Magistrate in Chihli. Administrative Commissioner for Shantung, 1915. Taoyin in Charhar, August 1916. Author of Shih-ku-wen-chi. Non-partisan. Third class Wenhu and Chiaho, Second class Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hu Shih, (Hu Suh), (胡適)** .—Anhui. Born Dec. 17, 1891. B.A. Cornell University (U. S. A); Ph.D. Columbia University. Professor of Philosophy and of English, Government University of Peking since 1917. Dean, 1922. Author. Outline of Chinese Philosophy, Vol. I, (1919) and of Shang Shi Hsi (1920), Essays, etc. Organized and edited a weekly review called "Nu li," 1922. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hu, Suh** see Hu Shih. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hu Sze-yi, (胡思義)** .—Kiangsi. Magistrate in Yunnan. Salt Commissioner for Chêkiang, 1915. Supervisor of the Hwai An Customs (North Kiangsu), April 1917. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hu Wei-teh, (胡維德)** .—Chekiang. Minister to Russia, 1904. Junior Councillor, Board of Foreign Affairs, September, 1907. Minister to Japan, March, 1908. Star of 2nd Class, 1st Division, January, 1909, Junior Vice-President, Board of Foreign Affairs, May, 1910. Senior Vice-President, July, 1910. Adjoint Director-General of Revenue Council, July, 1910. Acting Minister of Finance, November, 1911. Appointed Acting Minister for Foreign Affairs in the Republican Government, pending the arrival from Europe of Lu Cheng-hsiang. Controller-General of the Revenue Council (Shuiwuchu), March, 1912. (He was nominated as Minister of Communications in July, but his name was rejected by the National Council.) Minister to France, Spain and Portugal, November 24, 1915. Minister to Japan, since 1920. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hu Yen-hung** see Hu Han-min. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hu Ying, (胡瑛)** .—Hunan. Commissioner for Land Reclamation, Sinkiang and Chinghai, May, 1912. Being involved in the rebellion of 1913, he fled from China. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hu Yuan-hui, (胡源滙)** .—Chihli. Born 1881. Graduate of a Japanese University. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chief of the Printing Bureau of the Ministry of Finance, Dec. 1917. Chinputang. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Hua Nan-kuei, (T. T. Hoa) (華南圭)** .—Kiangsu. Prepared in Soochow. Studied in Peking National University and in France. Engineer, Peking-Hankow Railway, 1911. Member of Ministry of Communications. Managing-Director, Pien-Lo (Kaifeng-Loyang) Railway, 1919. Re-joined Peking-Hankow Railway, 1920. Has served on various committees in the Ministry of Communications. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**Huang Chang, (黃璋)** .—Szechuan. Born 1878. A Graduate of a High School in Szechuan, and a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan and a member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. After returning from Japan he was selected as member of the Provisional Szechuan Assembly, and was a teacher of the High School and Law School in Szechuan. He established a Law School and a Finance School in Szechuan. Kunghotang. Author of The Latest Method of Book-keeping. — p. 820 ([scan leaf 893](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n893/mode/1up))

**HUANG CHIH-JUI, (黄芝瑞)** .—Hupeh. President, High Court of Justice, Kansu, Dec. 1915. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG CH'IANG, (黄强)** .—Kwangtung. Born 1887. Obtained preparatory education in France. Studied in an aeronautics school in England. Graduated from the Peiyang military training school. Superintendent of Customs, Chaochow, Kwangtung and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Swatow since August 24, 1920. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG CH'ING-LAN, (黄慶瀾)** .—Shanghai. Born 1874. Studied in the Normal School Department of Nanyang College, Shanghai. Chief, Higher Procurator's Court, Hupeh, Chief, Shanghai Local Court, 1911 to 1912. Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Wenchow, Chekiang, 1918. Chin Hua Taoyin, December, 1918. Kuei-chi Taoyin since December, 1919. Author: Comments on the New Criminal Code. Fourth, Third, Second class Chiaho; Second class Tashou Chiaho; Third class Pao Kuang Chiaho and Fourth class Wenhu Decorations. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG CHUN, (黄濬)** .—Fukien. Born 1891. Educated in the Imperial Language School, Peking. Chujen. Secretary, Ministry of Communications, 1913. Councillor, Ministry of Finance, 1914. Secretary since 1916. Secretary to the President's office, 1918. Was editor of a newspaper and member of Kunghotang and Chinputang. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG CHUNG-YING, Admiral** —Chief of Naval General Staff, April, 1912. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG FU, (黄郛)** .—Chekiang. Graduated from Army Cadet College, Japan. Served on Staff of General Chen Chi-mei, and the Kiangsu Tutuh. After a visit to Europe and America wrote two books on "Lessons of the European War and the Future of China," and "The World After the War." In 1921 was sent to study economic conditions in Europe and America, and joined the Chinese Delegation at Washington as High Adviser. On his return to China was appointed Director General of the Finance Commission. Nominated Minister for Foreign Affairs in February, 1923. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG HSI-CH'UAN, (黄錫銓)** .—Kwangtung. Born 1881. Studied in Japan. Was a Taotai in Kwangsi; Chinese Consul at New York, and then transferred to Peru. In 1912, was High Adviser to the Tutuh of Kwangtung, and Adviser to the Defence Commissioner there, and then became Speaker to the Kwangtung Assembly. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Author of Comment on the Commercial Treaty between Great Britain and China, Experiments on Agriculture and Forestry (six volumes) etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1912. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG HSIAO-CHIU, (黄霄九)** .—Kwangtung. Born 1884. Studied three years in Japan. Secretary of Tutuh's office, Kwangtung, 1912. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Opened two newspaper offices, which were shut down by officials for promulgating revolutionary ideas, but was afterwards engaged as editor of Jen Chuan Pao. Kungmintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG JUNG-LIANG, (Huang Yungliang, or Y. L. Huang), (黄榮良)** .—Anhui. Born 1875. A.B. Studied in the University of Nanking, 1890-96. Teacher, University of Nanking, 1896-97, Dean, 1897-99. Studied Liberal Arts in Baker University, 1900-03, at Columbia University 1903-5. Second Translator, Chinese Legation, London, 1906-7. Consul in New Zealand, 1908-10. Consul-General in Australia (Melbourne) 1910-13. Assistant Secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1914-16. Special Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Chihli, November 4, 1916. Appointed Minister to Austria 1920. Third class Wenhu. Second class Tashou Chiaho and Third class Paokwang Chiaho decorations. — p. 821 ([scan leaf 894](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n894/mode/1up))

**HUANG KAI-WEN, (黄開文)** —Kwangtung. Born 1865. A Graduate of Peiyang Telegraph College. Acting Intendant of Industry, Mukden, August, 1907. Degraded to Sub-Prefect, August, 1909. Supervisor of Customs, Hankow, December 1, 1912. Chief of the Hankow office of the Kin-Han Railway Company. Chief of Bureau of Rites in the President's Office. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG KUO-EN, (Wong Ko-un), (黄國恩)** —Kwangtung. Born 1887. Graduate, University of Wisconsin, U.S.A. LL.B. Law Professor, Futan University, Shanghai, 1911. Lawyer, one of the seven promoters who organized the first Bar Association of China, 1911. Secretary and Legal Adviser to Chou Tzu-chi, when the latter was Civil and Military Governor of Shantung, 1912-13. President of the Shantung Provincial College, 1912-13. Compiler, Ministry of Communications, 1913. Secretary, Ministry of Finance, 1914. Chief Auditor for Hupeh, Hunan, Anhui and Kiangsi Salt Districts, with Headquarters at Hankow, 1914. Member of Customs Revenue Administration, Peking, 1915. Private Secretary to Tuan Chih-kuei, ex-Minister of War, 1917-20. Military Judge, Metropolitan Garrison Guards Headquarters, 1917-20. Lecturer of Peking Government University, 1917-21. Assistant Secretary to Ministry of Communications, 1921. Member of Salt Adminstration, 1921. Assistant Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. 1915-22. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG P'EI-SUNG, (黄培松)** —Fukien. Military Optimus, 1880. Brigade General, Ch'iung-Yen-chen, Kwangtung, July, 1906. Defence Commissioner, Fukien, July 24, 1913. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG SHIH-LUNG, (黄士龍)** —Defence Commissioner, Kwangtung, August 10, 1913. Rewards Commissioner of the Forces, Kwangtung, August 8, 1913. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG, Y. L** see Huang Jung Liang. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG YEN-P'EI, (黄炎培)** —Kiangsu. Born Sept. 6, 1879. Graduated from the Nanyang University. D. Litt. Vice-Chairman, Kiangsu Provincial Educational Association, and Chairman, China Vocational Education Association. Member of Kiangsu Provincial Assembly Author of various works on Commerce, Education and Travel. Appointed Minister of Education, Dec. 25, 1921, but did not accept office. Reappointed, June 12, 1922, in Dr. W. W. Yen's Cabinet. Second Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG YU-CH'ANG** —Hunan. Born 1882., A Graduate of a Law College in Japan. Speaker of the Hunan Provincial Assembly. Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. After the Revolution, was appointed Director of a Law School of Hunan, and Controller of the fifth final electoral district. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG YUAN-WEI, (黄元蔚)** —Kwangtung. Born 1884. Educated in Japan. For sometime, Secretary to the Civil Governor of Kirin, later successively became member of Ministry of Finance. Bank of China, Bank of Communications. In the year 1920, member of Military Government in Canton as member of the Finance Department. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG YUN-P'ENG, (黄雲鵬)** —Szechuan. Born 1883. A Graduate of Tungwen College in Szechuan and of a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan, and of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. In the time of Revolution he organized the Minkuo Lien-ho Hui in Shanghai, and was editor of the Shanghai Ta Kung Ho Jih Pao. Was a member of the Szechuan Provisional Assembly, and afterwards Director of the Chunchang Bank of Chungking. Kunghotang. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUANG YUNGLIANG** see Huang Jung Liang. — p. 822 ([scan leaf 895](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n895/mode/1up))

**HUNG TZU-CHENG** (洪自成).—Military Governor of the Western Circuit of Kiangsi. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**I TSUNG-KU'EI** (易宗夔).—Hunan. Born 1875. Studied in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan, representing Mongolia, and a Member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. One of the representatives sent by the provinces to request the opening of a Parliament. Was a member of the late Tzu Cheng Yuan, and was prominent in the impeachment of Prince Ching. Was connected with the Revolution started by Tang Chai-tsang.
