On the failure of Kang Yu-wei's reform scheme he retired into seclusion and wrote several volumes on the subject of Reforms, until the time when schools were started everywhere, when he took up teaching. While in Japan he edited a revolutionary periodical. Kuomintang. Author of Huang Hai Lou Essays and Poetry. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**I WEN-KU'EI** (易文燴).—Kweichow. Born 1888. Educated in Japan. Chujen. Presiding judge, Yunnan Higher Court of Justice in 1917. Chief Procurator for Yunnan since Mrach 26, 1917. Procurator, Central Procurator's Court since Feb. 1920. Third class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**JAO HAN-HSIANG** (饒漢祥).—Hupeh. Studied in Japan, Secretary to Tutuh of Hupeh, Commissioner of Administration for Hupeh. Civil Governor of Hupeh. Secretary to President Li Yuan-hung. Appointed Chief of Bureau of Immigration, June 15, 1922. Third Order of Merit. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**JAO MENG-JEN. (Dr. Loe Meng Len)** (饒孟任).—Kiangsi. Born 1882. Chinshih. Studied law in England. LL.D. Secretary to the President. President, Peking National Law College. Member of House of Representatives. Co-Director, Bureau of Currency. Chinputang. Second class Tashou Chiaho, Chiaho, decorations. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**JEN HUNG-CHUN, (H. C. Zen)** (任鴻雋).—Szechuan. Born 1886. Studied in Japan, and Cornell University (U.S.A.), B.A. and Columbia University, A.M. Professor, Peking National University, 1920. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Education, 1921. Director of Szechuan Iron and Steel Works, 1922. President of the Science Society of China. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**JEN KO-CHENG** (任可澄).—Kweichow. Chujen. Started a Normal School and a law school. Chief, Auditing Bureau for Kweichow. Governor of Yunnan, 1915. Civil Governor of Kweichow, 1920. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**JUAN CHUNG-CHIH** (阮忠枢).—Anhui. Born May 31, 1865. Has been in civil service in Kirin since 1896. Was Taoyin and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs of the Kirin-Changchun district. Taoyin and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs of the I-Lan district, Kirin since April 5, 1914. Appointed Head of Bureau of Gold Purchase in Kirin, June 1, 1920. Third class, Second class, Chiaho; Second class Tashou Chiaho and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**KALACHING, Prince** see Kung Sang No Erh Pu. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**K'ANG P'EI-HANG** (康佩珩).—Shansi. Born 1876. Studied in Japan after having graduated from Shansi University. At the end of a year he returned with several friends to start a revolution, but failing in his attempt in Mongolia, he was placed under arrest by the General there. Subsequently returned to his native place to do educational work. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. He does not belong to any party. Author of The Principle of Evolution, etc. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**K'ANG YU-WEI** (康有為).—Kwangtung. A well-known reformer, who started his campaign in favour of reform in the South by means of leaflets and lectures. Was received in audience by the late Emperor Kwang Hsu on June 14, 1898, on the recommendation of Weng T'ung- ho, the Imperial Tutor. He at once obtained a strong influence over the Emperor, whose famous reform decrees of 1898 were inspired by K'ang. Was proscribed, and ordered to be decapitated when the Empress Dowager effected her coup d'état, but escaped, and subsequently resided abroad, principally in America. An advocate of the adoption of Confucianism as the State Religion. Returned to China under the Republic. Was prominent in Peking at the time of Chang Hsun's restoration of the Manchu dynasty, July, 1917, and figured in the appointments made at that time. Now resides at Shanghai. — p. 823 ([scan leaf 896](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n896/mode/1up))

**KAO CHIA-CHI, (高家驥)** —Heilungkiang. Born 1882. Graduated from Law Department, National Law College, Peking. Member, first Republican Parliament. Member, Contitution-Crafting Committee. Vice-Chairman, Provincial Assembly, Heilungkiang. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO CHUANG-KAI** —Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Amoy, April 25, 1913. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO CHUNG-HO, (高仲和)** —Hupeh. Born 1875. A Graduate of a Law School in Japan. After the Revolution he was appointed Councillor of the Tutuh's office, Hupeh. He led troops and captured several cities, and after the Declaration of the Republic he disbanded more than 4000 troops. He was editor of newspapers in Hupeh. A member of Tsan Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Author of Huifumong. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Member of Ministry of Interior. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO EN-HUNG, (高恩洪)** —Shantung. Was a telegraph operator, from which position he rose to be Chief of Material department of the Chinese Telegraph System with headquarters at Shanghai. Appointed Minister of Communications, May 24, 1922. Acting Minister of Education before Tang Erh-ho attended office. Resigned, Dec., 1922 on fall of the Wang Chung-hui Cabinet. First class Tashou Chiaho Pao-kwang Decoration. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO ERH-CHIEN, (高爾謙)** --Fukien. Junior Secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, August, 1907. Yen-An-K'ai-Kung Tao, Yunnan, February, 1908. Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Yunnan, February, 1908. Commissioner for the Macao Delimitation, 1909. Senior Councillor, the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, July, 1910. Double Dragon, 2nd Class, 2nd Grade, June, 1911. Provincial Treasurer, Yunnan, July, 1911. Acting Provincial Treasurer of Szechuan, November, 1911. Vice-Minister for Foreign Affairs; resigned May, 1918. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO LING-WEI, (高凌霨)** —Chihli. Provincial Treasurer of Hupeh when Chang Chih-tung was Viceroy. Devoted much of his time to the promotion of education. Resigned from Government service after the formation of the Republic. After the Chihli-Anfu struggle of 1920 was appointed Vice Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, and later, Minister of Finance. Minister of Interior, 1921. Minister of Communications for a few weeks in 1922, and again Minister of Interior in Dr. Yen's Cabinet. Reappointed Minister of Interior, Jan. 1923. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO LING-YU, (高凌霨)** —Tientsin. Chujen. Has been Commissioner of Education for Hupeh in the Ching Dynasty. Member of First Republican Parliament. Appointed Vice-Minister of Industry and Commerce in the first Chin Yung-peng Cabinet of 1920. Appointed Minister of Finance, Oct. 28, 1921. Transferred to Minister of Interior, Dec. 25, 1921. Acting Minister of Communications, May 1922. Resigned, June 11, 1922. Appointed Civil Governor of Chihli, June 18. 1922 and refused. Was Minister of Agriculture and Commerce in Dr. C. H. Wang's Cabinet, 1922. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO SHEN** —Secretary. Ministry of Navy, September 8, 1912. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO SUNG-JU, (高松如)** —Chihli. Director-General, Chihli Provincial Bank and Industrial Taotai of Hupeh under Manchu Government; Director, Hupeh Government Bank; Co-Director, Hupeh Government Mint. Superintendent of Mines in Hupeh. — p. 824 ([scan leaf 897](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n897/mode/1up))

**KAO TSENG-CHUEH, (高增爵)** —Shensi. Chushi Police Intendant, Szechuan, spring, 1908. Pacificator in Shensi, November, 1911. Acting Civil Governor of Shansi, October 19, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan, 1914. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KENG CH'UN-YEN, (耿春宴)** —Honan. Born 1883. Studied in Japan. A member of the Central Educational Society, and a member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Teacher and Director of various schools. Chinputang. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KENG YEN** —Councillor, Department of Legislation. Cabinet, August 31, 1912. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KING, P. C** see Chin Pang Chêng. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KING, THOMAS** see Chin Chih. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**Koo, V. K. Wellington** see Ku Wei Chun. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KU CHUNG-HSIU, (谷鍾秀)** —Chihli. Born 1874. Graduate of the Peking Government University and a returned student from Japan. Was a Teacher in the High Normal School in Chihli. Was a Secretary to the Governor of Chekiang, establishing eight judicial courts in the Province. After the outbreak at Wuchang, was elected to proceed to Wuchang and Nanking to assist in the organization of the new Government, and was appointed to draft the Provisional Constitution, etc. Was a member of the late National Council, and of the Chung Yi Yuan, and member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Kuomintang. Author of Outlines of Chinese History, Moral Philosophy, The Essentials of a Constitution, Financial Reforms, and a book on Poetry, Translator of several works from foreign languages into Chinese. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Minister of Commerce, 1916. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KU I-TS'ENG, (顧儀曾)** —Peking. Born Oct. 9, 1888. Graduated in the Law Department of Government Law School, Peking. Secretary, Ministry of Interior since Nov. 2, 1919. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KU MING-TE** —Sinkiang. Born 1869. Studied in Sinkiang High School, and a graduate of the school established by Tzu Yi-chu. Vice-Speaker of Sinkiang Provincial Assembly. Chinputang. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KU P'IN-CHEN, (顧品珍)** —Yunnan. Born 1881. Graduated from a Military School in Japan. Upon his return to China, Ku was engaged to train officers in Yunnan. Brigadier General of the 1st. Brigade and General of the 1st. Army Division. Joined the Canton Military Government and is the Commanding Officer of the Yunnan Allied Army. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KU WEI-CHUN, (Dr. V. K. Wellington Koo), (顧維鈞)** —Kiangsu. Ph.D. in Law, Columbia University, New York. Member of Phi Beta Kappa Society. Secretary to Cabinet, May, 1912; retired July, 1912. Secretary to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, August, 1912; Councillor, 1914. Minister Plenipotentiary to Mexico, 1915. Was Minister at Washington, till 1920, when was appointed Minister to London. Was a member of the Peace Conference of Paris, 1918-19. China's representative on the Council of League of Nations. Chinese Delegate to the Washington Conference, Nov. 1921. Visited China, May 1922. Appointed Chairman of the Commission for the Study of National Finance, June 1922, and Minister of Foreign Affairs Sept. to Dec. 1922. Second order of Merit. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KU YUAN** —Secretary, Ministry of the Interior, June, 1912. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KUAN HAI-CH'ING, (關海清)** —Mukden. Born 1882. Educated in the Imperial Peking University. Chujen. Was successively Director of the Mukden Provincial College, Law School, Language School. Secretary to the Military and Civil Governors Appointed Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Fengtien February 1918. Author of "Lecture Notes on Logic." Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 825 ([scan leaf 898](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n898/mode/1up))

**KUAN KENG-LIN, (關賡麟)** --Kwangtung. Born November, 1880. Graduated in Japanese College. Studied Politics and Law in the Imperial Peking University for two and half years. Chinshih. Member of Board of Army 1903. Accompanied the Five Special Commissioners to study Government Systems in Europe and America, 1904. Member of Board of Communications, 1905. Assistant Director, Peking-Hankow Railway, 1910 Director, the same railway, 1912, to 1915. Member of Ministry of Finance, December, 1916. Department Chief, Ministry of Communications, July, 1917. Vice-Chairman and General Secretary, National Railway Association. Member, Finance Committee of the President's Office, Councillor Ministry of Communications, Jan. 1919. Chief, Publication Department in the same Ministry, July, 1919. Director, Hankow-Kwangtung-Szechuan Railway since July, 1920. Appointed President of Chiaotung University, June 15, 1922. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUAN MIEN-CHUN, (關冕鈞)** --Kwangsi. Born 1870. A Chinshih and Hanlin Compiler of the late Dynasty. Was Vice-Director of the Ching-Chang Railway in 33rd year of Kwang Hsu, and was Director of the Ching-Chang and Chang-Sui Railways since the 3rd year of Hsuan Tung. Received a 5th class Chia-ho Order in the 2nd year of the Republic of China. In the 31st year of Kwang Hsu was appointed second-class secretary to proceed to the United States, Russia, Germany, Austria and Italy with the Investigation Commissioners to study the political situations abroad, and at that time he travelled in Japan, Great Britain, France, Belgium and several other countries. Superintendent, Sha-hukou Customs, (Kuei-hua-cheng). Superintendent of Tsaipei Customs. Resigned, June 2, 1922. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUANG CHIN-P'AN, (Chin Pon Kwang). (關錦津)** --Kwangtung. Born 1892. Studied commercial economics in University of Colorado; and railway engineering in Columbia University. Secretary to Chinese Consulate General at San Francisco, 1914. Was railway and bridge engineer in America, 1915-16. Assistant Engineer, Chow-Hsiang Railway, 1917. Joined the Tientsin-Pukow Railway, 1918. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUANG FU** --Mongol Deputy Lieutenant-Governor of Ili, June, 1901. Retired, January, 1908. Acting Tartar General of Ili, June, 1909. Tartar General, April, 1910. Tartar General, Hangchow, February, 1911. Commissioner for Frontier, Ili, May, 1912. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUANG FU-SHAO, (Fong F. Sec.), (關富灼)** --Kwangtung. Born 1869. Studied in the Pomona College, University of California and Columbia University. M.A. Editor of the Commercial Press, Ltd., since 1908. Author, Joint-author and compiler of various text books, reference books, etc. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUANG SUN-MOU, (關孫謀)** --Kwangtung. Born 1863. A Graduate of Boston University. Engineer-in-Chief of the King-Chang and Chang-Sui Railways, and Superintendent of Workshops, since the 6th month of the 3rd year of Hsuan Tung. Has successively been Engineer, Engineer-in-Chief, etc., of Tsin-Pu, Outside Shanhaikuan, Ping-Li, Hsinmintun, Tientsin-Pukow Railways from 1885-1922. Received a 3rd class Chia-ho Decoration from the President. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUNG CHAO-YEN, (孔昭燊)** --Supervisor of the Canton Customs, 1915. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUNG CHENG, (龔政)** --Kwangsi. Born 1886. A Gratuate of the Japanese Imperial University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan and the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUNG CHI-PING, (龔積柄)** --Anhui. Born 1872. Chief, Shantung Higher Court. Salt Commissioner of Shantung, July 1912, Acting Civil Governor of Shantung, Feb. 1914. Taoyin in Shantung. Appointed Commissioner of Finance, Shantung, August 26, 1921. Second class Chiaho and Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 826 ([scan leaf 899](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n899/mode/1up))

**KUNG HSIN** Vice-Minister of Gold Mining Bureau, 1915. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUNG HSIN-CHEN, (龔心湛)** Anhui. Attached to Chinese Legations abroad, and was Taotai in Kwangtung and Yunnan. Chief of the Salt Bureau, June, 1914. Concurrently Director of the Inspectorate General, 1915. Vice-Minister, Minister of Finance and ex-Premier. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUNG SANG NO ERH PU, (貢桑諾爾布) (Prince Kalaching)** Chief of the Bureau of Mongolia and Tibet, September, 1912, till April, 1922. Prince of the Right Wing of the Kharach'in Tribe of the Chosot'u League of Inner Mongolia. Promoted from Second to First class Prince in October, 1912, for loyalty to the Republic. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUNG YUAN-K'AI, (龔元凱)** Anhui. Born 1870. Graduated from Chin Shih Kuan. Hanlin 1903. Wei Ch'uan Taoyin, April, 1919. Author of T'o K'an Poems. Non-partisan. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUO CH'UNG-CH'U, (郭崇渠)** Szechuan. Born August 22, 1882. Graduated from the Szechuan Higher Normal School. Studied for some time in the Peking Law College. Vice-Chairman, Szechuan Provincial Assembly since July, 1919. Member of Kuomintang. Fifth class Wenhu decoration. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUO CHUNG-HSI, (郭宗熙)** Hunan. Civil Governor of Kirin, since June 1916. Appointed Chinese President of the Chinese Eastern Railway, 1918. Has been Taoyin in Kirin. Delegate of Foreign Affairs for Kirin. Educational Commissioner of Kirin. Now retired. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUO HSI-JEN, (郭希仁)** Shensi. Born Feb. 6, 1882. Studied in the Hongtao School and the Provincial College of Shensi. Chujen. Commissioner of Education for the Province of Shensi since April, 1920. Author of various works on irrigation, history, travel and diplomacy. Formerly member of Kuomintang. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUO KAN-CH'ING, (郭幹卿)** Chihli. Born April 23, 1883. Graduated from the Administrative Department of the Peiyang Officers' Training College. Director, Government Mint at Wuchang, since July 1918. Fourth class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUO, DR. P. W** see Kuo Ping-wen. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUO PING-WEN, (P. W. KUO), (郭秉文)** Shanghai. Born 1880. Graduated from Lowrie Institute, Shanghai, of which he became a teacher. Served in Customs service in Shanghai, Kashing and Hangchow. He went to America in 1906, and attended University of Wooster and Columbia University and received the degree of Ph.D. from the latter. After his return to China, he was editor in the Commercial Press and Dean of Government High Normal College, Nanking. Appointed President of the same college, and also President of Southeastern University and of College of Commerce, Nanking and Shanghai. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KUO TSE-YUN, (郭則澐)** Fukien. Born 1882. Hanlin of the late Ching Dynasty. Studied in Waseda University, Japan. Customs Taotai of Wenchow and Chuchow, Chekiang. Police Taotai of Hangchow, 1911. Was twice Chief Secretary to the Cabinet during the establishment of the Republic and in Chien Nun-hsun's Cabinet. Again appointed Chief Secretary to the Cabinet of Chin Yun-peng, in 1918. Director Bureau of Economical study 1920. Chief of Bureau of Immigration, Dec. 18, 1921. Resigned June 15, 1922. Author of Memoirs of Japan, Notes on Legal Subjects, and a collection of poems. First class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho and First class Wenhu decorations. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KWONG, CHIN PON** see Kuang Chin'p'an. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**KWONG, K. Y** see Kuang Sun-mou. — p. 827 ([scan leaf 900](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n900/mode/1up))

**LAN CHIEN-SHU, (藍建樞)** —Fukien. Educated in the Naval College in Fukien. Senior member of Naval General Staff, April 1912. Commander-in-chief of Chinese Navy from March 28, 1918. Fourth Order of Merit. Second class Wenhu, Tashou Chiaho and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LANG KUO-CHEN, (K. T. Long), (郎國楨)** —Kiangsu. Born 1888. Graduated from Nanyang College, Shanghai, and University of Wisconsin, U.S.A. M.A. Engineer, Tientsin Telephone Administration, 1913. Acting Chief, 1916 Sub-Manager, China Electric Co., Ltd., 1918 to date. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LAO CHIH-CH'ANG, (S. W. Lao).—(勞之常)** —Shantung. Was Chief of the Shantung Conservancy Bureau. Chairman, Shantung International Relief Committee. Commissioner to the China International Famine Relief Commission. Appointed Vice-Minister of Communications, June 2, 1922. Appointed to assist Dr. C. T. Wang on the Commission for the Settlement of the Shantung Question, Nov. 1922. First class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LAO, S. W** see Lao Chih-ch'ang. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LAU, W. W** see Liu Huan-wei. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LEE, YEW C** see Li Yao-hsiang. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LEW YUK-LIN** see Liu Yu Lin. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHANG-T'AI, (李長泰)** —Peking. Born 1854. Graduated from Military Academy, Tientsin. Defence Commissioner of Taming, Chihli, 1913. Brigadier General, Commanding General of the 8th Army Division, 1914. Commander, Peking Gendermeric. 1917. Second class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. Second order of Merit. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHAO-CHEN, (李兆珍)** —Fukien. Governor of Anhui, 1915. Reappointed Civil Governor of Anhui, Autumn 1921, but was rejected by the people and held his office only a few days. Created Marquis, 2nd Order, December, 1915. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHAO-FU, (李肇甫)** —Szechuan. Born November 4, 1885. Studied Law in Japan. President, Szechuan Provincial Assembly since July, 1919. Member Kuomintang. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHENG-FEN, (李正芬)** —Yunnan. Born October 7, 1856. Chairman, Yunnan Provincial Assembly since September, 1918. Fourth Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHIN-MIN** —Fukien. Born 1878. Educated in a Japanese University. Chief of the Taxation Department of the Ministry of Finance. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-FANG, (李經芳)** —Anhui. An adopted son of the late Marquis Li Hung-chang. Minister to Japan. Minister to Great Britain, May, 1907. Acting Senior Vice-President of the Ministry of Communications, January, 1911. Director-General of the Chinese Imperial Post Office. Retired in January, 1912. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CH'ING-FANG, (李慶芳)** —Shansi. Born 1878. A Graduate of Shansi University and a University in Japan. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan and member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee and editor of Hsien Fa Hsin Wen. Was Chairman of Educational Society of Shansi. Chinputang. Author of The Construction of a Republic, etc. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-HSI, (李景曦)** —Fukien. Educated in the Naval College, Foochow. Departmental Head, Ministry of Navy since July, 1917. Second class Wenhu and Third class Chiaho decorations. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-HSI, (李經義)** —Anhui. Yung-Ning Tao, Szechuan, May, 1887. Salt Tao-tai, Hunan, August, 1893. Jud. Comm., June, 1897. Fin. Comm., Fukien, September, 1898, of Yunnan, January, 1899. Governor, Kwangsi, April, 1901, of Yunnan, June, 1901. Removed from office, May, 1902. Acting Governor of Kweichow, January, 1903. Governor, Kwangsi, May, 1904; retired, ill-health, October, 1905. Governor-General, Yun-Kuei Provinces, February, 1909. Member of the Tsan-chengyuan, 1915. Director of the Land Survey Office, 1915. ''Friend of Sungshan'' (see Hsu Shih-ch'ang). Elected Premier by both Houses of Parliament, May, 1917, but failed to take up appointment before the dissolution of Parliament, June, 1917. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 828 ([scan leaf 901](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n901/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-LIEN, (李景濂)** —Chihli. Born 1868. Educated in Leng Chi College, Chihli, and a Chinshih of the late Dynasty. Was a Teacher in Chinese at various High Schools and Universities. A member of Chung Yi Yuan. Author of several Chinese poetical and other works which have not yet been printed. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-MAI, (李經邁)** —Anhui. Son of Li Hung-chang. Minister to Austria-Hungary, September, 1905-7: Jud. Comm., Honan, March 1908; resigned, November, 1908. Acting Junior Vice-President, Minchengpu, March, 1911. Acting Vice-Minister of Yu-chuanpu, January, 1912. Relieved of post, January, 1912. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI CHING-MING, (李景銘)** —Fukien. Secretary, Ministry of Finance, September 1, 1912. Auditor, Audit Bureau, November 22, 1913. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI CHIU-YING, (李求膺)** —Shensi. Born 1883. A Graduate of the High School in Shensi, and a University in Japan. Was editor of the Min Lik Pao, Shanghai, and was a member of the Advisory Council in Nanking and Provisional National Council in Peking. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 5, 1913. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI CHUN, (李淮)** —Szechuan. Brigade General, Nan-Ao Chen, Kwangtung, August, 1905. Acting Brigade General, Pakhoi, July, 1907. In charge of piracy suppression in Canton Waters, 1907-8. Admiral, Kwangtung, July, 1909. Commissioner for Pacification, Kwangtung, August 14, 1913. Created (Brevet) General, August 14, 1913, and Chiangchun, Oct. 1922. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI HAN-CH'ENG, (李漢丞)** —Hunan. Born 1867. Graduate of a University in Japan. Hsiutsai of the last Dynasty. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Established a girls' school in Hengshan. Senior Clerk of the Ministry of Industry and Commerce, July, 1912. Resigned, March, 1913. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI HAN-FANG, (李含芳)** —Shensi. Secretary to the Tutuh of Shensi during the revolution. Elected member of Chung Yi Yuan. Principal of a school in his native place. Unseated by Presidential Order of Nov. 4, 1913. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI HAN-FANG, (李含芳)** —Shantung. Born Nov. 30, 1882. Graduated from the Science Department of the Higher Normal School, Peking. Vice-Chairman, Shantung Provincial Educational Association since July, 1917. Director, Girls' Normal School of Shantung since March, 1920, Fifth Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI HOU-CHI, (李厚基)** —Kiangsu. Born 1872. Commandant of Strategic Positions at Woosung, August 19, 1913. Brigadier-General of a Mixed Brigade in Fukien, 1913 Defence Commissioner in Fukien, 1914. Tuchun of Fukien, 1916. Civil Governor of Fukien. Expelled from Fukien as result of revolt in Oct. 1922. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI HSIEH-YANG, (李燮陽)** —Yunnan. Born 1881. Engineer. Graduate of Technical Department of Electricity and Railways in the Ohio Univers- ity, U.S.A. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 829 ([scan leaf 902](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n902/mode/1up))

**LI K'AI-SHEN, (李開佌)** —Hupeh. Acting Chief Civil Administrator, Kwangtung, September 13, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan, May 1914. Chief Secretary to the President, 1917. Resident Commissioner in Urga. Retired. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI KENG-YUAN (李根源)** —Yunnan. Member of House of Representatives. Concerned in the "second revolution" of 1913. Joined Yunnan rebellion, 1915. Appointed Acting Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, Dec. 1922. Appointment confirmed Jan. 1923 — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI KUO-CHEN, (李國珍)** —Kiangsi. Born 1881. A Graduate of a University in Japan. After returning from Japan was engaged as a Teacher in various Law Schools. Was instrumental in bringing about the Revolution in Kiangsi, and when the people there determined to kill Governor Feng, it was due to his speeches made before a great audience that the Governor was sent out of Kiangsi unmolested. Was a member of the Kiangsi Assembly and the late National Assembly. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan and a member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee, Chinputang. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Commerce; of Education, 1916. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI KWEI-YUAN, (李奎元)** —Peking. Lieutenant-General. Commanding General of the 11th. Army Division. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI LIEH-CHUN, (李烈鈞)** —Kiangsi. Studied six years in Japan. Tutuh of Kiangsi: dismissed and ordered to Peking, June 9, 1913. Deprived of all ranks and outlawed for his part in the rebellion, July 15, 1913. Reinstated as Tuchun of Kiangsi, 1916. Commander of Yunnan troops. Joined the Canton Government. First order of Merit. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI PING, (李鈳)** —Kansu. Born Feb. 17, 1887. Graduated from the Kansu Provincial College. President, Kansu Provincial Educational Association, since June, 1920. Kuomintang. Seventh class Chiaho decoration. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI PING-SHU, (李秉怨)** —Fengtien. Born 1877. A Graduate of a Normal School in Tokio. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was a Teacher and Director of several Normal Schools. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI SHENG-TO, (李盛鐸)** —Kiangsi. Born 1862. Chinshih. Director, Peking University. Minister to Japan and Belgium. Magistrate of Peking. Special Commissioner to foreign countries to investigate politics. Governor of Shansi. Political Adviser to the President. Chairman of the Senate, dissolved after the Anfu defeat in 1920. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI SHIH-WEI, (李士偉)** —Chihli. Member of the Tsanchengyuan. Resigned on becoming President of the Bank of China, 1915. appointed Minister of Finance, May 1921, but did not accept office. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI SZU-HAO, (李思浩)** —Chekiang. Born 1880. Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. Secretary in the Ministry of Finance in his early age, and concurrently rendered his service in the Salt Administration. Vice-Minister of Finance, 1916. Minister of Finance, 1919, Director-General of the Currency Reform Bureau. Director-General, Salt Administration. Member of the Anfu Political Club. Since the defeat of the Club he has been in hiding. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI TING-HSIN, (李鼎新)** —Fukien. Studied in England. Councillor, Ministry of Navy, September 5, 1912. Chief of Naval General Staff, December 11, 1912. Created Admiral (brevet), August 20, 1913. Commander-in-Chief of the Navy. Director of Kiangnan Arsenal. Cashiered of rank, but retained in office, December, 1915. Minister of Navy, May 1921 to date. Second order of Merit. — p. 830 ([scan leaf 903](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n903/mode/1up))

**LI TING-YU, (李廷玉)** Acting Military Assistant Governor of Ulliassutar, August 24, 1912. Acting Deputy Commissioner of Occupation, Kinkiang, August 1, 1913. Commissioner of Occupation, South Kiangsi, Dec. 1913. Dismissed, August, 1914. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI TSAI-KENG, (李載賡)** Honan. Born 1883. Graduate of a Law School in Japan. Was Secretary of the Ministry of Communications and a member of the Advisory Council in Nanking Government. Was editor of Hsin Hwa Pao, Tientsin. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Author of The details of the Mines of Honan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI TSO-JEN, (李作人)** Shantung. Born 1850. Has been in business for over fifty years. President of Chefoo Y. M. C. A. since 1916. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI YAO-HSIANG, (Yew C. Lee), (李耀祥)** Kwangtung. Born 1882. Studied in Harvard University, U.S.A. Engineer, Peking-Kalgan, Canton-Hankow, and Chuchow-Chin-chow Railways, 1906-18. Surveyor, Chihli River Commission. 1918 to date. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI YI-YU** Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Pakhoi, April 25, 1913. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI YU-LIN, (李玉麟)** Special Commissioner for Disbanding troops in Anhui, Nov., 1922. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI YING-CH'UAN, (李英銓)** Kwangtung. Born 1876. Graduate of Law School in Kwangtung. Was Director of a Normal School at Kwangtung; Delegate for prison reform at Wenyuenhsien; a practitioner in the local court of Kwangchow; Judge in Yuyuanhsien and Nanhsunghsien; and a lawyer in Kuangchow. Member of the Senate. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI YUAN-HUNG, (黎元洪)** Hupeh. Born Oct. 19, 1864. Studied at Peiyang Naval College, graduating in 1888 after a course of six years. Served on a cruiser during the Sino-Japanese War. After the war he was engaged for service at Nanking by Viceroy Chang Chih Tung. On the latter's transfer to Wuchang he accompanied him to assist in the organization of the modern troops there. Thence he went to Japan for two years to study fortification. On his return he became a Major in the cavalry in 1895, and subsequently held several commands, including that of Colonel in the 21st Brigade. He was in charge of the organization of the Changteh Manoeuvres in 1905 and for the five following years served on the staff at Wuchang. On the outbreak of the Revolution at Wuchang he was forced into accepting the command of the revolutionary forces, whose operations he directed thenceforward. He was mainly instrumental in arranging for the Shanghai Peace Conference. After the abdication of the Manchus he was elected Vice-President of the Republic, and appointed Chief of the General Staff and Tutuh of Hupeh. (November, 1911). Given rank of General on September 7. Acting Tutuh, Kiangsi June 8, 1913. Re-elected Vice-President of the Republic. Oct. 7, 1913. On the death of Yuan Shih-kai, he became President of the Republic (June, 1916), resigned July 1, 1917, when Chang Hsun carried out his coup d'etat. Author of various lecture notes not published. Formerly member of Chinputang but resigned therefrom when accepting Chief of General Staff. Re-assumed the Presidency, June 11, 1922. First Order of Merit. First class of Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI YUAN-LIANG, (李元亮)** Shantung. Born 1884. A Graduate of the High Normal School in Shantung. A member of the Shantung Provincial Assembly. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Teacher to several Schools in Shantung. Chinputang. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LI YUNG-HAN, (李永瀚)** Honan. Born Sept. 1859. Studied in a normal school in Japan. Vice-Chairman, Honan Provincial Assembly. Formerly of Kunghotang. — p. 831 ([scan leaf 904](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n904/mode/1up))

**LIANG CH'ENG-CHIU, (梁成久)** —Kwangtung. Born 1859. A Hsiutsai of the late Ching Dynasty. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Director and Teacher of several Schools, and is now one of the promoters of the Society for the Revival of Confucianism. Chinputang. Author of a poetical work in Chinese. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 832 ([scan leaf 905](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n905/mode/1up))

**LIANG CH'I-CHAO, (梁啟超)** —Kwangtung. A brilliant scholar, who is best known as K'ang Yu-wei's most prominent disciple. Started the first Chinese daily paper in Peking, a small leaflet, containing an editorial only, which was given away gratuitously. Was proscribed, and had to fly for his life, after the coup d'état of 1898, and thereafter resided in Japan, where he conducted a paper in Chinese, devoted to the cause of reform. Like K'ang Yu-wei, advocated a limited monarchy in preference to a Republic. Returned to China after the Revolution, and then conducted a daily paper in Tientsin, advocating the spread of political education, and the diffusion of general knowledge among the Chinese. Appointed Vice-Minister of Justice in Yuan Shih-kai's first Cabinet, but refused to accept office. Editor of Yung Yen Pao ("Justice"), a bi-monthly periodical, and one of the chief organizers of the Chinputang. Minister of Justice in Hsiung Hsi-ling's Cabinet, September 11, 1913. Resigned 1914. Appointed Head of Currency Bureau, which was afterwards incorporated in the Ministry of Finance, November 15, 1912. Resides in Tientsin, and is a teacher of History in various colleges in North China. Visited Paris during the Peace Conference. — p. 832 ([scan leaf 905](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n905/mode/1up))

**LIANG FU. (梁宏)** —Fukien. Japan-educated. Was Presiding Judge, Procurator's Court for the Peking District. Appointed Chief Secretary to the Cabinet, Oct., 1922. Resigned Dec. 1922. — p. 832 ([scan leaf 905](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n905/mode/1up))

**LIANG JU-HAO, (better known as M. T. Liang), (梁如浩)** —Kwangtung. Educated in America. Taotai, Newchwang, April, 1906. Customs Taotai, Tientsin, April, 1907. Shanghai Taotai, October, 1907. Secretary of Board of Foreign Affairs, March, 1908. Chief Secretary to Government of Fengtien, July, 1908. Recalled to Peking, June, 1909. Vacated office, November, 1909. Offered post of Vice-Minister of Communications in Yuan Shih-kai's Cabinet of November 16, 1911, but did not accept. Nominated for the post of Minister of Communications in the first Republican Cabinet, but the nomination was rejected by the National Council. Elected Minister of Foreign Affairs on September 16, resigned November 15, 1912. Resides in Tientsin. Organized and was President of North China International Society of Famine Relief 1920-21. President, China International Framine Relief Commission. Adviser to the Chinese delegation to the Washington Conference, Nov. 1921. Was appointed Commissioner on April 26, 1922, by President Hsu to negotiate with the British Government on the question of return of Weihaiwei to China. First class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 832 ([scan leaf 905](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n905/mode/1up))

**LIANG, L. K** see Liang Lai K'uei. — p. 832 ([scan leaf 905](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n905/mode/1up))

**LIANG LAI-K'UEI, (L. K. Liang). (梁賓奎)** —Fushan, Kwangtung. Born 1879. Studied at Peiyang University and Anglo-Chinese College at Tientsin. Studied Agriculture in Massachusetts Agriculture College, 1904-8; at Cornell University, 1908-10. B.S., M.S. Chinshih, 1910; Hanlin, 1911. Director, Experimental Station, Department of Agriculture, Government University, Peking, 1911-12. Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry, July 31, 1912. Vice-Minister of Agriculture and Forestry, 1912-1913. In business since 1911. Author of Alkali Investigation; Wheat. — p. 832 ([scan leaf 905](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n905/mode/1up))

**LIANG LAN-HSUN, (梁瀾勳)** —Kwangtung. Born 1871. Studied in the Queen's College, Hongkong. Associate of Arts Oxford University, Eng. Formerly Chinese Consul General at Melbourne, Australia. In 1920 became Superintendent of Customs, Kwangtung, and Commissioner of Customs for Kwangtung. Translated a certain book on the Science History of the 19th century. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIANG, M. T** see Liang Ju-hao. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIANG PEI, (梁培)** —Kwangsi. Born 1880. A Graduate of the High Preparatory School at Kwangsi. Was a Teacher to several Schools. During the Revolution he assisted in the capture of the military depôt in Kwangsi. Chairman of Kuomintang branch at Nanning, and editor of the Minjengpao and Hsichiangpao of Kwangsi. Member of the Senate. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIANG SHANG-TUNG, (梁上棟)** .—Secretary to the Ministry of War, 1915. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIANG SHENG-TE, (梁聲德)** .—Heilungkiang. Was a petty official during the Manchu regime. Became member of Heilungkiang Provincial Assembly during the first and second elections and is now Chairman of the Assembly. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIANG SHIH-YI, (梁士詒)** .—Kwangtung. Secretary to Tang Shao-yi on the occasion of his mission to India in 1906. Chief of Department of Board of Communications, 1907. Director of Railways in Board of Communications, 1907. Assistant Director of Chiao T'ung Bank, 1907. Senior Secretary of Board of Communications, July, 1909. Vacated appointments of Director of Railways and Assistant Director of Chiao T'ung Bank, February, 1911. Acting Vice-Minister of Communications, November, 1911. Acting Director of Imperial Chinese Posts, December, 1911. Acting Minister of Communications, January to March, 1912. Appointed Chief Secretary in the President's Office after the abdication of the Manchus (April, 1912). Acting Vice-Minister of Finance, May-September, 1913. Member of the Tsanchengyuan (Council of State). Director-General of the Shuiwuchu (Customs Administration), 1915. Director-General of the Bureau of Taxes, 1915. Resigned after failure of Yuan Shih-kai's monarchical project, of which he was one of the chief promoters. Speaker of the Senate May-October, 1918. Appointed, under influence of General Chang Tso-lin, Premier, Dec. 24, 1921, but was rejected by General Wu Pei-fu. Ordered to be arrested for trial, May 5, 1922, when Chang Tso-lin was defeated by the Chihli party. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIANG TUN-YEN, (梁敦彥)** .—Kwangtung. Educated in America. For several years interpreter to Viceroy Chang Chih-tung. Customs Taotai, Hankow, October, 1903, and at Tientsin, October, 1904. Minister to America, May 1907 (but did not proceed). Junior Vice-President of Board of Foreign Affairs, July, 1908. Acting President of same Board, January, 1909. President of Board of Foreign Affairs, January, 1909. Associate Controller of Grand Revenue Council, March, 1910. Retired on account of illness, July, 1910. Minister to America, 1911. Appointed Minister for Foreign Affairs in Prince Ching's Cabinet of May, 1911, and Yuan Shih-kai's Cabinet of November, 1911, but did not return to China to take up the post. Minister of Communications, 1915. Figured in Chang Hsun's Monarchical Coup. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIANG YEN-FANG, (梁懩芳)** .—Kwangtung. Born 1888. Graduated from the Department of Politics of the Kwangtung Law College. Presiding Judge, Higher Procurator's Court for Kwangtung since May 28, 1918. Fourth Class Wenhu Decoration. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIAO HSI-HSIEN, (廖希賢)** .—Szechuan. Born 1885. A Graduate of Tungwen College in Szechuan, and a University in Japan. Was editor of Szechuan Jih Pao, and he established a Finance College in Szechuan, which at present has more than 500 students. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Kunghotang. — p. 833 ([scan leaf 906](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n906/mode/1up))

**LIAO HSIEH, (廖燮)** Honan. Born October 26, 1875. Studied in the First Normal School, Honan. Vice-Chairman, Honan Provincial Assembly. Formerly of Kuomintang. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIEN WEN-CH'ENG** Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Changchun, April 25, 1913. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN CHANG-MIN, (林長民)** Fukien. Secretary to the Council of State, 1915. Minister of Justice, July, 1917. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN CHENG-HSUAN, (林正煊)** Kwangtung. Born 1885. Graduated from the Kwangtung Provincial College. Chairman, Kwangtung Provincial Assembly since 1919. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN HAN, (林翰)** Fukien. Born 1879. Graduated in political science from a college in Japan, 1911. Teacher to the Public Law School of Fukien. Vice-Chairman, Fukien Provincial Assembly, 1913. Chairman, Fukien Provincial Assembly. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN LO-CHUAN** Fukien. Born 1879. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Editor of several Fukien newspapers. Chairman of the Association of the Representatives of Chinese Residents Abroad. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN PAO-YI, (林葆懿)** Fukien. Born 1862. Graduated from the Navigation Department of the Foochow Naval College. Commanding officer in the Pei Yang Navy Division. Admiral of the First Fleet of Chinese Navy. Minister of Navy in the Canton Military Government. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN PO-HO, (林伯和)** Kwangtung. Born 1875. Established a Po-Ai Hospital in Chunhwahsien, and was Director for two years. Was four years Director of a Middle School in Au-men. In the time of Revolution he was appointed by the Tutuh of Kwangtung Pacificator of San Lo, and after two months he was made the Chief of General Department under the magistracy of Shuntehhsien. A member of the Y.M.C.A. Committee in Canton. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN SUNG-LING, (林崧齡)** Kirin. Born June 1868. Educated in the Ts'ung Wen Seminary, Kirin. Commissioner of Industries since July 1920. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN TUNG-SHIH, (Lynn Tong-Sih, or Dun-Min T. S. Lynn).—(林桐寶).—** Fukien. Born 1881. Graduate, University of Paris. Attache to the Chinese Legation, Paris, 1902. Secretary, 1905-09. First Secretary of the Special Mission sent to Belgium, Denmark, Spain and Sweden, August 1909. First Secretary to the Chinese Legation, Paris, 1910. Charge d'Affaires, Paris, August, 1911. Charge d'Affaires in Portugal, France, Habana and Brazil. Adviser on Foreign Affairs to the President, 1918. Member of Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1918 to date. Second class Paokuang Chiaho and Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIN WEN-YUAN, (凌文淵)** Vice-Minister of Finance in Dr. Wang Chung-hui's Cabinet, 1922. When Premier Wang Ta-hsieh refused to assume his concurrent post of Minister of Finance, Lin was in charge of the Ministry. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LING PING, (凌冰)** Honan. Born July 14, 1894. A. B. Stanford University, 1916. M. A. Columbia University, 1917. Ph.D. Clark University, 1919. Dean of Nankai College. Tientsin, since 1919. Author of Public Schools and the War, and Outline of Psychology of the Children. Commissioner of Education for Honan, Summer, 1922. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIU CH'ANG-PING, (劉長炳)** Yunnan. Acting Legal Commissioner, Sinkiang, December 16, 1912. Acting Head of Law Preparation Department, Sinkiang, January 19, 1913. Acting Taoyin in Sinkiang, Dec. 1917. — p. 834 ([scan leaf 907](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n907/mode/1up))

**LIU CHEN-HUA, (劉鎭華)** .—Honan. Born Oct. 1883. Graduated in the Law College, Peking. Civil Governor of Shensi since April 1, 1918. Also Commanding officer of the "Sung" Army. Acting Tuchun of Shensi, May 10, 1922. First class Tashou Chiaho and Second class Wenhu decorations. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU CHENG-YUAN** —Assistant-Director of the Military Affairs of Shensi. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU CHIH-CHIEH, (劉之潔)** .—Chihli. Japanese educated. Commissioner of Defence and Commander of 19th Army Corps, Kiangpeh, May, 1912. Commissioner of Occupation of Soochow, August 27, 1913. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU CHIH-CHOW, (劉治洲)** .—Shensi. Born 1881. A Graduate of the Shanghai Li-hua College. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. After returning to his native place from Shanghai, he was engaged as Lecturer in several Schools. When his Province adopted the cause of Revolution, local "tufeis" arose and he trained a band of volunteers to suppress them, and he also held Fencheng against Sheng Yun for more than seventy days, until the inauguration of the Republic. Chenyuhui. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU CHING-JEN, (劉鏡人)** .—Kiangsu. Minister to Holland September, 1911 Minister to Russia, September, 1912. Appointed Minister to Japan, but did not proceed. President, Commission for Russian Affairs, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1922. First class Tashou Chiaho decoration — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU CHU-WU, (劉祖武)** .—Yunnan. General. Appointed Civil Governor of Yunnan on outbreak of revolt. 1915. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU CHUAN-SHOU, (劉傳綬)** .—Fukien. Born 1870. Graduated in 1889 from the Peiyang Naval College, Tientsin. Was Councillor, and Department Head, Ministry of Navy. Vice-Minister of Navy. Counsellor of the Chiangchunfu, April 1918. Adviser to the Ministry of Navy. Nonpartisan. Fourth order of Merit. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU CHUNG-HUA, (劉鍾華)** .—Yunnan. Born 1875. Graduated from Japanese College. Studied in two Normal schools in Tokio. Is teacher of Analytical chemistry in the Provincial Technical College and First Government Middle School. Vice-Chairman, Yunnan Provincial Educational Association, in 1911-1915. Re-Elected August 1919. Author and compiler of various lecture notes in chemistry. First Class Tashou Paokwang decoration. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU EN-YUAN, (劉恩源)** .—Co-Director, Administration of Kiaochao Port, Dec. 1922. Minister of Finance, Jan. 1923. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU FENG-SHU** —Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Ichang, April 15, 1913. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU FU, (劉稹)** .—Hunan. Born 1883. Educated in Japan. Compiler, Law Compiling Bureau. Nanking Provisional Government 1912. Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry, May, 1912. Councillor, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, Member of Political Conference. Examiner, Civil Service Examination, January, 1914. Councillor, Ministry of Interior, Member of Committee on Convocation of Parliament. Honorary Member of Financial Investigation Commission of the Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. Adviser to the Industrial Lottery Bureau. Director of Bureau of Port Development of Hankow since January 1917. Appointed Vice Minister of Interior, Nov. 1922. Wrote several books on Salt administration, mining, politics, diplomacy, finance, etc. Second class Chiao decoration. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU HSIEN-SHIH, (劉顯世)** .—Kweichow. Chiangchun and Acting Civil Governor of Kweichow. Ordered to vacate his post, February, 1916. (Joined Yunnan revolt.) Commander-in-Chief of the Kweichow troops. Succeeded by General Lu T'ao. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU HUAI-SEN, (劉槐森)** .—Anhui. Acting Commissioner of Occupation of Kiukiang, August 4, 1913, of Kung Nan (South Kiangsi), Oct. 1913. — p. 835 ([scan leaf 908](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n908/mode/1up))

**LIU HUAN-WEI, (W. W. Lau), (劉寰偉)** —Kwangtung. Born 1891. Graduated from Canton Christian College, 1909. Cornell University 1914 A.B., 1915 C.E. U. S. Army Field Engineer School, Fort Leavenworth. Graduated 1916. Member of faculty, Tsing Hua College, Peking, 1916-18. Engineer Surveyor, Chihli River Commission, Tientsin, 1918-19. Member of faculty, St. John's University, Shanghai, 1919-22. Asst. Professor, St. John's University, 1922. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU HUI-T'UNG, (劉會同)** —Kirin. Born April 10, 1888. Studied in the Kirin Normal College. Vice-Chairman, Kirin Provincial Assembly since September 1, 1918. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU JO-TSENG, (劉若曾)** —Chihli. Chinkiang Taotai, January, 1906. Proctor. Bureau of Government Administration, June, 1906. Vice-Director of High Court of Justice, November, 1906. High Commissioner Revision of Law March, 1911. Resigned May, 1912. Provincial Treasurer, Chihli, December, 1912. Acting Chief Civil Commissioner, Chihli, July 7, 1913. Member of Tsanchengyuan, Feb. 1914. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU KAN** —Shensi. Born 1874. Graduate of Hengtao College. A Hsiu-tsai of the late Dynasty. The Speaker of the Shensi Assembly. Was a Teacher at several Schools, and was Vice-Speaker of the Provisional Assembly of Shensi. Kuomintang. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU KUAN-HSIUNG, (劉冠雄)** —Fukien. Educated at the Royal Naval College, Greenwich, and subsequently given a commission in the Chinese Navy. Distinguished himself at the Battle of the Yalu in the China-Japan war. Appointed Minister of the Navy in the first Republican Cabinet, which post he held for some years. Admiral Liu was a member of the National Council at Nanking, and also of the Southern Delegation which was sent to Peking to congratulate President Yuan Shih-kai upon his election. Acting Minister of Communications in July. Acting Minister of Education, January 13. Inspector-General of Southern Seas, August 14, 1913. Acting Tutuh of Fukien (additional), November, 1913. Minister of the Navy, July, 1917. Appointed Pacificator of Fukien, Nov. 1922. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU KUEI-YI, (劉揆一)** —Hunan. Educated in Japan, where he studied law and politics. Formerly edited several papers, and it was as a journalist that he achieved distinction during the Revolution. Minister of Commerce and Industry, August, 1912. Resigned July 18, 1913. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU LIEN, (劉[illegible])** —Kiangsi. Born 1875. Studied Law in Japan. Was Teacher to several Government and private Schools in his own Province. Was for six months Chief of the Department of Justice in Kiangsi. Chief of the Commission for the examination of judicial officials. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU LING-CHOU, (劉[illegible])** —Shensi. Born 1857. Vice-Chairman, Kansu Provincial Chamber of Commerce 1908-1909. Chairman since Sept. 1920. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU MENG-TEN, (劉夢[illegible])** —Chihli. Born 1882. Was Director-General of the Tientsin Mint. Appointed Governor of the Metropolitan District, May 19, 1922. 5th Order of Merit, 2nd class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**LIU P'ENG-SHOU, (劉彭壽)** —Chihli. Born 1875. Graduate of Chihli High Normal School. Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. Since the 33rd year of Kwang Hsu he was engaged as a Teacher in various High Schools in Chihli. Was a member of Red Cross Society during the Revolution; and established twelve Primary and High Schools in his native place during the past ten years. Member of Chihli Assembly, and President of the Committee of the House, June. 1912. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan Chinputang. Appointed Superintendent of the Tientsin Cus- toms, March, 1922. Author of Outlines of Military History for Normal Schools, and Outlines of the History of the West. — p. 836 ([scan leaf 909](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n909/mode/1up))

**Liu Shih-hsun, (劉式訓)** —Kiangsu. Born 1868. Studied French for fifteen years both at Shanghai and Peking. Was Chinese Minister to France, and Acting Minister to Germany and Portugal. Member of the Arbitration Department of the Hague. President's Adviser on Foreign Affairs. Vice-Minister of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, January to August 8, 1913. Resigned. Special Commissioner Foreign Affairs, Shanghai. Vice-Minister of Foreign affairs, 1920. Resigned, Jan. 5, 1922. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Liu Shu-shun, (劉樹春)** —Kirin. Born 1885. Studied in a native normal school. Vice-Chairman, Kirin Provincial Assembly since autumn, 1921. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Liu Sung-hui** —Director of the River Conservancy in Honan, 1915. Assistant Resident at Kobdo, 1915. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Liu Tao-jen, (劉道仁)** —Supervisor of the Changsha Customs and Special Delegate of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in Hunan, 1915. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Liu Tsun-hou, (劉存厚)** —Szechuan. Born 1885. Graduated in Infantry Department of a Japanese Military College. Major General in the Army. Commanding General of the 2nd Army Division and concurrently Defence Commissioner of Chungking. Tuchun of Szechuan. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Liu Yu-lin, (Lew Yuk Lin), (劉玉麟)** —Kwangtung. Born 1862. Graduated from Philips Academy Andover, Mass., U. S. A. LL. D. (Cantab Hon. causa) Consul-General in South Africa, October, 1904. Attached to Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1908. Chinese Representative at the International Opium Commission, Shanghai, January 1909. Junior Councillor, Waiwupu, July, 1910. Minister to Great Britain, September, 1910-4. High Political Adviser to the Military Government. Salt Commissioner for Kwangtung and Kwangsi. Second class Double Dragon Medal, Tashih Chiaho, First class Wenhu Decoration. Received also decorations from Belgium, Portugal, Italy, Russia and Germany. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Liu Yuan, (劉垣)** —Chihli. For sometime Manager, Tientsin Branch of the Bank of Communications. Was in banking business in Peking. Vice-Minister of Commerce, October 25, 1913. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Lo Chia-heng, (羅家衡)** —Kiangsi. Born 1883. Graduate of a University in Japan. Established a Law School in Kiangsi and was teacher there. When the Revolution broke out in Wuchang, was appointed Chief of the Bureau of Civil Affairs, Kiangsi. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Lo Chiao-han, (羅朝漢)** —Studied in the Peiyang Telegraph School. Director, Chinese Government Telephone Administration, Peking Office, since April, 1912. Fourth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Lo P'an-hui, (羅泮輝)** —Kwangtung. Born 1881. Graduate of Tientsin University; of Harvard University and Chicago University, U.S.A. Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Kwangtung, April 25, 1913. In 1911 during the Revolution he was appointed an official of the Legislative Bureau and Vice-Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs, Kwangtung, and then Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs. Kuomintang. Author of The Laws of China in English. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Lo Pei-chin, (羅佩金)** —Yunnan. Born 1879. A Graduate of the Military Officers' Training School. Major-General of the Army. While in Japan he edited a periodical called Yunnan Cha Chih, which promulgated revolutionary doctrines. Invited to Yunnan by Viceroy Li Ching-hsi, and given various appointments. Before the Revolution was appointed Brigadier of the 74th Brigade. By the help of General Tsai Ao the whole Province was captured and troubles from local "tufeis" sup- pressed. Was appointed Military Delegate to Peking, and Military Councillor of the President's Office. Chief Civil Administrator of Yunnan, January, 1913; resigned April 4, 1913. Tuchun of Szechuan, July, 1916. — p. 837 ([scan leaf 910](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n910/mode/1up))

**Lo Tsung-yi, (羅忠詒)** —Fukien. Born 1886. Cambridge University; B.A. (Economics Tripos). A Chin-shih and Hanlin Compiler of the late Dynasty. English Secretary to the Governor of Kwangsi, 1909. Joined Waiwupu, 1910. Transferred to the Chiaotungpu on the establishment of the Republic. Director of the College of Communications, 1912. Secretary in the President's Office and Member of the Office of the Master of Ceremonies, 1913. First Secretary of the Chinese Legation in London, July, 1914. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Lo Wen-kan, (羅文幹)** —Kwangtung. Born 1888. Studied law in England, M.A. Oxon, and member of Inner Temple. Judicial Commissioner for Kwangtung, 1911-1912. Procurator-General, 1912-1915. Vice-President, Law Codification Commission since 1916. Professor of Law, Peking Government University and School for Judicial Officers. Appointed Vice-Minister of Justice, Dec. 29, 1922. In charge of the Ministry, April 22, 1922. Appointed Chief Judge of the Supreme Court, June 15. 1922. Minister of Finance, Oct. to Dec. 1922. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Loe Meng Len, Dr** see Jao Meng-jen. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Long, K. T** see Lang Kuo-chen. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Loo, Hing-yun, (H. Y.)** see Lu Hsing-yuan. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Lou Tseng Tsiang** see Lu Cheng-hsiang. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Lowe, S. K** —Educated first in Tientsin University and afterwards in Japan, England and Belgium. Appointed Chief of the Bureau of Political Affairs in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in May, 1920. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Lu Ch'ang-yu, (陸長佑)** —Kiangsu. Born Nov. 12, 1862. Commissioner of Administration for Chihli since July 1917. Second class Tashou Pa kwang Chiaho and Fourth class Wenhu decorations. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**Lu Cheng-hsiang, (Lou Tseng Tsiang), (陸徵祥)** —Kiangsu. Born 1871. Educated in the Shanghai Language School and the Tung Wen Kuan, Peking. Interpreter of the Chinese Legation at St. Petersburg, 1890. Attaché, 1892. Secretary, 1895. Deputed to accompany the Chinese Envoy Extraordinary to the Coronation of the late Tsar. Chinese Delegate at the Hague Conference in 1899. Minister to the Netherlands, 1905. Delegate at the Second Hague Conference in 1907. Minister to Holland (renewed) in 1908. Negotiated the Consular Convention with Holland. Sent to the Hague to exchange ratifications of the Convention in 1911, and thence proceeded to St. Petersburg to undertake negotiations with the Russian Government for the revision of the Treaty of 1881. Appointed Minister to Russia. Elected Minister of Foreign Affairs in the first Republican Cabinet, and arrived at Peking on May 24th 1912 to take up the post. When Tang Shao-yi, the Premier, was granted sick leave, Lu was appointed acting premier, and on the resignation of Tang, was elected Premier but retained the portfolio of Foreign Affairs. Was on sick leave during August and September and resigned Premiership on September 23. On the resignation of Liang Ju-hao, he was appointed Minister of Foreign Affairs, November 15, 1912. Resigned September 4, 1913. Master of Ceremonies at President Yuan's Inauguration. Acting Secretary of State and Minister of Foreign Affairs, 1915. Chief Delegate to the Paris Peace Conference. President, National Famine Prevention Commission since May 19, 1921. Appointed Minister to Switzerland, June 15, 1922. Non-partisan. Chiaho Pao-kuang Chiaho and Second class Wenhu decorations. — p. 838 ([scan leaf 911](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n911/mode/1up))

**LU CHIN, (陸錦)** —Chihli. Born 1880. Studied Military Science in a Japanese School. Was connected with the Army in Chihli and Shantung before he became Assistant-Chief of the General Staff and Commanding General of the 9th. Army Division. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU CHIU-SHAN, (盧金山)** —Appointed Commander 18th Division, August 31, 1921. Hupeh Commander of the 3rd and 4th Mixed Brigade. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU HAI-HUAN, (呂海寰)** —Shantung. Minister to Germany, June, 1897. President of Censorate, August, 1901. Minister of Foreign Affairs, November, 1901. Commissioner for Treaty Revision, 1902. President of Board of War, January, 1906. Again Minister of Foreign Affairs, June, 1907. President of Revenue Council, Sept. 1907. Director-General of Tientsin Pukow Railway, January, 1908, from which post he was removed in July, 1909. Placed in charge of Red Cross work, by Imperial Decree, during the Revolution, an appointment which was confirmed by the President in September, 1912. President, Shantung Famine Relief Society, 1920-21. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU HSIAO-CHIH, (路孝植)** —Shensi. Born May 3, 1883. Studied Agriculture in Japan. Chujen. Appointed Commissioner of Education for Hupeh, July, 1919. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU HSING-LIN, (陸杏林)** —Kwangsi. Born 1880. Studied Local Administration in Kwangsi. Member, Tzu Yi Chü, Kwangsi, 1909. Ch'ing Lien Taoyin since October, 1915. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU HSING-YUAN, (Hing-yun Loo or H. Y. Loo), (盧興原)** —Macao. Born 1885. After studying Chinese classics he went to England in 1909 under private scholarship. He stayed in England for seven years and attended Oxford University and the Inner Temple in London. He learned the French Language in France for about a year. He was called to the English Bar in 1916 and took his M.A. Degree at Oxford in 1915. Since he returned to China he has been practising law in Shanghai and Canton. Was a lecturer on law, Soochow University and has on two occasions acted as Departmental Chief of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in the Military Government of Canton. President of the Euro-American Returned Students' Association, Adviser to the Civil Governor of Kwangtung and a Compiler in the Canton Law Commission in 1920. Appointed Chief Justice of the Supreme Court at Canton Feb. 1921. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU HSUEH-CHIH** —Shensi. Born 1882. A Graduate of a University in Japan. Acting Secretary of the Ministry of Education, appointed May 17, 1913. Was Director of the Agriculture College, Hunan and teacher of a High School, Kiangsu. Was Chief of Technical Department of the Ministry of Education. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU HUNG-YING** —Military Governor of the East Circuit in Kansu, 1915. Vice-President of the Law Compilation Bureau, 1918. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU KUNG-WANG, (呂公望)** —Chekiang. Born 1880. Graduated from the Army Officers' Training School, Paoting. Was a training officer in Chekiang for a number of years after graduation. During the Revolution of 1911, was Chief of Staff, Chekiang Revolutionary Army. Became General of the Chekiang 11th Brigade in 1912. In June of the same year, was appointed commanding Officer of the 6th. Division. One month later, was appointed Defence Commissioner of the Kashing-Wuchow district, Che. Appointed Tuchun and Civil Governor of Chekiang, March, 1916. Commander-in-Chief, Chekiang Provincial Army, July, 1918. Became Chief of Staff, Canton Military Government in April, 1920. Second Order of Merit, First class Tashou Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**LU PEN-CHUAN** —Delegate for Foreign Affairs, Shansi, April 15, 1913. — p. 889 ([scan leaf 912](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n912/mode/1up))

**Lu Pi, (盧弼)** —Hupeh. Secretary Department of Civil Service, Cabinet, August 17, 1912. Secretary to Cabinet, October 18, 1912. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Pu-o, (盧步峨)** —Shansi. Born 1877. Vice-Chairman, Shansi Provincial Chamber of Commerce. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Tao, (盧燾)** —Kwangsi. Commanding Officer of the 2nd Brigade of the First Army Division of Kweichow. Succeeded General Liu Hsien-shih as Commander-in-Chief of Kweichow troops, 1920. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Tiao-yuan, (呂調元)** —Anhui. Chinshih. Was magistrate of Tientsin. Governor of Hupeh. Governor of Shensi, 1915. Civil Governor of Anhui, 1920. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Tung** —Acting Councillor, Ministry of Finance, January 23, 1913. Councillor, Ministry of Finance, April 1, 1913. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Ting, (陸定)** —Kiangsu. Born 1873. Studied in Japan. Was Chief of the Audit Department in the Ministry of Finance, Chief Secretary of Tzu Cheng Yuan, and is Councillor to the Ministry of Finance and member of several Committees of the Ministry. Kuomintang. Author of Banking System, etc. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Tsung-yu, (陸宗輿)** —Chekiang. Born 1875. A Graduate of a University in Japan, and was appointed to accompany the Commissioners to foreign countries to study political affairs. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan, and Chairman of the Committee of the Bank of Communications. Was a Teacher in the Government University in Peking, 1902; Chief Secretary in Chungweimen Octroi, 1903; studied politics in foreign countries, 1904; a member of the Tzu Cheng Yuan, 1909; Vice-President of the Board of Finance, 1911. Chinputang. Author of The Currency, etc. Minister plenipotentiary to Japan, 1915. Manager-in-Chief of the Currency System Bureau. Was forced to retire from official life by strong public sentiment against the so-called pro-Japanese officia's in May, 1919. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Yung-hsiang, (盧永祥)** —Shantung. Born 1867. Educated in the Military School, Shanhaikwan. Military Commissioner of Shanghai and Woosung, January 6, 1917. Tuchun of Chekiang, August 16, 1919. Abolished his Tuchunship himself and became Director of Military Affairs of Chekiang, June 1922. First Order of Merit. Tashou decoration. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lu Yung-ting, (陸榮廷)** —Kwangsi. Born 1856. At one time a leader of bandits, but reclaimed and took military service under Government. Commander of an expedition under Viceroy Tsen Chun-hsuan against bandits in Kwangsi. Formerly a Brigade-General of the troops on the left bank of the West River. Tutuh of Kwangsi, 1914, afterwards Chiangchun. In the Civil War of 1917-18 joined the South and became Minister of War in the Canton Government. Inspecting General of Liang Kwang. Was driven out of Kwangsi by Chen Chiung-ming, 1921. Was appointed High Defence Commissioner for Kwangsi Frontier Nov., 1922. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**Lung Chi-kuang, (龍濟光)** —Yunnan. Tso-Chiang Tao, Kwangsi, January, 1908. Commander-in-Chief, Kwangsi, March, 1909. Pacificator, Kwangtung, July 26, 1913. Tutuh and Acting Chief Civil Administrator, Kwangtung, August 3, 1913. Additionally to Command the Water Police, and Ships of War on the West River and coasts of Kwangtung, August 22, 1913. Chiangchun, Kwangtung, 1914. Field-Marshal. Created Duke of the First Order, December, 1915. Created Prince of the second degree for services in Yunnan, January, 1916. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))
