**Lung Chien-chang, (龍建章)** —Kwangtung. Born 1872. Councillor, Ministry of Communications, May, 1912. Civil Governor of Kweichow, 1914; cashiered January, 1916. Minister of Communications, 1917. — p. 840 ([scan leaf 913](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n913/mode/1up))

**LUNG MIN-HSIU, (龍敏修)** —Honan. Born 1877. Graduated from a three year course in the Police Officers' Training School, Tientsin. Commissioner of Police, Honan, since Nov. 1918. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**LYNN, LUN-MIN T. S** see Lin Tung-shih. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**LYNN TONG-SIH** see Lin Tung-shih. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA AN-LIANG, (馬安良)** —Kansu. Born 1853. Mohammedan. Brigade-General, Ili, October, 1903. Commander-in-Chief, Kansu, April, 1912. Second class Wenhu and Chiaho decorations. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA CHEN-PIN, (馬振[illegible])** —Taoyin of the Liukiang Circuit in Kwangsi, 1915. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA CHIN-HSU, (馬金敍)** —Anhui. Formerly Brigade-General in several places. Commander-in-Chief, Kansu, February, 1912. Commander-in-Chief, Chihli, April, 1912. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA CHUN, (馬駿)** —Shansi. Born February 4, 1882. Studied at University of Shansi, M.A., and Imperial College, London. Inspecting Commissioner for Hotung, 1913. Supervisor General, Eighth Mining District, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, 1914. Member of Parliament. Hotung Taoyin, Shansi, since May 22, 1918. Was member of Mintang; now non-partisan. Third class Chiaho and Fifth Wenhu decorations. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA FU-HSIANG, (馬福祥)** —Kansu. Brigade-General. Palikun, New Dominion, March, 1909. Acting Chief Executive Officer, Kokonor, July-August, 1912. Commander of Guards Division, Altai, October 10, 1912. Was Military Commissioner of Ninghsia, Kansu. Appointed Tartar General of Suiyuan, 1921. First Class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA HSIAO-CHIN, (馬小進)** —Kwangtung. Born 1887. Studied in the following schools: School of Political Science, Canton: St. Stephen's College, Hongkong; Columbia University, U.S.A., and New York University, U.S.A. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was an officer of the Southern Branch of Tung Meng Hui eight years ago. Was editor of several papers and periodicals, and has been Peking correspondent to several papers and periodicals. Kuomintang. Author of The Diary of a Travel, The Literature of the World, The Diary of Shiaocheng, The Latest General Outline of Finances, Liberty of the Citizens, and The Suffragists. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA, K. S** see Ma Kuo-chi. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA KUO-CHI, (K. S. Ma), (馬國贊)** —Kiangsu. Graduate, St. John's University, Shanghai; and Harvard University and Columbia University, U.S.A. Dean, Wuchang Teachers' College, 1917. Professor, Tsing Hua College, 1917-20. Member, Secretaries' Office of the President. Member, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA LIANG, (良馬)** —Chihli. Born 1876. Lieutenant-General, Defence Commissioner of Tsinan, Shantung, and Commander of the 2nd Division of the Frontier Defence Troops, till 1920. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA LIANG, (馬良)** —Kiangsu. Born 1840. Acting President of Peking Government University, October 18, 1912; resigned, December 27, 1912. Appointed one of President's nominees on Political Council, November, 1915. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA LIEN-CHIA, (馬聯甲)** —Lt.-General in the Army. Commanding General of the first Anhui Army Division and Defence Commissioner of Southern Anhui. Appointed Assistant to the Tuchun of Anhui on the removal of Ni Ssu Chung, 1920. Was ordered to attend to the Military affairs in Anhui upon the abolishment of the Tuchun in that province, Oct. 7, 1922. — p. 841 ([scan leaf 914](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n914/mode/1up))

**MA LIN-YI, (馬鄰翼)** —Honan. Born 1874. Chujen. Was interested in education in his native province, having been the Head of Paoching (Honan) Educational Association, Superintendent of Honan Middle School, of Railway School, etc. Later he joined the Board of Education as its Councillor. Member of Commission for Preparation of Constitutional Government. Was sent to Kansu as Commissioner of Education for that Province. There he became successively Commissioner of Industries, of Finance, Taoyin of P'inglang, etc. Was Commissioner of Education for Anhui till 1920 when he became the Commissioner of Education for Chihli. Promoted to Vice-Minister of Education, May, 1921. Resigned, Dec. 27, 1921. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MA LUNG-PIAO, (馬龍標)** —Shantung. Lieutenant-General, Mongol Red Banner, January, 1911. Commander of 5th Division, Assistant Commissioner for Defence of Shantung, March, 1912. Inspector General of Peking December, 1917. Third Order of Merit. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MA TSUN-FA, (馬存發)** —Yunnan. Military Governor of the Chaochow Circuit in Kwangtung, 1915.—Lieut. General in the Army. Brigade General Kwangtung First Mixed Brigade. Age 57. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MA YIN-CH'U, (馬寅初)** —Chekiang. Born 1884. Studied in Peiyang University, Graduated from Yale University, U.S.A. 1910. M.A. Graduated from Columbia University, 1914. Ph.D. Professor, Peking Government University. Became its Dean. Dean of Commercial College, Shanghai. Advisor to the Chekiang Hsing Yeh Bank. Special member, Bank of China. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MAI YING-CHUN, (Y. D. Mark), (麥英俊)** —Kwangtung. Born Aug. 21, 1892. Graduated in Law from Purdue University (U.S.A.) 1917 and in Commerce from University of Chicago, 1919. B.A. Deputy Chief, Bureau of Foreign Affairs, Kwangtung, from Aug. 1920 to May 1921. Appointed Superintendent of Wuchow Customs and concurrently Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Kwangsi. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MAO CHI-CHENG** —Councillor, Ministry of War, December 12, 1912. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MARK, Y. D** see Mai Ying-chun. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MEI HUA-CHUEN, (梅華銓)** —Born in U.S.A., 1883. Studied in Kwangtung, 1890-95; Hongkong, 1895-96; San Francisco, 1896-98; and New York, 1898-1904. College of the City of New York, 1904-08; and Columbia University, 1908-11. B.Sc. LL.B. M.A. Phi Beta Kappa. Assistant Manager, Chinese Students' Monthly, 1909-11. Graduate law course, New York University, 1913-14, Juris Doctor, June, 1914. Law clerkship in Wall Street firm, 1914-15. Court interpreter, New York Courts, 1910-15. Writer on Chinese affairs, New York journals, 1909-15. English Secretary, Tung Meng Hui, New York branch, 1911-15. Secretary and Director, The China Society of America, Inc., New York, 1912-15. Admitted to bar of New York Supreme Court, also bars of United States District Court, and United States Circuit Court of Appeals, 1915. Returned to China, Sept. 1915. Secretary, National Committee, Y.M.C.A.'s of China, and also Editor-in-chief, China's Young Men, 1915-16. Instructor in a law school, Shanghai, 1915 to date. Office Manager and editor, Intelligence Bureau, Republican Government of China, 1916. Admitted to Shanghai American Bar and Mixed Court Bar, 1916. Official Reporter, United States Court for China, 1916-17. Director, Chinese Y.M.C.A., 1917 to date, its Vice-Chairman, 1918-19. Practising attorney and Counsel-at-law, Shanghai, 1917 to date. Legal Adviser to Commissioner of Foreign Affairs, Shanghai, 1917-18; to Administration of Sequestered German and Austrian Vessels, Shanghai, 1918. Trustee, Cantonese Union Church, Shanghai, 1918-20. Director, Yangtsepoo Social Centre, 1918 to date. Secretary, Columbia Alumni Association of China, 1918-20, Chairman, 1920-22. Executive Committeeman, American University Club, 1917-18. Committeeman, Society for Constructive Endeavour, Shanghai, 1918. One of organizers and directors of International Anti-Opium Association, 1919-22. Director, Intelligence Bureau, Southern Peace Delegation, Shanghai, 1919. Member: Far Eastern American Bar Association, American Returned Students' Club, American University Club. Adviser, China Bureau of Public Information, Shanghai, 1919 to date. Author of various articles published in magazines in China and abroad. — p. 842 ([scan leaf 915](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n915/mode/1up))

**MEI KUANG-HSI, (梅光羲)** .—Kiangsi. Born 1878. Graduate of a University in Japan, and Chujen of the late Ching Dynasty. Started various colleges at Hupeh; introduced the system of Procuratorate and Prison Reform in Hupeh and Kwangtung; started the Library in the Government University at Peking; and established the Society for the Investigation of Buddhism at Hupeh and the Buddhist Society at Peking. Is the author of Outlines of Buddhist Doctrines. Chief of the Department of Navigation of the Ministry of Communications, 1914. Member, Civil Court of Equity. Superintendent of Telegraphs at Tsinan. Departmental Chief, Bureau of Mongolian and Tibetan Affairs. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**MENG EN-YUAN, (孟恩遠)** .—Chihli. Born 1855. Military Governor of Kirin, 1915. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**MI FENG-CHI, (米逢吉)** .—Chihli. Born 1882. After attending school at home, entered a university in Japan in 1904, studied Political Economy, graduating in 1911. Started a Model Farm in his native district before going to Japan. Was interested in local government and public welfare affairs since his return to China. Elected member Chihli Provincial Assembly in 1912, and became its Vice-Chairman in 1921. During 1913-1916, Mi promoted an Agricultural Colonization Society, known as the Ping Feng Shih, with a capital of $40,000 and 20,000 mu of land; this Society introduced agriculture in Suiyuan and West Mongolia. Organized the Hopei Daily News, 1919, and became its Editor. Author of various articles published in the News. Kuomintang. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**MIAO CHING-KUEI, (苗經魁)** .—Chihli. Born December 14, 1859. Vice-Chairman, Kirin Chamber of Commerce since 1913. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**MO YUNG-HSIN, (莫榮新)** .—Kwangsi. Promoted from a common soldier. Defence Commissioner in Kwangsi and Kwangtung. With the support of General Lu Yung-ting, Mo was made Tuchun of Kwangtung. Was driven back to Kwangsi by General Chen Chiung-ming in 1920. Third Order of Merit. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**MOU CHI-CHENG** .—Shantung. Born 1879. Graduate of Peiyang Military School; after having graduated in a Japanese college, he again graduated in the Japanese Institute for Military Officers. Major-General, November, 1st year of the Chinese Republic, and appointed Councillor of the Ministry of War the same month. After returning from Japan at the end of the reign of Kwang Hsu he was made Tutor and Director of several Military Schools; was Brigadier of the Mixed Brigade of Shensi, 2nd year of Hsuan Tung; was the Chief Councillor for military affairs in Shensi; and Councillor for Military Affairs in the President's office. A non-partisan. Translated several military works. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**MOU LIN, (牟琳)** .—Kweichow. Born 1877. Graduate of a Normal School in Japan, and a Chujen of the late Dynasty. In the 1st year of Hsuan Tung he was elected member of the Tzu Yi Chu of Kweichow, and a member of Tzu Cheng Yuan. Established more than 100 primary schools. Chinputang. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**MU CHAO-JEN** .—Shantung. Born 1879. Graduate of the Normal School in Shantung. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Has hitherto been engaged in educational works for more than ten years. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 843 ([scan leaf 916](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n916/mode/1up))

**Mu Ou-ch'u, (穆藕初)** —Kiangsu. Born 1876. American educated. Studied in the University of Wisconsin and in an agricultural school in Texas, U.S.A. M.A. Manager of two leading spinning mills in Shanghai and of one in Chenghsien, Honan. Director, Shanghai Chamber of Commerce and of Industrial Bank. President, Committee on Cotton Culture of the Spinning Millers' Guild and of Cotton Exchange, Shanghai. Author of the books on industry. Adviser to the President and to the Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**Na Tung, (那桐)** —Manchu. Sub-Chancellor, Grand Secretariat, December, 1899. Minister, Tsung-li Yamen, June, 1900. Junior Vice-President, Board of Revenue, November, 1900. Special Embassy to Japan, July, 1901, when he ceased to be Minister of Tsung-li Yamen. Minister, Board of Foreign Affairs, February, 1902. President, Board of Revenue, June, 1903. President, Board of Foreign Affairs, and member of Finance Committee, November, 1903. Grand Secretary, January, 1906. Member Revenue Council, January, 1918. Inspector-General of Troops, November, 1908. Junior Guardian of Heir-Apparent, December, 1908. Grand Councillor, January, 1909. Acting Viceroy of Chihli, June, 1909. Appointed Vice-Premier on the formation of Prince Ching's Cabinet in May, 1911. Relieved of office, and made Vice-President of the Privy Council on November 1, 1911. Retired into private life during the latter stages of the Revolution, and lived at Tsingtao. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**Na Yen-t'u (Prince No), (那彥圖)** —Mongol. A Prince of the First Order, and Jassak of the Khalka Mongols. Superintendent of Customs at Peking, September, 1896. General Chamberlain, November, 1903. Lieutenant-General of Bordered Yellow Banner, 1903. One of the Mongolian members of the National Council and of the Kung-ho-tang, and Tartar General of Tarbagatai, 1914. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**Nan Kuei-hsing, (南桂馨)** —Shansi. Born 1883. After graduation from the Imperial Shansi University, Nan went to Japan for further education, graduating from a Police Officers' Training School there. Sian Superintendent of Police and Commissioner of Police for Shansi since June, 1917. Second class Wenhu and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**Nan Yao-chun, (南岳嶻)** —Shensi. Born 1883. Graduated from a law school in Peking and in political science from a Japanese University. Was Intendant of Police in his own province before he was elected Chairman of Shensi Provincial Assembly. 4th class Chiaho decoration. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**Nemokawangton** —Mongol. Duke of the 2nd Order. Prince of the 3rd Order, February, 1916. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**Ni Ssu-ch'ung, (倪嗣沖)** —Anhui. Born 1871. Acting Commissioner for Internal Affairs, Heilungkiang, August, 1908; substantive, April, 1909; degraded, December, 1909. Provincial Treasurer, Honan, December, 1911; relieved, July 21, 1912. Appointed to reorganize Honan Frontier Affairs, July 5, 1912. Occupation Commissioner, Huanpei (Anhui Northern Circuit), July 22, 1913; additionally, Huanpei, Intendant, July 22, 1913. Tutuh of Anhui, and additionally Acting Civil Administrator, July 27, 1913. Field-Marshal, 1915. Created Duke of the First Order, December, 1915. Remained in Anhui as Tuchun; resigned October, 1920. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**Nieh Hsien-fan, (聶憲藩)** —Anhui. Educated in Japan. Was Defence Commissioner of Chefoo. Civil Governor of Anhui. Appointed Commander of the Peking Gendarmerie, May 28, 1922. Chiangchung, Oct. 1922. First class Wenhu decoration. — p. 844 ([scan leaf 917](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n917/mode/1up))

**NIU CH'UAN-SHAN, (鈕傳善)** .—Born 1877. Was a magistrate and later a Taotai in Ching Dynasty. Acting Civil Governor for Shensi, 1914. Director of the Sale of Tobacco and Wines, 1915-1916. Appointed Vice-Minister of Finance, June 1, 1921. Resigned, Nov. 6, 1921. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**NIU YUNG-CHIEN, (鈕永建)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1873. Studied in Hupeh Military Academy and in Japan and Germany. Assistant Chief of General Staff, Nanking Military Government. Was Military representative stationed at Shanghai. Councillor to President. April 19, 1912. Joined the rebellion of 1913 and outlawed. Visited America 1914. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**OU-YANG CHEN-SHENG, (歐陽振聲)** .—Hunan. Born 1882. Studied in the Wen Pu Tung School Hupeh and graduated in Political Economy in the Waseda University, Japan. Delegate from Hunan in the Provisional Parliament of Nanking, 1911. Member of Senate in the Provisional Parliament of 1912. Member of House of Representatives, 1913. Secretary, Military Government in Canton, August 23, 1918. Vice-Minister of the Canton Military Government, since May 27, 1920. Author-compiler of History of Chinese Philosophy (in printing). Member of Kuo Min Tang, 1912; of Cheng Hsueh Hui, 1916. Third class Chiho decoration. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**OU-YANG CH'I** —Kiangsi. Born 1878. Studied in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Director and Engineer of the Yukan Coal Mine in Kiangsi, and was an Assistant of the War Office at Kiukiang during the time of the Revolution. Kuomintang. Author of Essays on Kungyang, Kuliang, and the Book of Rites, A Collection of Poems, Old Chinese Mining Methods and the Implements used, a few novels, and other works. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**OU-YANG P'U-TSUN, (歐陽溥存)** .—Kiangsi. Graduated in political science from a Japanese University. Was sometime member of Ministry of Interior and is a Taoyin in Kansu. Author of two Chinese dictionaries published in Shanghai. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**OU-YANG WU, (歐陽武)** .—Kiangsi. Born 1878. Defence Commissioner, Kiukiang. Dismissed and degraded, July 31, 1913. Brought to Peking for trial, and sentenced to eight years imprisonment. Specially pardoned by the President. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**P'A LO-TA, (帕勒塔)** .—Sinkiang. A Mongolian prince. Studied in Japan. General Administrator of Altai, May 1912. Member of Senate, 1917. Member of Chiangchunfu. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**P'AN CHU-YING, ([illegible])** .—Shantung. Born 1877. Graduated from an Army Officers' Training School in Japan. Member of Chiangchunfu. Brigadier-General in the army. Was at different times, Director of Aeronautic Bureau. Chiangchun of Suiyuan. Deputy Tutung of Kwei-hua, Tutung of Suiyuan. Military Adviser to the President, to the Inspecting-Generals of Chihli, Shantung and Honan; and of Manchuria. Commander of the 20th Army Division. Second class Chiaho, Wenhu and Tashou Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**PAN FU, (潘復)** .—Shantung. Born 1871. Chujen. Industrial Taotai of his own province, 1911. Appointed Chief of Industrial Bureau, 1913. Organized Lufeng Spinning Mill in Tsinan, Autumn, 1915. Shortly afterwards, he was appointed Associate Director of National Conservancy Bureau. In December, 1918, he was appointed Associate Director, Grand Canal Improvement Board. Vice-Minister of Finance, December 1919. Concurrently Director of National Salt Administration. Resigned Nov. 5, 1921. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**PAN HSUEH-HAI, (潘學海)** .—Kiangsi. Born 1876. A Graduate of a University in Japan. Chief of the Kiangsi Bureau of Legislature for one year, after the Revolution, and member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Non-partisan. Translated into Chinese a Japanese book on The Constitution of the Japanese Empire. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 845 ([scan leaf 918](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n918/mode/1up))

**PAN LING-KAO, (潘齡皋)** —Chihli. Hanlin of Ching Dynasty, Magistrate in Kansu. Held various positions in Shensi and Kansu. Commissioner of Finance, Kansu. Taoyin and Customs Superintendent in Kansu. Appointed Civil Governor of Kansu, Oct. 6, 1921. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**P'AN TSU-YI, (潘祖彝)** —Fukien. Born 1882. Graduate of Fukien High School, and a returned student from Japan. In 1912, editor of the Chang Pao in Fukien, but the paper was closed by force by Pan Shiao-sung. In the same year was elected member of the Fukien Assembly, and a member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Is connected with several commercial enterprises, such as tea, paper, silk, and medicine. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**P'AN WAN-CH'AO, (潘萬超)** —Heilungkiang. Graduate of middle school in his native province. Is now Vice-Chairman of Heilungkiang Provincial Assembly. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**PAO KWEI-CHING, (鮑貴卿)** —Fengtien. General. Graduated from the Kai Ping Military Academy. Appointed Defence Commissioner of Tatung, Anhui, 1913. Director, National Military Training Institution. Appointed Military Governor of Heilungkiang, July, 1917, and concurrently appointed to act as Civil Governor. Military Governor of Kirin, 1919. Director-General Chinese Eastern Railway. Appointed Minister of War, Dec. 25, 1921. Resigned, June 11, 1922. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**PEI CHI-HSUN, (裴其勳)** —Honan. Graduate of Peiyang Military Academy. Military tutor of Shantung Army. Defence Commissioner of Kirin and Chang-hun, 1915. General First Mixed Brigade. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**P'ENG CHAO-HUANG, (彭兆璜)** —Honan. Born May, 1873. For sometime studied in the normal school in Honan. Graduated from a Law College in Tokio, Japan. Chairman, Honan Provincial Assembly. Formerly of Kuomintang. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**P'ENG KE-HO, (彭兆荷)** —Kweichow. Born Oct. 1893. Studied in the Wu Cheng Middle School (Peking) and graduated from a school in Japan. Vice-President (acting for the President), Kweichow Provincial Educational Association. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**PENG KUANG-LIEH, (彭光烈)** —Szechuan. General of 2nd and 3rd Szechuan Army Divisions. Defence Commissioner of Chaokuang in Szechuan, July 1914. Military Councillor at the Chiangchünfu. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**P'ENG T'EO-SHENG** —General. In command of troops on the Tibetan frontier, 1910-18. Besieged by Tibetans in Chamdo for three months; capitulated April, 1918. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**PENG YUNG-I, (彭允彝)** —Appointed Acting Minister of Education Dec. 1922. Appointment confirmed Jan. 1923. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**Pi KUEI-FANG, (畢桂芳)** —Peking. Born 1865. Studied Russian Language in Peking. Councillor at Tarbagatai, May, 1912. Tuchun and Civil Governor of Heilungkiang, 1916. Tutuh and Chief Civil Administrator, Heilungkiang, July 16, 1913. Promoted full General of Manchu Banner Forces, November 28, 1913. High Military Adviser to the President. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**PI WEI, (畢煒)** —Anhui. Japan-educated. Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Anhui, April 15, 1913. Member, Ministry of Communications. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**PIEN FU-SUN. (F. S. Bien), (卞福孫)** —Kiangsu. Born 1887. Studied in Brown University, U.S.A. Judge, Peking Local Court, 1909-11. Manager, Bank of China, Antung Branch, Sept. 1917. Transferred to Pengpu, Jan. 1919. Sub-Manager, Mukden Branch, Oct. 1919. — p. 846 ([scan leaf 919](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n919/mode/1up))

**PIEN SHOU-CHING, (邊守靖)** .—Chihli. Born 1884. A graduate of the Law School of Tokio College, Japan. Teacher of Police and law schools in Paotingfu. In 1910 Pien helped in organizing the Bureau of Judicial Affairs in Chekiang. Appointed Chief of the Bureau of Administrative Affairs in Chekiang. Was elected Vice-Speaker of the Provincial Assembly of Chihli in July, 1912, and later became Speaker till present day. Mr. Pien is also an Adviser to the Police Administration of Chihli, and Director-General of the Ling-Ching Mining Corporation, Tientsin. Promoted and was elected Chairman of the Anglo-American-Chinese Commercial Club, Tientsin. Manager, Ho Pei Jih Pao, Tientsin. — p. 847 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n920/mode/1up))

**PIEN SHOU-SUN, (Z. S. Bien), (卞壽孫)** .—Kiangsu. Born in Hupeh 1884. Studied in the Chentan College, Shanghai and Brown University, (U.S.A.) Ph.B. (in Economics) Member, Organization Commission, Bank of China and Secretary, 1912. Assistant, Department of Note Issue, 1913. Associate Chief Auditor, 1914. Chief Auditor, Aug. 1914. Organized the Chung Foo Union Bank, 1916, Auditor and Manager of its Tientsin Office, 1914. Assistant Manager Bank of China, Tientsin Office, 1918, Manager, 1919 to 1922. Transferred to Peking, 1922. Author of Education as a Means of Social Progress. Fifth class Chiaho decoration. — p. 847 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n920/mode/1up))

**PIEN YIN-CH'ANG. (alias Pien Yueh T'ing), (卞陰昌字月庭)** .—Chihli. Born 1870. During the Manchu regime, Mr. Pien was a Senior Secretary of the Board of Works and of Board of Justice. Was elected Associate Director, Tientsin General Chamber of Commerce. When the Tientsin Merchants' Volunteer Corps was organized, he became its Commanding Officer. Member of Tientsin Red Cross Society. In 1913, Mr. Pien was elected Chairman of the Executive Committee of the United Chamber of Commerce of Chihli. In the same year, he was appointed President of Panama Exhibition Participation Committee. Counsellor to the former President Feng Kuo-chang, who was then Tutuh of Chihli, September, 1913. Adviser to the Civil Governor of Chihli, 1914. Became the President of the Association for the Protection of China's Sovereignty and Territory during the Lao Hsi Kai dispute. Was commissioned by the Minister of Finance to investigate taxation conditions in Chihli in connection with tariff revision, 1917. Was elected member of Parliament, August, 1918. President, Tientsin General Chamber of Commerce, November, 1919 up to date. President National Associated Chamber of Commerce. — p. 847 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n920/mode/1up))

**PIEN YUEH-TING** , see Pien Yin-chang. — p. 847 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n920/mode/1up))

**PING K'E-CHUAN, (邴克莊)** .—Fengtien. Born 1885. Studied police administration in Fengtien during Ching dynasty. Elected member of Fengtien Provincial Assembly, 1912. Become Member of Parliament Dec. 1912. Appointed Political Advisor to the Governor of Fengtien, 1914. Reelected member of Parliament 1918. Appointed Taoyin in Kirin, 1920. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 847 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n920/mode/1up))

**Po CH'ANG-CHIEH, (柏常潔)** .—Shensi. Born 1870. Graduate of a University in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chinputang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 847 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n920/mode/1up))

**Po WEN-WEI, (柏文蔚)** .—Anhui. Educated in the Military Academy, Wuchang. Commander of Battalion of 9th Division at Chinkiang, 1911. After the fall of Nanking took an expeditionary force to Pukow and defeated the Imperialists. Tutuh of Anhui, July, 1912. Given rank of Chung Chiang (Lieutenant-General) in October, 1912. Frontier Commissioner of Shensi and Kansu, June 30, 1913. Deprived of all ranks for participation in the rebellion, July 27, 1913. High Military Adviser to the President. Member of Chiangchunfu. — p. 847 ([scan leaf 920](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n920/mode/1up))

**PU-ER-KO-T'E** —Outer Mongolia. Born 1869. Visited Japan in March of 28th year of Kwang Hsu. Educated in Chinese and Mongolian literature. A Mongolian Meilin, representing the tribe of Dzasaktu of Outer Mongolia. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. Chinputang. — p. 848 ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n921/mode/1up))

**P'U JUN** —Manchu. Vice-President of Board of Works in October, 1903. Assistant Military Governor, Kobdo, Boy, 1908. Vacated office and came to Peking for service, September, 1911. Military Governor of Uliassutai, May, 1912. — p. 848 ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n921/mode/1up))

**P'U LUN (Prince), (溥倫)** —Manchu. Great-grandson of the Emperor Tao Kwang, who died in 1850. Prince of the Fourth Order. Vice-President of Imperial Clan Court, September, 1900. Lieutenant-General, Bordered Yellow Banner, February, 1901. Imperial Commissioner, St. Louis Exposition, 1904. Superintendent, Peking Octroi, September, 1907. President of the National Assembly during period of organization and its first session (September, 1907, to January 11, 1911). Appointed Minister of Agriculture, Commerce, and Industry, spring, 1911. Removed from office during the Revolution. Among Chinese he is the most popular member of the Imperial Clan. He was one of the few Princes who did not flee from Peking on the inauguration of the Republic. Represents Imperial Family at most Republican functions. — p. 848 ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n921/mode/1up))

**P'U YI (溥儀) (HSUAN TUNG), Ex-Emperor** —Son of Prince Ch'un (Tsai Li) and nephew of the Emperor Kwang Hsu. Born on February 11, 1906. Succeeded to the Throne, under the Regency of his father, on November 14, 1908, and adopted the reign-title of Hsuan Tung. Abdicated on February 12, 1912. His mother was the daughter of the late Jung Lu. Under the Republic the ex-Emperor continued his studies under his old tutors and also received instruction from Mr. R.F. Johnston, C.B.E. On June 30, 1917, Chang Hsun carried out a monarchical coup in the interests of the Manchu Dynasty. Hsuan Tung ascended the throne, but the "monarchy" survived little more than a week and once again his name was affixed to an "abdication." Cut off his queue in May 1922. Married in December 1922. — p. 848 ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n921/mode/1up))

**SA CHEN-PING, (薩鎮冰)** —Fukien. Born March 30, 1859. Brigade-General. Educated in Naval School, Foochow, and Royal Naval College, Greenwich, England, K.C.M.G. Admiral Commanding Peiyang Squadron, 1903. Admiral, Kwangtung. August, 1905. Commander-in-Chief, land and sea, Kwangtung, November, 1906. Land command withdrawn, January, 1908. Commissioner for Naval Reorganization, June, 1909. Vacated office, July, 1909. Admiral of fleet, July, 1909. Accompanied Prince Tsai Hsun on naval mission to Europe, Oct. 1909; and to Japan and America, August, 1910. In command of Imperial naval forces during Revolution. Took a naval force up to Hankow but owing to lack of supplies and continual defections among his crews was forced to vacate his command and escaped to North China. Director General of Land and River Police, Shanghai and Woosung, August 14, 1913. High Inspector of Kwangtung and Fukien, 1917. Appointed Minister of Navy, December 4, 1919. Sent on special mission to pacify Fukien, and appointed Civil Governor for Fukien, Oct. 15, 1922. First class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 848 ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n921/mode/1up))

**SAO-KE ALFRED SZE** see Shih Chao Chi. — p. 848 ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n921/mode/1up))

**SHANG CHEN-FUEI, (尚鎮圭)** —Shensi. Born 1874. Graduate of a Technical School in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was Teacher at a Middle School in Tungchow, and Chairman of the Educational Society of Taleihsien, and was the Representative of Tutuh Chang in Shanghai and Hankow during the Revolution. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 848 ([scan leaf 921](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n921/mode/1up))

**SHAO CHANG, (邵章)** Chekiang. Hanlin. Judge, Administrative Court and is now (Dec. 1922) in charge of the Court. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHAO CH'ANG-JUNG, (邵長鎔)** Kiangsu. Born 1863. Member of Tzu-yiehu. Kiangsu, Industrial Secretary in the Tutuh's Yamen. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chinputang. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN CHI-CH'ANG, (沈其昌)** Chekiang. Born 1881. Educated in Japan. President of the High Court of Justice in Kwangtung, 1915, of Shantung, Feb. 1917. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN CHIA-YI, (沈家彜)** Kiangsu. Born 1882. President of the High Court of Justice in Fengtien, 1915. Presiding Judge, Supreme Court (Civil Division). — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN CHIN-CHIEN, (沈金鑑)** Chekiang. Civil Governor of Chekiang. Was summoned to Peking by President Li Yuan-hung, Oct., 1922. First Class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. Third Order of Merit. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN CHUN-JU, (沈鈞儒)** Chekiang. Secretary of Ministry of Finance in the Ching Dynasty. Commissioner of Education, Chekiang, August, 1912. Member of Ministry of Civil Affairs. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN JUI-LIN. (沈瑞麟)** Chekiang. Attached to Waiwupu, 1908. Minister at Vienna, August, 1910. Appointed Vice-Minister of Foreign Affairs, Jan. 5, 1922. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN PAO-CHANG, (沈寶昌)** Chekiang. Chujen. Procurator, Peking during 1912. Secretary of Ministry of Justice. Was magistrate of Shanghai, 1915. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN TSENG-YUEH** Acting Superintendent of Bank of Kwangsi. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHEN YUN-P'EI, (沈雲沛)** Kiangsu. Senior Secretary, Board of Commerce, January, 1907. Junior Vice-President do., March, 1908. Assistant Director, Tientsin-Pukow Railway, July, 1909. Acting President, Board of Communications, August 10. Acting Junior Vice-President, Board of Civil Appointments, January, 1911. Vacated office June, 1911, but retained post of Assistant Manager, Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Advisory Minister, Privy Council, August, 1911. Manager of Pukow-Hsinyang Railway, January 13, 1913. Was actively interested in Yuan Shih-kai's monarchical movement. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHIH CHAO-CH'ANG** Chekiang. Born 1872. Chujen. Secretary to the Chinese Legation at St. Petersburg, 28th year of Kwang Hsu. Chinese Secretary to the Second Peace Conference at The Hague. First Secretary in the Chinese Legation at The Hague, and also First Secretary of the Chinese Legation in Rome. In the 1st year of Hsuan Tung he acted as Minister to Germany, and returned to China in the 2nd year. Is now Departmentai Chief of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Author of Comment on the Sino-Russian Treaty. Chinese Consul-General in the Philippines, 1915. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHIH CHAO-CHI, (SAO-KE ALFRED SZE), (施肇基)** Chekiang. Educated in America, at Cornell University. Acting Junior Secretary, Board of Communications, December, 1906. Removed from office, January, 1907. For some time Managing Director of the Luhan Railway. A Director of the Northern Railways, 1908. Customs Taotai, Harbin, spring, 1908. Acting Commissioner for Foreign Affairs, Kirin, July, 1910. Junior Councillor, Board of Foreign Affairs August, 1910; Senior Councillor, August, 1911. Imperial Commissioner to the International Plague Conference at Mukden, April, 1911. Nominated Chinese Minister to America, Peru, Mexico, and Cuba just after the outbreak of the Revolutoin, but did not proceed. Minister of Posts and Communications, and for a time Acting Minister of Finance in the first Republican Cabinet. Resigned on account of ill-health in June. Is related, by marriage, to Tang Shao-yi. Appointed Officer of Ceremonies at the President's Office, December 27, 1913. Minister to Great Britain, June 20, 1914. Minister to Washington, 1921. Chinese delegate to the Washington Conference, Nov. 1921. Visited China, Nov. 1922. Nomination as Minister of Foreign Affairs rejected by Senate, Jan. 1923. Second Order of Merit. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 849 ([scan leaf 922](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n922/mode/1up))

**SHIH CHAO-TSENG, (施肇曾)** Chekiang. Born 1868. Attaché to Chinese Legation, Washington, 1893-95. Consul, New York, 1896-97. Commissioner of the Shanghai-Nanking Railway, 1905-7. Assistant Director of the Peking-Hankow Railway, 1910-11. Director-General of the Lunghai Railways and Associate Director-General of the Tatung-Chengtu Railway, 1913-22. Second class Paokuang Chiaho and Second class Wenhu Decorations. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHIH KUO-CHEN, (施國楨)** Kansu. Born September 25, 1886. Studied in the Provincial College (Kansu) and the Government Law School (Peking). Principal, Public Law School of Kansu. 1918 (?). Vice-Chairman, Provincial Educational Association of Kansu since June 1920. Formerly of Kuomintang. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHIH LU-PEN, (施履本)** Hupeh. Graduated from a Japanese University. Member of Ministry of Foreign Affairs since the Republic, became Special Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Shantung in August, 1919. Before proceeding to the present post, was on various occasions connected with the President's office. Author of a Japanese word book. Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho, Tashou Chiaho and First class Tashou decorations. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHIH PIN, Dr., (施斌)** Second Secretary, Chinese Legation, Washington. Summoned by Minister Yen of Foreign Affairs to serve in the Ministry, Aug. 1921. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHIH TSE-HSIEN, (史澤咸)** Shantung. Born 1884. Studied in a University in Japan. During Revolution was appointed Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs in the Tutuh's office, and then a member of the Advisory Council in Nanking. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan, and a member of the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Has formed a Society for the Cultivation and Colonization of the Frontiers, and has raised a capital of more than $500,000 to start colonization and cultivation in Kirin and other places. Non-partisan. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHIH TSO-LING** Fukien. Born 1865. Graduate of the former Naval School at Tientsin. Captain of the Navy, and Chief of the Naval Education Department. Non-partisan. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHIH YU, (施愚)** Szechuan. Chinshih. Educated in Europe. Councillor, Privy Council, August, 1911. Deputy Commissioner, Constitutional Department of Cabinet, February, 1912. Chief of Legislation Bureau and Chief of Bureau for Codification of Law, July, 1912. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHU HUNG-YI** Anhui. Born 1868. Studied in the Government University in Peking, and was appointed to proceed to Japan to investigate the police system. Has been Chief of Civil Affairs, Department of the Ministry of the Interior. Was Director of the Government Mint in Fengtien, and was appointed to start the Electric Light Company there. Chinputang. Author of Diary of the Investigation of the Police System in Japan, and The Yiyuan Poetry. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHU, S. S** see Hsü Shih-hsiang. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SHUI CHIH-HENG, (薛之珩)** Police Superintendent of Peking since 1921. Second Class Tashou Packwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SIA CHWANG-TING** see Hsieh Chuan T'ing. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**SING TSEN-SENG** Son of the Rev. Sing Eng-teh. Educated at the C.M.S. School and Trinity College, Ningpo, where he subsequently became a Master and Headmaster. Ordained Deacon, October 20, 1889. Pastor of Ningpo City, 1911. Archdeacon of the Diocese of Chekiang. Pastor at Hangchow, 1917. Consecrated as first Chinese Anglican Bishop (of the Chung Hua Sheng Kung Hui), October 2, 1918. — p. 850 ([scan leaf 923](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n923/mode/1up))

**Soong Tsung-faung** see Sung Chun-fang. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Su K'an-shih, (栗戡時)** —Hunan. Born 1879. Studied in Japan. Was Chief of the Department of Foreign Affairs, and Supervisor of Changsha and Yochow Customs. Resigned June, 1913, and since then he has been High Adviser to the Tutuh of Hunan. Was Vice-Speaker of Hunan Tzu Yi Chu. Non-partisan. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Su Wei, (栗威)** —Honan. Born February 6, 1876. Studied in Law in Japan. Superintendent of Changsha Customs and Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Hunan since June 21, 1920. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Su Yu-fang, (蘇毓芳)** —Fengtien. A Graduate of the Law School in Fengtien. A member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. In the 2nd year of Hsuan Tung he organized the Tung Tze Hui, with the object of demanding a Parliament: he gathered together more than 5,000 students, and by their earnestness, Viceroy Hsi Liang promised to memorialize the Throne for them. Was imprisoned 27 days for being suspected as revolutionist, when securing arms for Pao An Hui. Chinputang. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun, C. Y** see Sun Chung Ying. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun Chen-chia, (孫振家)** —Was a Taoyin in Hupeh. Recommended by Wang Chan-yuan to be Civil Governor of Hupeh, but was rejected by the people. Governor of the Metropolitan District. Co-Director of the Shantung Relief Bureau, May 19, 1922. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun Chi-ch'ang, (孫其昌)** —Fengtien. Born 1884. Graduated in 1911 from a Japanese normal school. Was for some time Commissioner of Education in Heilungkiang. Appointed Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for the same province, July 14, 1921. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun Ching, (孫儆)** —Kiangsu. Born 1867. Chujen. Was once magistrate in Szechuan. Vice-Chairman, Kiangsu Provincial Assembly. Author of Peimen Poems and Notes on Japan (in Chinese). Formerly of Kung-hotang. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun Ching-ch'ing, (孫鏡清)** —Szechuan. Born 1884. Studied in Japan, and a Graduate of the Law College in Peking. Was Magistrate of Hsiangcheng, Honan, was one of the promoters of the Shanghai Chung Kuo Kung Hsueh; and was teacher to several Law Schools. Is now a lawyer and a member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Kuomintang. Author of The Principles of the Constitution of the Ching Dynasty, etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun Chung-ying, (C. Y. Sun), (孫仲英)** —Kiangsu. Until recently, was interested in business. Now Sun resides in Tientsin and is identified with many forms of social service. Notably in the Anti-opium propaganda and famine relief work of 1920-21. Sun also promoted and successfully conducted the United War Work Compaign of 1918. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun Hung-yi, (孫洪伊)** —Tientsin. Born 1870. Was elected member of the House of Representatives and has been Minister of Interior. Second order of Merit. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sun Lieh-chen, (孫烈臣)** —Fengtien. Born 1871. Began his life as a common soldier. Held rank of Lieutenant General, in the Ching Dynasty. Was Commander of the Fengtien Patrol Force. Later he became Major General and Commander of the 54th Brigade of the 27th Division. Military Governor of Heilungkiang, June, 1920. Also Director-General, Chinese Eastern Railway. Transferred to be Tuchun of Kirin since spring, 1921. Second order of Merit. — p. 851 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**SUN PAO-CH'I, (孫寶琦)** —Hangchow, Chekiang. Born 1867. Yinsun. Member, Board of Justice and Taotai in Chihli. Minister to France, June, 1902. Chief Secretary, Government Council, summer, 1906. Governor of Peking, autumn, 1906. Minister to Germany, April, 1907 to December, 1908. Assistant Director, Tientsin-Pukow Railway, Sept. 1908. Returned to Peking, January, 1909. Acting Governor (June, 1909) and Governor (November, 1910) of Shantung. Shantung prematurely proclaimed its independence and Sun accepted the post of President of the Province. November 15, 1911. On November 29, two days after recapture of Hanyang, it returned to the Imperial fold, whereupon Sun tendered his resignation in a penitent memorial. Was pardoned by the Throne, but resigned in December, 1911. Director-General, Revenue Council, May 11, 1913. Minister of Foreign Affairs, September 11, 1913. Director Audit Office, 1915-1916. Minister of Finance, 1916. Director, Shui Wu Chu. Director, Government Famine Relief Bureau, June 17, 1922. First class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. Third Order of Merit. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN P'EI, (孫培)** .—Anhui. Born 1880. Studied in the Imperial University in Japan. A Councillor of Political Affairs to the President: a member of Committee in the Cabinet: a delegate for the Preparation of the National Assembly: and a Councillor of the Ministry of the Interior. Vice-Minister, 1922. A member of the Committee of the Chinputang. Author of the Spirit of the Constitution, etc. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN SHI-YI** —Consul-General in Cuba. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN SHIH-WEI, (孫世偉)** .—Chekiang. Born 1882. Graduated from a Japanese University. B.S. Secretary to Governor of Chekiang, 1912. Commissioner of Industries for Chekiang, Feb. 1913; of Interior, Sept. 1913. Appointed Taoyin of Ting-Chang Tao, Fukien, 1914; Administrative Commissioner for Shansi, Sept. 1914. Was Acting Civil Governor of Shansi, Oct. to Nov. 1916, when became a Taoyin in that province. Administrative Commissioner for Honan, since June 1917. Is a Commissioner of the China International Famine Relief Commission. Wrote a book on Japanese government finance. Member of Chinputang. Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho and Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN TAN-LIN. (孫丹林)** —Minister of Interior, May-Dec. 1922. First Class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. Second Order of Merit. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN TAO-JEN, (孫道仁)** .—Hunan. Formerly expectant Taotai of Fukien. Transferred to the military service, and appointed Brigade-General of the Foochow-Kienningfu military circuit, and Commander of the Ninth Division. Elected Tutuh of Fukien on the outbreak of the Revolution. July 12, 1912. Resigned December 5, 1913. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN TO-SEN, (孫多森)** .—Anhui. Born 1868. Intendant of Industry, Chihli, March, 1910; vacated, January, 1912. Industrial Commissioner, Anhui, May, 1912; vacated May, 1912. Manager, Bank of China, December 23, 1912. Chief Civil Administrator and additionally Acting Tutuh, Anhui, June 30, 1913. Relieved of both posts, July 27, 1913. Manager, Bank of China, June, 30—July 21, 1917. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN WEN, (Sun Yat-sen), (孫文)** .—Kwangtung. Born in 1866, the son of a farmer in Hsiangshan district. Learnt English at an early age, and studied under Dr. Kerr of the American Mission. Enrolled as a student of the Alice Memorial Hospital at Hongkong in 1887, whence he graduated as "Licentiate of Medicine and Surgery, Hongkong," in 1892. Started to practise in Macao, where he organised the Young China Party. Subsequently settled in Canton, where he became an active revolutionary. After the failure of a conspiracy at Canton in 1895 he fled to Macao, and thence proceeded to Hongkong, Japan, Honolulu and America, in all of which places he obtained adherents to the Reform Party. Arrived in England in 1896, and on October 11, of that year was kidnapped outside the Chinese Legation by orders of the Chinese Minister. It was intended to ship Dr. Sun to China as a lunatic, but he managed to make his plight known to Dr. Cantlie, who was instrumental in effecting his release after twelve days' imprisonment. Subsequently Dr. Sun toured through Europe, America and the East as a revolutionary propagandist. In Japan (with General Huang Hsing) he was instrumental in founding the Tung Meng Hui. Was in England when the Wuchang outbreak occurred, but came out to China at the end of 1911, and was elected Provisional President of the Republic by the Nanking Council. Resigned from the Presdency on the abdication of the Manchus, on the understanding that Yuan Shih-kai should be elected to succeed him, and proceeded on a tour to Wuchang and South China, where he advocated a socialistic policy. Came to Peking at the President's request in August, 1912, and was accorded an enthusiastic welcome. Advocated an extensive programme of railway construction, and on September 10. was appointed by the President "to consider and draft plans for a national system of railways," and to "submit and discuss the same with international financiers." Visited Kalgan, and on September 17, left for Taiyuanfu and Shanghai. Strongly advocated the transfer of the capital from Peking to Wuchang or Nanking. His authority as Chief of the National Railway Corporation was cancelled on the outbreak of the rebellion, and Dr. Sun subsequently took up his residence in Japan. Dr. Sun was in Shanghai in 1920, but proceeded to Canton in 1921 when the Kwangsi officials were ejected by General Chen Chiung-ming, and was "elected" "President" of China by the so-called Parliament there in April 1921. He was expelled from Canton by Chen Chiung-ming in the summer of 1922, and returned to Shanghai. Author of Elementary Treaties on People's right, Sun Wen Hsueh Shuo and Industrial Plans. — p. 852 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**SUN WU, (孫武)** —Hupeh. A noted Hupeh revolutionary, whose accident with a bomb in the Russian Concession at Hankow in October, 1911, led to the discovery of the revolutionary headquarters, and precipitated the Revolution. Sun Wu was injured by the explosion, and owed his life to the devotion of his friends, who succeeded in concealing him in a place of safety until he had recovered. He subsequently played a prominent part in the events of the Revolution at Wuchang, becoming Chief of the Military Department in that city. It was mainly through his instrumentality that the counter-revolutionary plot hatched at Wuchang in August was frustrated. Educated at the Military Academy, Wuchang. After a visit to Japan he returned and opened schools with the object of spreading revolutionary ideas. Connected with the Changsha riots of 1910. In February, 1911, when in Wuchang, he arranged with Huang Hsing for a simultaneous outbreak in Wuchang and Canton in May. The latter plot miscarried, but Sun Wu's opportunity came with the subsequent railway unrest. Instrumental in forming the Kunghotang Party. First Class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 853 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**SUN YAT-SEN** see Sun Wen. — p. 853 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**SUN YUN-YU, (孫潤宇)** —Kiangsu. Born 1878. A Graduate of Peiyang University and a Law School in Japan. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan and the Constitution-Drafting Committee. Kuomintang. Author of Outlines of Administration, etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 853 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**SUNG CHUN-FANG, (Soong Tsung-faung), (宋春舫)** —Chekiang. Studied in Genoa, Switzerland. Professor, Peking National University. Secretary, Ministry of Finance. Deputy Chief of Loan Department, Minis- try of Finance. Author of "Parcourtant le Monde en Flammes" (1917), "La Litterature Chinoise Contemporaine" (1919) and some dramatic works. — p. 853 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**SUNG FA-HSIANG** (Fartsan T. Sung), (宋發祥).—Fukien. Born August 26, 1883 Educated in America. B.Sc., M.A., (Ohio Wesleyan) B.Sc., (Chicago). Teacher, Fukien Provincial College. Professor of Chemistry, Government University, Peking, 1908-12. Technical Expert Ministry of Finance, 1914-1916. Co-director, Assaying Office, 1912-13. Director, Soochow Mint and Co-Director Bureau of Engineering. 1914. Co-Director, Bureau of Printing and Engraving, 1916. Inspector General of Mints, 1914-16. Adviser to Tuchun of Kiangsu, 1915-16. Director, Nanking Mint, 1917. Political Councillor to the President since 1919. Author of Qualitative Analysis of Important Metals. Third class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**SUNG, FARTSAN T** see Sung Fa Hsiang. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**SUNG HSIAO-LIEN** (宋小濂).—Kirin. Acting Commissioner of Internal Affairs, Heilungkiang, March, 1911, and Commissioner, December, 1911. Tutuh of Heilungkiang, 1914. Member of Tsanchengyuan, May 1914. Director, Chinese Eastern Railway, 1920. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**SUNG LIEN-KUEI** .—Director-General of Mining Operations for the province of Yunnan. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**SZE, ALFRED** see Shih Chao Chi. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**SZE HSI-CHANG** (姒錫章).—Chekiang. Born 1870. Chinshih. Graduated from a Japanese Law School, and has been magistrate of Hsunhua and of Tientsin before becoming Taoyin of Tsin-hai-tao, Chihli in 1919. Second class Tashou Paokwang Chiaho decoration. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**SZE-TU YING** (司徒穎).—Kwangtung. Born 1878. Studied in the Imperial Peking University. Chujen. Member of Provisional Senate, 1912. Member of Chung Yi Yuan. Commissioner of Industries of Kansu since 1917. Author of books in Chinese on Mining and Industry. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**TAI CH'EN-LIN** (戴陳霖).—Chekiang. Studied in language schools in Shanghai and Peking. Graduated from a College in France. Attached to Chinese Legation at Paris. Councillor of the Waichiaopu, April, 1912. Chinese Minister at Madrid. Appointed Minister to Sweden, Norway and Denmark, June 16, 1922. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**TAI CHIH-CHIEN** (Tse-chien Tai) (戴志騫).—Kiangsu. Born 1883. Graduate, St. John's University, Shanghai, 1912. A.B. University of the State of New York, Albany Library School, 1918. B.L.S. Teacher to a middle school in Wenchow, 1907. Librarian, St. John's University, 1904-14. Assistant Librarian, Camp Upton Library, Camp Upton, Long Island, N.Y., 1918-19. Librarian, Tsing Hua College, Peking, 1914 to date. Acting Business Manager, Tsing Hua College, 1922. Author of Bibliography of 250 books on China with descriptive and critical notes; and many magazine articles. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**TAI, TSE-CHIEN** see Tai Chih-chien. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**TAN CHEN** (覃振).—Hunan. Graduate of a Law College in Japan, a member of the Tung Meng Hui. Secretary to the Tutuh of Hupeh. Member of Chung Yi Yuan. Was imprisoned for five years for promoting the Revolution in Hunan. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**TAN CHUNG-MOU** .—Chargé d'Affaires in Peru. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**TAN HSUEH-HENG** .—Director River Conservancy Works of Kwangtung. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**T'AN JUI-LIN** (譚瑞霖).—Kwangtung. Born 1885. Graduate of Preliminary Normal School Kwangtung. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan. After having graduated he travelled in the Straits Settlements, where he established two Primary Schools. During the Revolution he drove away the Imperialist troops from Hsinhuihsien in response to the call of the Lieutenant-General Hwang Min-tang. Was elected Speaker of the Town Council of the Hsien. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 854 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**TAN PEI-SEN** —Chargé d'Affaires at Madrid. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**T'AN SHOU-K'UN** —Hupeh. Born 1876. After the Revolution was appointed Secretary to the Tutuh of Kwangtung. Resigned the secretaryship, and became Sub-Editor of the Min Lik Pao, Shanghai. Then became Editor of the Chung Pao at Wuchang. Was Military Adviser to the Tutuh of Hupeh, and Speaker of the Hupeh Assembly. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**T'AN YEN-K'AI, (譚延闓)** —Hunan. Obtained degree of Chinshih, and in 1904, at the early age of twenty-five, was appointed a Hanlin Compiler. Retired from official life shortly afterwards, and lived in his native district until the formation of the Hunan Provincial Assembly, of which he was elected Chairman. Was elected Chief of the Military Department of Hunan on the outbreak of the Revolution, but after the assassination of two rival Tutuhs he was elected to that office in his native province. Relieved of his post, October 13, 1913; became Tuchun of Hunan, Aug. 1916. Again Tuchun and Civil Governor, Dec. 1918. Was succeeded by Chao Heng-ti as Commander-in-Chief of Hunan Allied troops. Appointed Minister of Interior, June 12, 1922, but did not accept office. Second order of Merit. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**T'ANG CHI-YAO, (唐繼堯)** —Yunnan. Acting Tutuh of Kweichow, April, 1912. Commander-in-Chief of Yunnan and Kweichow troops for the relief of Szechuan, August 26, 1913. Acting Tutuh of Yunnan, September 28, 1913. Acting Chief Civil Administrator of Yunnan, October 4, 1913. Chiangchun of Yunnan, 1915; subsequently Tuchun and Civil Governor until 1921, when he was driven out of the Province. Was Chief of the General Staff in the Canton Government, 1918. Returned to Yunnan again in March 1922. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**T'ANG CHUN** —Deputy Governor, Bank of China, August 21, 1913; Governor, do., September 25, 1913. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**TANG CHUNG, (湯中)** —Kiangsu. Councillor, Ministry of Education, August, 1912. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**TANG HSIANG MING, (湯薌銘)** —Hupeh. Educated at the Liang Hu College, Wuchang, and obtained degree of Chujen before leaving for France to undergo a course of naval training. After graduating from a Naval College he served on a French warship, and returned to China in the autumn of 1911. A nephew of Tang Hua-lung. Appointed Vice-Minister of the Navy on the formation of the first Republican Cabinet. Vice-Admiral, Nov. 3, 1912. Acting Tutuh of Hunan, October 24, 1913. Chiangchun and Civil Governor of Hunan, 1914. Appointed Civil Governor of Hupeh, July 7, 1922, but was not allowed to assume office by the Tuchun. First class Tashou Chiaho Decoration. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**TANG ER-HO, (湯爾和)** —Received medical training abroad. Was Acting Minister of Education in Wang Ching-hui's Cabinet, 1922. First Class Chiaho decoration. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**TANG KU-SE, (唐古色)** —Member for Mongolia of the Tsan Yi Yuan. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**TANG SHAO-YI, (唐紹儀)** —Kwangtung. Educated in America. Secretary to Yuan Shih-kai while the latter was Imperial Resident in Korea. Consul-General in Korea after the Chino-Japanese war. Then employed on the staff of the Northern Railway Administration. In Shantung with Yuan Shih-kai, winter, 1900. Customs Taotai, Tientsin February, 1902. Special Commissioner to Tibet, September, 1904. Proceeded to India as special Envoy, to negotiate the Tibet Convention, which was subsequently completed at Peking in April, 1906. Acting Junior Vice- President of the Board of Foreign Affairs, November, 1905. Substantive Junior Vice-President of the Board of Foreign Affairs, February, 1906. Director-General Shanghai-Nanking, and Lu-Han Railways, 1906. Comptroller-General of Revenue Council, May 1906. Senior Vice-President of Board of Communications, November, 1906. Continued to act as Vice-President of Board of Foreign Affairs. First Governor of Fengtien on reorganization of government of Manchuria, April, 1907. Special Envoy to America to thank the Government for waiving part of the Boxer Indemnity, July, 1908. Resigned Governorship of Fengtien, July, 1909. Expectant Vice-President Board of Communications, and Acting President, August, 1910, and resigned in the spring. Appointed Minister of Communications on the dismissal of Sheng Hsuan-huai on October 26, 1911. Proceeded to Shanghai as Yuan Shih-kai's delegate to negotiate with the revolutionary leaders in December. Resigned his position as delegate on December 27. Appointed Premier, after abdication of the Manchus on February 12. Resignation as Premier accepted on June 27, when he was appointed Superior Adviser to the President on State Affairs. A member of the Tung Meng Hui. One of the four Directors of Canton Government, 1918. Minister of Finance at Canton, 1919-22. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 855 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**TANG TIEH-CHIAO, (湯鐵樵)** —Hunan. Born 1877. Studied in Hunan and graduated from a law college in Japan. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Justice, October 1913. Appointed a Councillor, Ministry of Justice, 1913. Third class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**T'ANG TSAI-FU, (唐在復)** —Kiangsu. Born 1877. Graduate of Kwang Fang Yen College, Shanghai; of the Tungwen College, Peking; and the Paris University. Secretary to the Chinese Legation at Paris and The Hague, and at one time Acting Chinese Minister in France, Holland, and Russia. Was Vice-President of the Board of Foreign Affairs in the last Dynasty. Junior Secretary Waichiaopu, March, 1912; Councillor, Waichiaopu, April, 1912. Is now (1922) Minister to Italy. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**TANG TSAI-LI, (唐在禮)** —Kiangsu. Deputy Chief of the General Staff, 1915. Resigned same year. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**T'ANG TSUNG-KUO, (唐宗郭)** —Wusih, Kiangsu. Born October 25, 1890. Graduated from Kiangsu Provincial College and in the Law Department, Peking Government University. B.A., LL.B. Member of Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce 1917. Wrote a book on famine relief and on the Industrial History of China. Fourth class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**TANG TSUNG-YU, (唐宗愈)** —Kiangsu. Commissioner of Finance in Heilungkiang, and Director of Banks, 1915-1917. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**TANG YANG-TENG** —Formerly Inspector-General of the Yangtze and Director-General of the Canton-Hankow Railway. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**T'AO CH'ANG-SHAN, (陶昌善)** —Chekiang. Born 1878. A Graduate of an Agricultural School in Japan. A Chief of the Department of Agriculture, appointed December, 1912. Chairman of the Society of Agriculture. Commissioner of Industry, Kirin, September 1917. Author of Agricultural Affairs. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**TAO, L. K** see Tao Lu Kung. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**TAO LU-KUNG, (L. K. Tao), (陶履恭)** —Tientsin. Born November 5, 1887. Studied in the Higher Normal School, Tokio, and London School of Economics and Political Sciences. B.S., (London) Dean of History Department, Higher Normal School, Peking, 1914. Professor of Social and Political Sciences, 1915-18. Dean of the Faculties, Peking National University, 1919. Prof. of Social and Political Sciences. 1920. Joint-author, Village and Town Life in China, Educational Sociology (in Chinese), etc. — p. 856 ([scan leaf 929](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n929/mode/1up))

**TIEN CHUNG-YU, (田中玉)** .—Chihli. Lieutenant-General. In the Generalissimo's Office. Appointed Joint Investigator of the Likin system, 1916. Defence Commissioner of Yenchow, Sung., Aug. 1913. Vice-Minister of War, June 1915. Tutung of Chahar, June, 1916. Tuchun of Kirin and now of Shantung and for some time acted as Civil Governor in that province — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TIEN PU-CH'AN, (田步蟾)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1866. A Chinshih of the last Dynasty. Graduate of the Law Department of the Chinshih Academy. Chief of the Department of Cultivation and Cattle Raising in the Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry. Was sent to Changpaishan to investigate forests and mines, to investigate industry in the Three Eastern Provinces, and to the North of Kalgan to manage the cultivation and cattle raising there. Was a Supervisor of the Construction of the Imperial Tombs. Appointed Commissioner of Industries for Shensi, Sept. 1917. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TIEN WEN-LIEH, (田文烈)** .—Hupeh. Governor of Honan, 1915. Minister of Agriculture and Commerce, Nov. 1917. Second Order of Merit. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TIEN YING-HUANG, (田應璜)** .—Shansi. Born 1866. Chujen. Magistrate in Hupeh for some time. Member and Vice-Chairman of the Senate, dissolved after the Anfu defeat in 1920. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TING CHENG** —Lieutenant-General. Bordered Red Banner Manchu Corps. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TING CH'IEN, (丁蓫)** .—Honan. Born 1881. Graduate of a Law College in Japan. Was deputed to recruit troops in Wuhu and other places during the Revolution. A member of the Advisory Council in Nanking Government. Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Chinputang. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TING CHIN, (M. K. Tinn), (丁錦)** .—Kiangsu. Born 1878. Studied Chinese under private tutors. Graduated from Nanyuan College, Shanghai, 1897. Proficient in Japanese language. Through introduction of Wu Shi Lung, former President of Peking Government University, joined Tuan Chi-jui who then was in service under the Viceroy of Chihli, as his translator. Graduated from the Pei Yang Military Academy in 1902. Engaged upon graduation by ex-President Feng, who was then engaged in training troops. Became teacher of Royal Military College, 1903. Member of Army Board 1905. Studied military tactics under a Japanese Professor. Adviser to the Army Board, 1906. A year later, General Ting became Chief of General Staff in the Yamen of Viceroy of Yunnan and Kweichow. Remained quietly in Shanghai during the revolution. Appointed Councillor to the Ministry of Army, 1914. Chief of Military Operations Department of the Ministry of Army, 1917. Appointed Brigadier General and given the command of a brigade of the Frontier Defence Army, 1919. Became Chief of Chinese Government Aeronautic Department at the end of 1919. Second class Chiaho, Wenhu and Paokuang Chiaho decorations. Fifth Order of Merit. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TING HUAI, (丁槐)** .—Yunnan, General. Headquarters Staff. Formerly Provincial Commander-in-Chief, Yunnan. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TING NAI-YANG, (丁乃揚)** .—Chekiang. Born 1870. Was Magistrate of Peking in the Ching regime. Salt Commissioner of Changli (Chihli) Transferred to Lianghuai (North Kiangsu and Anhui), April 1, 1921. First class Tashou Chia, Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho, and Wenhu decorations. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TING P'ING-LAN, (丁平瀾)** .—Fukien. Studied in the Naval College in Fukien and graduated from an Engineering (Railroad and Bridge Engineering) College in France. Director, Chengting-Taiyuan Railway since June 1908. Third and Fourth Class Chiaho decorations. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**TING, S. Y. W** , see Ting Shih-yuan. — p. 858 ([scan leaf 931](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n931/mode/1up))

**Ting Shih-to** —Member for Shantung of the National Council. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Ting Shih-yuan, (S. Y. W. Ting), (丁士源)** —Chekiang. Born 1879. Attended St. John's University, Shanghai. Was during early years in insurance business. Entered the Government service through the assistance of a certain Manchu prince. Was once an important officer of Chienmen Octroi, Peking. Conducted a newspaper while a tax officer. After leaving Peking, he was in England a year or two studying. Was then delegated to attend the Hague Conference. Upon return to China, he was appointed Chief of Martial Law Department in the Ministry of War, Adviser to the Ministry of Interior and Director of Public Schools in Peking. Appointed Customs Taotai of Hankow, and concurrently Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Hupeh, January, 1914. Removed from office, July 1916. Appointed Director, Peking-Hankow Railway, 1918. After effecting the Union of the Peking-Hankow and Peking-Suiyuan lines, he became the Director of these two railways. Upon dissolution of the Anfu Political Club by the armed interference of Generals Tsao Kun and Chang Tso-lin, July, 1920, in which struggle, Ting was Chief of Marshal Tuan's Aviation Department, he fled and became one of the guests of the Imperial Japanese Legation, Peking, from which he escaped, Nov. 16, 1922. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Ting, T. Y** see Ting Ta Yuan. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Ting Ta-yuan, (T. Y. Ting), (丁達元)** —Kiangsu. Born November 20, 1872. Studied in the Peiyang Telegraph School and in a school in Korea. Chief, Telegraph Department, President's Office, 1912. Director, Telegraph office, Wuchang, since August 1920. Fifth class Wenhu and Sixth class Chiaho decorations. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Ting, V. K** see Ting Wen-chiang. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Ting Wen-chiang, (V. K. Ting), (丁文江)** —Kiangsu. Born 1887. Studied in Cambridge, England; Glasgow, Scotland; Freiburg, Germany. Graduated 1911. B.Sc., D.Sc. Director of the Geological Survey, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce, 1913. Became its Hon. Director, 1922. General Manager, Peipiao Coal Mining Co. Author of Geology of the Yangtze Delta, Fifty Years of Mining in China, etc. Second class Chiaho and Third class Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Tinn, M. K** see Ting Chin. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Tong Hollington K** see Tung Hsien-kuang. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Tsai Cheng-hsun, (蔡成勳)** —Tientsin. Tutung of Suiyuan and Commanding General of the First Army Division. Became Minister of War, May 1921. Proceeded to Kiangsi to defend it against Li Lieh Chun, June 1922. Appointed to be in command of the Kiangsi troops (without the title of Tuchun), June 15, 1922, when Chen Kwang-yuan left his post. Second Order of Merit. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Tsai Hsun, (載洵)** —(Prince).—Brother of the late Emperor Kwang Hsu. High Commissioner for Naval Reorganization, June, 1909. Naval Mission to Europe, October, 1909. Consulting Member of Government Council, July, 1910. Naval Mission to Japan and America, August, 1910. Minister of the Navy, 1911. Removed from office after outbreak of Revolution. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Tsai Ju-kai, (蔡儒楷)** —Kiangsi. Chujen. Graduated from Peiyang University, Tientsin. Expectant Taotai and later became President of his own College. Was Commissioner of Education for Chihli, 1910. Acting Minister of Navy, Feb. 1913. Governor of Shantung, May 9, 1914 to July 6, 1916. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Tsai Li** see Chun, Prince. — p. 859 ([scan leaf 932](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n932/mode/1up))

**Tsai T'ao, (載濤)** —(Prince).—Brother of the late Emperor Kwang Hsu. Charged with formation of a new Imperial Guard, December, 1908. Chief of the General Staff, June, 1909. Mission to Japan, America, and Europe to study military matters, March, 1910. Removed from office during the Revolution.
