Associate Delegate of the Peking Government to negotiate peace with the South at Shanghai, in 1919. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Wu Tsung-lien** (吳宗濂).—Kiangsu. Attache to Chinese Legations abroad. Acting Senior Secretary, Waiwupu, September, 1908. Acting Junior Councillor, January, 1909. Minister to Italy, August 1909. Special Commissioner of Foreign Affairs, Kirin, Nov. 1916. Now resides in Peking. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Wu Wen-ping** (吳文炳).—Anhui. Chief of the Constabulary Court of Foochow, Sept., 1915. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Wu Yu-lin** (Y. L. Woo), (吳毓麟).—Chihli. Born 1875. Admiral. Graduated from the Peiyang Naval College of which he was a teacher after graduation. Departmental Chief of the Ministry of Navy. Appointed Director-General of Taku Naval Dockyard and of Peiyang Iron Works, Tientsin. Managing-Director, Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Minister of Communications, Jan. 1923. Second class Wenhu, second class Paokuang Chiaho, second class Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Yang Ch'i-kuan** (楊其觀).—Kwangtung. Born May 1887. Graduated in the French Department of Hupeh Language College. Chief Secretary, Department of Finance in the Military Government 1920. Translated Modern History of the World. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Yang, Chien** see Yang Chuan. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Yang Ch'ing** .—Yunnan. Born 1845. A Chujen of the late Dynasty. Was sent by the Viceroy of his Province to investigate Educational Affairs in Japan, where he remained and studied for one year in a Normal School. Most of his time was employed in Educational Affairs. Member of the Ts'an Yi Yuan. When the election of Speaker took place he was elected as Provisional Speaker of the House, being the oldest member of that House. Kuomintang. Author of the Records of Yunnan, etc. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. Commissioner of Trade and Foreign Affairs in the South. Councillor in the office of the Generalissimo of the Yangtze. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Yang Ching-chun** (楊慶鎔).—Chihli. Superintendent of Police at Tsinan. Taoyin of Tsinan. Commissioner of Foreign Affairs for Shantung. Commissioner of Finance, Kiangsi. Civil Governor of Kiangsi. Resigned, June 15, 1922. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Yang Chuan** (Yang Chien), (楊銓).—Kiangsi. Born 1893. Graduate, Cornell University (U.S.A.), M.E. and Harvard University, M.B.A. Editor of Science, 1914-21. Secretary of the Science Society of China, 1919-22. Dean of Commercial Department, Teachers' College, Nanking, 1919-21. Professor of Factory Management, Southeastern University, 1921-22. Author of Factory Management and Personal Efficiency, two works in Chinese. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**Yang I-te** (楊以德).—Chihli. Born 1871. Was a petty employee of the Peking-Mukden line, member of the Tientsin detective force, etc., before he eventually became Police Taotai for Chihli in the Ching dynasty. Has been the head of Chihli police force ever since. Brigadier General. 5th order of merit, second class Wenhu, Chiaho, second class Tashou Chiaho, Tashou Paokuang Chiaho decorations. Made Chiangchun Oct., 1922. — p. 878 ([scan leaf 951](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n951/mode/1up))

**YANG JUN, (楊潤)** —Kiangsu. Studied in the Kiangsu Provincial College (Nanking). Member of Parliament, in the first and second republican parliaments. Director, Bureau of Engraving and Printing since Sept. 11, 1920 to June 1922. Second class Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG MIEN-CHIH** —Hunan. Born 1879. Graduate of the Normal School at Hunan and Graduate of a Japanese University. Councillor of the Ministry of Agriculture and Forestry, appointed August, 1912. Kuomintang. Author of Philosophy, Poems (six volumes), The Blind (five volumes), and Talks. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG MING-YUAN, (楊銘源)** —Shensi. Born 1879. Studied in a University in Tokio, Japan. Acting Commissioner of Finance, Shensi, July 13, 1921. Fourth class Wenhu decoration. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG PAO-LING, (楊豹靈)** —Kiangsu. Born 1887. Studied in Anglo-Chinese Colleges of Shanghai and Soochow. After leaving school, Yang was teacher to several schools in Chinkiang and Changchow. When Viceroy Tuan Fang was in Nanking, he passed his official examination successfully by which he was sent to United States as a government student. Attended Cornell University and Purdue University and got the degree of C.E. from the latter. Returned to China in 1911. During the outbreak of revolution at Wuchang, he participated in the Red Cross work and acted as a war correspondent of the China Press. Appointed by the President Li Yuan-hung as Commissioner for Foreign Affairs at Wuchang. Was Dean of Civil Engineering Department of the Institute of Technology, Changsha. Departmental Chief of the National Conservancy Bureau of which Chang Chien was the Director-General. As a technical expert, Yang devoted his services to surveying the Huai River and the Grand Canal and in the Flood Relief Work of 1917 in Chihli. Appointed member of the Commisison for the Improvement of the River System of Chihli, since 1917. Vice-President, Chinese American Engineers' Association, Co-Director, Tientsin Telegraph Administration, May, 1922. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG SHAO-CHUNG, (楊紹中)** —Honan. Born 1881. A Graduate of the Normal School in Honan and of the Law College in Peking. Judge in the Chihli Higher Court. Speaker of Honan Assembly. Was Teacher to various Normal and Law Colleges. Chinputang. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG TING-TUNG, (楊廷棟)** —Kiangsu. Studied in Japan. Teacher to a Normal School. Secretary of Foreign Affairs of Kiangsu during the Revolution. Member of Tsan Yi Yuan. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG TSE** —Member for Kirin of the National Council. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG TSENG-HSIN, (楊增新)** —Yunnan. Age 57. Chinshih. Aksu Taotai, Sinkiang, 1908. Acting Commissioner of Finance, Sinkiang, April, 1912. Military and Civil Governor for Sinkiang since June 5, 1912. Second Order of Merit. First class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. Awarded first order of Merit, Oct. 15, 1921. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YANG TU, (楊度)** —Honan. Born 1875. Chujen. Studied law in Japan. Upon his return, Yang worked up till he was Vice-Minister in the Board of Education. Since the Republic, Yang was once High Political Adviser to President Yuan Shih-kai. Member of Tsanchengyuan, June, 1914. Was one of the Promoters of the Yuan's Monarchical project. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YAO CHEN, (姚震)** —Anhui. President of the Court of Revision, 1913. Noted for pro-Anfu Intrigue. Has been in hiding in the Japanese legation, Peking till Nov., 1922, when he escaped. — p. 879 ([scan leaf 952](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n952/mode/1up))

**YAO HUA, (姚華)** —Kweichow. Member for Shantung of the National Council. Was once Director, Peking Girls' Normal School. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YAO LIEN-K'UEI, (姚聯奎)** —Anhui. Born 1860. Taming Taoyin, Chihli, since Jan. 13, 1916. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YAO SHIH-KUANG** —Senior Councillor. Board of War, July, 1907. Acting Junior Vice-President of the Board, March, 1909. Expectant Vice-President of a Board, December, 1910. Advisory Minister to the Privy Council, August, 1911. Director of Peiyang University. Vice-President of Ministry of Interior in 1910. Vice-Chief (and for some time Acting Chief) of Bureau for Mongolia and Tibet, July, 1912. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YAO YU, (姚煜)** —Salt Commissioner for the two Kuang provinces, 1915. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YEH CHING-HSIN, (T. H. Yeh), (葉景莘)** —Chekiang. Born 1881. Studied in Peiyang University, Tientsin, and Birmingham University and Victoria University, England. B.Sc. Member of Ministry of Finance, 1913. Chief of Minting Department, Bureau of Currency, 1914. Secretary, Ministry of Finance, 1917. Secretary to the President, 1919 to date. Third class Chiaho decoration. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YEH KUNG-CH'O, (葉恭綽)** —Kwangtung. Born in Peking, November 24, 1881. Studied in Imperial University of Peking. Was Chief of Railway Department, Ministry of Communications and Vice-Minister of the same Ministry. After being a teacher in various schools in Hupeh, he joined the Ministry of Communications and was one of the officials who planned to get back the control of the Peking-Hankow Railway from the foreigners. Appointed Councillor to negotiate between the North and the South. A delegate to prepare for the organization of the Provincial Government. Was a member of the Finance Committee; Vice-Chairman of the Railway Association; Chairman, Commission on Technical Terms of the Railways; Commission on Unification of Railway Accounting System. Vice-Minister of Communications, July 3, 1913 to 1915. Vice-Minister of Finance, 1917. Reappointed Vice-Minister of Communications. Resigned October, 1918. Commissioner to study Industry and Communications to Europe. Appointed Minister of Communications, August 11, 1920. Resigned, May 1921. Special Commissioner for Promotion of Industries of which office he was released Jan. 5, 1922. Reappointed Minister of Communications, Dec. 25, 1921. Cashiered all decorations and merit, May 5, 1922, when Chang Tso-lin was defeated by the Chihli party. Fled to Japan. Second class Chiaho, Tashou Paokuang Chiaho, First class Wenhu decorations. Wearer of Medals from Belgium, Japan and Denmark. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YEH, T. H** , see Yeh Ching-hsin. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YEN, BAYORK C** , see Yen Chü. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YEN CHIA-CHIH, (嚴家熾)** —Kiangsu. Born 1873. Scholar of the old school. Was an official in Kiangsi, Kwangtung during Ching regime. Was successively appointed Superintendent of Customs, Canton, Commissioner of Finance for Kwangtung, and for Hunan since the Republic. Was also once Acting Governor of Hunan. Commissioner of Finance for Kiangsu since Oct. 1920. Second Class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**YEN CHIH-I, (嚴智怡)** —Tientsin. Born 1882. Graduated from the Technical College, Tokio, Japan. Was member of the Provincial Government of Chihli. Special Commissioner from Chihli to the Panama Exposition, San Francisco, 1915. Departmental Head, Ministry of Agriculture and Commerce. Appointed Chihli Commissioner of Industries, October, 1917. Second class Paokuang Chiaho and Tashou Chiaho decorations. — p. 880 ([scan leaf 953](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n953/mode/1up))

**Yen Chu** (Bayork C. Yen), (嚴璆).—Fukien. Born 1874. Studied in Naval College, Tientsin, and London University. Attached to Chinese Legation in London and Paris. Chief, Bureau of Foreign Affairs, Kwangtung. Councillor, Salt Administration, 1916. Vice-Minister of Finance and Chief of Salt Administration, May 1922. Second class Tashou Chiaho and Third class Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 881 ([scan leaf 954](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n954/mode/1up))

**Yen, Hawkling L** see Yen Hê Ling. — p. 881 ([scan leaf 954](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n954/mode/1up))

**Yen He-ling** (Hawkling L. Yen), (嚴鶴齡).—Yu-yao, Chekiang. Born 1880. Studied in the St. John's University, Shanghai, 1896-1903. Graduated therefrom with diploma, 1903; A.B., 1907. Instructor, St. John's University, 1903-6. Teacher, Fu Tan College, Woosung, 1907-8. Editor, St. John's Echo, 1901-3. Studied political science at Columbia University, U.S.A., 1909-11. A.M., 1909; Ph.D., 1911. Member, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1911-12. Commissioner of Foreign Affairs, Chekiang, 1912-13. Secretary, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1913-16. Director-in-charge, Tsing Hua College. Formerly member of Constitution-Study Commission. Member, Peace Conference Preparation Commission. Technical Delegate to the Paris Peace Conference. Member, Office of the Cabinet in 1916. Councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs. Chief Secretary to the Chinese Delegation, Washington Conference, 1921. Author of A Survey of the Constitutional Development in China. Managing Editor, Chinese Social and Political Science Review. Second class Paokuang Chiaho and Wenhu Decorations. Second class Grand Officier of Italy. Second class Grand Officier of Greece. — p. 881 ([scan leaf 954](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n954/mode/1up))

**Yen Hsi-shan** (閻錫山).—Shansi. Born 1882. A Graduate of a Military Staff School in Japan. Lieutenant-General with the brevet rank of General of the Army, and Tutuh of Shansi. In Japan he joined the Tung Meng Hui, and after returning from that country he was appointed Director of the Military School and Chief of the 86th Regiment. When the Revolution broke out, he took up the revolutionary cause, and was elected Tutuh of Shansi. He led an army and occupied Koupei and district. Was the first to propose that the troops should be disbanded in order to curtail expenses, and he himself disbanded more than 30,000 troops in his Province. When the rebellion in the South broke out he was a strong supporter of the Central Government. Tuchun of Shansi since 1916. Concurrently Civil Governor of Shansi. Non-partisan. Author of The Discipline of the Revolutionary Army. Known as the "Model Tuchun." — p. 881 ([scan leaf 954](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n954/mode/1up))

**Yen Hsiu** (嚴修).—Tientsin. Born April 2, 1860. Educated in family school. Shinshih. Hanlin. Vice-Minister of Education in the late Ching Dynasty. Appointed Minister of Education, Feb. 20, 1914, but did not accept office. Member of Tsanchengyuan, May, 1914. Director of Nankai College, Tientsin. — p. 881 ([scan leaf 954](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n954/mode/1up))

**Yen Hui-ch'ing** (W. W. Yen), (顏惠慶).—Shanghai. Born 1877. Had early education in local schools. Studied in the Episcopal High School, Virginia, U.S.A., 1895-7 winning therefrom Gold Medal for English Composition and debating. Studied in the academic and law departments of the University of Virginia, receiving degree of B.A. and law diploma. Member of the Phi Beta Kappa Society. Professor of English. St. John's University, Shanghai, 1900-1906. Chinshih, Hanlin. One of the founders and honorary secretary of the World's Chinese Students' Federation, Shanghai. Member of various Educational and Social organizations. LL.D., Peking, 1906. Secretary to the Chinese Legation at Washington, 1908-10. Was recalled to Peking to organize the press Bureau, becoming its director. Junior Councillor, Ministry of Foreign Affairs, 1911. After various promotions, was appointed Vice- Minister of Foreign Affairs, April, 1912. Minister to Germany and Denmark, 1913, 1918-1920. Plenipotentiary to the Opium Conference at The Hague, May 26, 1913. Appointed Minister of Foreign Affairs, August 11, 1920. Appointed Acting Premier, Dec. 18, 1921, upon resignation of Chin Yun Peng. Reappointed Acting Premier, June 11, 1922, when Li Yuan-hung re-assumed the Presidency, but resigned a for weeks later. Author, translator and editor of various books most noted of which is the Standard English-Chinese Dictionary, published in Shanghai. Second order of Merit. — p. 881 ([scan leaf 954](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n954/mode/1up))

**YEN P'AN-CH'UN, (焉泮春)** —Fengtien. Born 1862. A Graduate of Yanchenhu School in Fengtien. Hsiutsai. A Member of the Chung Yi Yuan. Was an investigator of the various societies; a member of the Fengtien Tzu Yi Chu and a Vice-Speaker of the Provisional Fengtien Assembly. Chinputang. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**YEN TE-CHING, (顏德慶)** —Shanghai. Born Oct. 1878. Graduated from Tung Wen Kuan. St. Xavier's School, Shanghai, and University of Virginia and Lehigh University, U.S.A. Engineer to Canton-Hankow, Shanghai-Nanking, Peking-Kalgan and I-Wen Railways, 1902-09. Councillor and Technical Expert, Ministry of Communications, 1912. Managing-Director, Canton-Hankow Railway, 1912. Chinese Representative, Inter-Allied Technical Board, 1919. Technical Delegate, Pacific Conference, 1921. Associate Director-General, Han Yueh-Chuan Railways; and Councillor, Ministry of Communications, 1922. Second class Tashou Paokuang Chiaho, Chiao and Wenhu decorations. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**YEN TUN-YUAN, (言敦遠)** —Kiangsu. Vice-Minister of the Interior, October 28, 1912. Resigned, July 8, 1912. Resides in Tientsin 1922. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**YEN, W. W** , see Yen Hui Ch'ing. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**YIH ERH-WEI** .—President of the High Court of Justice in Szechuan, 1915. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**YIN CHANG, (蔭昌)** .—Manchu. Attached to Prince Ch'un's mission to Germany in 1901. Minister to Germany, August, 1901. Appointment renewed, December, 1904. Director of Noble's College, Peking, December, 1905. Provincial Commander-in-Chief, Kiangpei, September, 1906. Vice-President, Army Board, November, 1906. Minister to Germany for second time, September, 1908. Inspector-General of Autumn Manœuvres in Anhui, November, 1908. Proceeded to Germany, spring, 1909. Acting President, Board of War, March, 1910. Inspector-General of Divisions in vicinity of Peking, August, 1910. Minister of War in Prince Ching's Cabinet, December, 1910. Rank of General, March, 1911. Appointed Commander-in-Chief of the Imperial Forces, and sent to the front, on the outbreak of the Revolution at Wuchang. Recalled and appointed Chief of the General Staff, in place of Prince Tsai Tao, in November, 1911, from which post he subsequently resigned. Chief Military Secretary to the President. Member of the Tsangchengyuan (Council of State). Director of the Generalissimo's Office, Peking. Chief of General Staff, Dec. 1917. First order of Merit. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**YIN CHANG-HENG, (尹昌衡)** .—Szechuan. Born 1884. Graduate of the Officers' Training School in Japan. Lieutenant-General of the Army with the brevet rank of a full General. Was Tutuh of Szechuan, and Occupation Commissioner of Szechuan Frontier. Pacification Commissioner of Szechuan Frontier, and Acting Tutuh, September, 1912. Suppressed disturbances in Szechuan. Defeated Tibetan troops and recovered the frontier of Szechuan. Resigned his membership from political parties. Author of General Principles for Military Officers, Military Morality, etc. Second Order of Merit; Wenhu, second class. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**YIN JU-CHI** .—Member for Chekiang of the National Council. — p. 882 ([scan leaf 955](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n955/mode/1up))

**Yu Hua-lung** (于化龍).—Chihli. Educated in the Surveying School, Paoting. Departmental Head, Ministry of War since Sept. 3, 1920. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Hung-ch'i** (于洪起).—Shantung. Born 1876. Graduate of the High Normal School of the Government University at Peking, and a Chujen. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Jen-feng** (俞人鳳).—Chihli. Born 1873. Graduated from the Railway Department of the Pei Yang Military Academy. Has been Engineer of the Peking-Mukden, Peking-Kalgan, Canton-Hankow railroads. Since the Republic, Yu was connected with the Ministry of Communications and was appointed Assistant Managing-Director of the Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Managing-Director, Peking-Hankow Railway, 1920. Second class Wenhu, Third class Chiaho and Second Class Tashou Chiaho. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Jih-chang** (David Z. T. Yui), (余日章).—Hupeh. Studied in Wenhua College, Wuchang, and St. John's College, Shanghai. Teacher at Wenhua College for some time. Studied in America and got the degree of M.A. Member of several well-known Societies in America. Delegate for Foreign Affairs of President Li Yuan-hung. General Secretary of the National Committee of the Chinese Y.M.C.A. up to date. Sent by Shanghai people to the Washington Conferences as the "People's Delegate." Nov. 1921. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Lang** (毓朗).—Prince. A noble of the Imperial House. Vice-President, Board of Police, October, 1905. Assistant Comptroller Hatamen Octroi, September, 1906. Vice-President, Board of Interior, November, 1906. Prince of the Third Order, February, 1908. Charged with others with the formation of the new Imperial Body Guard, December, 1908. Commander of Peking Gendarmerie, January, 1909. Head of the General Staff, June, 1913. Grand Councillor, August, 1910. Chief of General Staff (with Prince Tsai Tao) in Prince Ching's Cabinet, an office from which he was removed on November 1, 1911. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Ming-hsin** (廣銘新).—Chekiang. Born 1880. Graduated from the Imperial University, Tokio, Japan. Chinshih. Was a member of the Ministry of Education before he became Commissioner of Education for Shansi, 1917. Author of several scientific works and poetry. Second class Tashou Chiaho decoration. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Pao-hsuan** (于寶軒).—Kiangsu. Member of the Board of Interior. Departmental Chief, Ministry of Interior. Secretary, Ministry of Communications, July, 1917. Vice-Minister of the Interior, Dec. 1917. Organizer and Controller of the National Plague Prevention Service. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Szu-hsing** (于馴舆).—Anhui. Born 1862. Commissioner of Education for Heilungkiang since April 1921. Second class Tashou Chiaho and third class Paokuang Chiaho decorations. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Tao-hsuan** .—Anhui. Graduate of a Japanese Law College. A member of the Tung Meng Hui, and one of the Anhui members of the National Council. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Te-chun** (Yü De Djuen), (于德濬).—Shantung. Studied in Tung Wen Kuan, Peking, and in England. Member of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs since the Ching dynasty. Secretary to the Chinese Delegation, Washington Conference, 1921. Second class Chiaho decoration. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**Yu Wen-ting** (余文鼎).—Chekiang. Graduate of Tung Wen Kuan, Shanghai. Went to America with Tang Shao-yi. Interpreter to Mr. Tang when he went to England. Member of Board of Communications Chief of the Bureau of Printing and Engraving, 1913. — p. 883 ([scan leaf 956](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n956/mode/1up))

**YU YUN-LUNG, (由雲龍)** —Yunnan. Born August 1887. Graduated from the Imperial University (Peking). Magistrate in Yunnan. Commissioner of Education, Yunnan, Feb. 1913. Acting Commissioner of Administration, Yunnan. Chief Secretary to the Tutuh, Director of Conservancy Bureau. Salt Commissioner for Yunnan since September, 1, 1916. Fourth Order of Merit, Second class of Chiaho decoration. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUAN CHIA-P'U, (袁家普)** —Hunan. Commissioner to the China International Famine Relief Commission. Financial Commissioner of Yunnan, May, 1912. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUAN HSI-T'AO, (袁希濤)** —Kiangsu. Born 1865. A Graduate of the Shanghai Lungmen College, and was appointed to proceed to Japan to investigate educational affairs. Chujen of the late Dynasty. Was two years a member of the Educational Committee of his native town; three years a member of the Shanghai City Municipal Council; two years a member of the Kiangsu Educational Society; and three years a Sectional Chief of the Chihli Educational Society. In the fifth month of the first year of the Republic was appointed Chief of the Department of General Education. Has for years devoted himself to educational affairs. Vice-Minister of Education, 1915. Non-partisan. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUAN JUNG-SOU** —Chekiang. Born 1881. Graduate of the Political Science Department of the University at Tokio. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan, and editor of the Kuominkungpao. Was Professor of Political Science in the Frontier College. Non-partisan. Author of many Essays on Politics published in various papers. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUAN K'E-TING, (袁克定)** —Honan. Eldest son of President Yuan Shih-kai. Expectant Taotai, employed upon special service in Manchuria, 1906. Junior Secretary of Board of Commerce, April, 1907. Sustained a serious accident while in Honan, in the spring of 1911, as the result of a fall from a horse. Went to Germany in 1913 for medical treatment. Director-General of the Kailan Mining Administration. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUAN PING-HUANG, (袁炳煌)** —Hunan. Born 1882. Graduate of the Police College of Sinkiang. A member of the Chung Yi Yuan for Sinkiang. Kuomintang. Unseated by Presidential Order of November 4, 1913. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUAN TA-HUA, (袁大化)** —Anhui. Judicial Commissioner, Shantung. May, 1906. Financial Commissioner, Honan, December, 1906. Acting Governor, August, 1907. Acting Governor, Shantung, March, 1908. Mourning, April, 1908. Governor of Sinkiang, November, 1910. Resigned, April, 1912, and ordered to take command of troops in Sinkiang and assist the Tutuh to restore order. Appointed to the Council of Regency on the abortive restoration of the Manchu Dynasty, July, 1917. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUI, DAVID Z. T** see Yu Jih-chang. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUN PAO-HUI, (恽寶惠)** —Peking. Councillor of the Ministry of War. Chief Secretary to the Cabinet since 1920. Director, Government Famine Relief Bureau since 1920. First Class Tashou Chiaho Paokwang decoration. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**YUN SHAO,* (雲韶)** —Nanking. Born 1871. Commissioner of Industry, Chekiang, since Oct. 1917. Second class Chiaho and Third class Paokuang Chiaho and Wenhu decorations. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))

**ZEN, H. C** see Jen Hung-chun. — p. 884 ([scan leaf 957](https://archive.org/details/china-year-book-1923/page/n957/mode/1up))
